# Open Threads

# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT V (Chapters 291–300) — OPEN, UNPAID, AND CARRIED INTO THE CLOSE

**These sit above the `VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT IV` block and are the live thread list for the Volume 06 close. Volume 06 is complete on the page at Chapter 300 and nothing in this list is resolved by that. The close writes no prose and settles none of these; it records them, dates them, and hands them on.**
**A REVIEW REPAIR PASS RAN OVER MOVEMENT V AFTER THE BATCH WAS WRITTEN AND ALL THIRTY-EIGHT FINDINGS THAT WERE REAL ARE APPLIED.** It is written up in full at section 8 of `workspace/volume-06/batch-0005/SUMMARY.md`, which is the dated account and which also carries the re-measured figures. **Nothing was restarted: no chapter was replaced, no scene rewritten, no beat moved, no day of the day map moved, and the load-book run is still 294 to 303 with no duplicate and no gap.** The measured figures on the ten files moved, and every figure in that file that describes them was re-run on the files rather than carried: bold spans are 6.061 per thousand against a ceiling of 10.75 with a worst chapter of 8.10 against 12.4, the eight-word overlap mean is 60.6 against the 105.5 that file first published, and the apparatus share is 47.3 per cent against the 48.9 it first published.

**WHAT THE PASS CHANGED IN THIS BLOCK, IN ONE PLACE: item 5's heating day is the Saturday before the Wednesday of week ninety-nine and both facts are now there; item 14's refrain count is five and not six and the four chapters are named; item 17's chapter and load-book counts for Talia Venn are five and four and the correction says which half of the old claim was false. Nothing in this block was resolved, and the close still writes no prose.**



1. **THE CLAUSE IS NAMED, RESTRICTED, IN FORCE AND NOT DESTROYED, AND ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED PEOPLE IN THIS CITY CAN STILL INVOKE IT.** A restriction and not a repeal. The number of them is not a conspiracy, is not on a list, is not reducible, and **nobody in this case can produce it.** A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts was told the true thing and is owed that answer and will not get a second one. **Nothing in this case repealed it and nothing in this case could.**
2. **A TEMPORARY CONSENT STANDARD WAS ADOPTED IN ABOUT FOUR MINUTES IN ABOUT NINE HEADS AND ON NO PAPER, AND AN AUDIT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE IN ROOMS WITH A LIST WAS ORDERED WITH NO MINUTE OF IT.** No copy of the standard exists. The audit exists as nine conversations in nine rooms and as a list, and the nine of them are owed that they are not on a page. **Neither is enforceable by anybody outside the nine heads and neither will be.**
3. **THE CONTINUITY OFFICE KEPT ITS EMERGENCY AUTHORITY, AGREED TO NOTHING, WAS NOT DEFEATED, WAS NOT TURNED, AND WAS RIGHT ABOUT ONE THING.** A standard adopted as temporary with no date on it runs until somebody with a stamp puts a date on it; that has happened twice in that city in nineteen years; the first temporary thing outlasted the permanent thing it replaced by about six years and nobody noticed until it was over. **He is not a post, holds nothing, and has not been asked to be anything.**
4. **ABOUT NINE PEOPLE IN A COMPANY NOW KNOW A THING IS BEING LOOKED AT, AND ONE OF THEM HAS NOT BEEN TOLD WHAT TO DO AND HAS NOT ASKED.** A man of thirty-four who buys training for a company that runs about four of the rooms in the audit. He was told nothing before the morning of the Wednesday, was told he was one of the nine for a fortnight on his own time in his own company, and was warned in a room that **if it is on a page then he is on a page.** He has not answered that and is not to be asked again.
5. **THE HEATING IS ON AND IT COSTS MORE, AND THE FORTNIGHT IS UNPAID BY ANYBODY AND IS NOT GOING TO BE RESOLVED.** Restored on a Saturday, before the Wednesday of week ninety-nine, in about four minutes, by a landlord who went round four units, in a building he has never been inside, with nobody asked, nobody paying and nobody thanked. The Wednesday of week ninety-nine is the day this case found out and not the day the valve was turned, and a landlord's notice about that valve, signed by a managing agent and dated nine days after the man who opened it, is canon from an earlier chapter and is a document near the heating; **Chapter 300's first draft denied that any notice existed anywhere near it four lines after naming one, and both sentences are now true.** Four names on the lease and four names now cost more, and each of the four named the cost out loud before signing in a week in the spring before last. **No fund, collection, subscription, grant, list, minute, notice, form, leaflet, registration, appeal or form was put anywhere near it in about six weeks and none was proposed by anybody, and nothing that happened in a room in Crown Terrace paid for any of it.**
6. **THE WORD IS UNCORRECTED AT NINE AND TEN, WAS SAID OUT LOUD ONCE TO ONE PERSON, AND IS NOT BEING REPORTED.** Not an apology, not a request, not a declaration; there was no scene. She is not reporting it, the not reporting is her decision and not his, he cannot ask her to and cannot ask her not to, and he does not get a copy of it. He has not said it to the officer of sixty, to the man of fifty-eight with a bad hip, or on a service road, and is not going to.
7. **THE WALL IS EIGHTEEN LINES IN TWELVE HANDS AND THIRTEEN NAMES.** No line has ever been removed and none has been rubbed off, and the first line is a cook's and is still the first line. The sixteenth is a woman of about sixty-eight's and is a refusal. The seventeenth is a clerk of forty-four's, it is signed, and it is about findability. **The eighteenth is a woman of sixty-one's, it is signed, it is about being written down, she walked about nine miles to write it, and nobody thanked her, nobody asked her name and nobody said she was welcome.** A later writer may carry seven figures for that board.
8. **THE EXCHANGE DID NOT OPEN AT THE SIXTEENTH SITTING AND THE REASON IS THE FIRST IN NINETEEN YEARS THAT IS ABOUT A ROOM AND NOT ABOUT A PERSON.** The book is at forty-eight lines, the telephone is at fifty-seven, **the telephone is nine ahead of the book** where it was five ahead at the start of this movement and the book was two ahead at the start of the volume. The direction is stated and the difference is refused, the count has been announced twenty-one times with sixteen of those on the sixteen Wednesdays this book holds and five predating it on a sheet not shown to anybody in nineteen years, and **no person and no writer may convert those four figures into one another or into a number of sittings.** She gave the no-conversion rule on the last morning of the volume and said she had said it for the last time. The ninth chair was against the wall at the close and was not moved back and its mover is not named. **The next sitting is the Wednesday of week one hundred and twelve, four weeks after this one, and it is the seventeenth and it is outside the volume.**
9. **THE FOUR SECONDS ARE SETTLED FOR ONE FRIDAY AND NOT FOR THE REASON ANYBODY WOULD WANT, AND IT IS NOT TO BE RE-OPENED.** The reason is that he was in the fourth row against the wall with the word available and did not say it, and it was told to nobody and not even to a service road. The man of about forty-four is still short of a reason and is not to be asked a second time. **A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip is not to be asked for the thing about the paper a sixth time; he has been asked five times in his head in about four months and has said it once and said he would not say it again.**
10. **THE TRUE CONSEQUENCE OF THE SENTENCE HAS BEEN SAID OUT LOUD ONCE AND IS NOT IMPROVED ON.** Four people were each findable and he was not one of them, and a man of twenty-nine who works on lifts said that next time it will not be the same four, and that on the Tuesday nothing happens. **That is the answer to a question this case has been asking for about four years and it is not the answer to the one it should have been asking, and a first-year of nineteen described the shape of that second question in about four words and then stopped on purpose and refused to say it out loud.**
11. **NINE KEYS ARE NEEDED AND TWO EXIST, SAID IN TEN LOAD BOOKS AND IN NO ROOM.** The first cannot be asked back by anybody and about six weeks of work did not fix it and invented nothing and created no holder and no list. The second is in the pocket of a woman of thirty-one, who has not been asked in three movements, has not been asked to be carried anywhere since she said no, and is owed nothing. The answer to the question on the back of a docket is on nothing.
12. **THE MASTER'S FIFTH LINE IS STILL BLANK, IS IN THE FOURTH POCKET OF A TOOL ROLL, AND NOBODY HAS EVER ASKED HIM TO FILL IT IN.** He opened the drawer on the Wednesday of the close, did not take the roll out, and decided nothing about it, and nobody in that shop knows it is blank. **It is the cheapest object in this case and the one that has cost the most and the volume close should not soften it.**
13. **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY IS IN THE CHAIR NEAREST THE DOOR AND WAS IN THE BACK OF A ROOM IN CROWN TERRACE, AND WAS ASKED NOTHING ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS.** She was let in at the door by a man holding a jug of water who does not ask anybody who they are. She was not asked why she comes, was not thanked, was not apologised to, was not described at the public body by anything but her place in a count of about nine, and is not the answer to a question. **Her page is the eighth thing in a ring binder and is unread and eight people have decided not to read it.**
14. **THE FIGURE OF ABOUT ELEVEN A WEEK WAS SAID OUT LOUD ONCE IN A PUBLIC ROOM AND NOTHING CAME OF IT.** The wording is the inherited clause, unabridged, with *a release* in it, and it occurs five times across four of the ten chapters — once in Chapter 291, once in Chapter 293, twice in Chapter 297 and once in Chapter 298 — and no variant of it that drops *a release* appears anywhere. The number is on the back of a woman's own card in a drawer in a flat in Nine Bridges, is on no form, and is not a rate. **It is the whole of its movement and it is not a fix, and the volume close should say so rather than count it as a step.**
15. **THE MAN OF THIRTY-FOUR WHO WORKS NIGHTS IS STILL A COUNT AND THE VOLUME CLOSE OWNS HIS NAMING.** He opened a door in an earlier run of days for no stated reason, he wrote the volume's fifteenth line, he is not in these ten chapters, and he is not to be asked about.
16. **A NAME THAT EXISTS IS NOT ATTACHED TO ANYTHING THAT HAPPENED.** Iona Sorn is in the prose of this manuscript as a voice, on a recording, once, in her own mouth, and by nobody else. She is not met, nobody went looking, nothing arrived from her and nothing will. **On the Monday of week one hundred and two a civic sequence ran in three districts and no character in this case attached her name to it, and Chapter 294's load book records that as a decision and gives the reason: if this case can name her as the author of a sequence, then the answer to everything she says is that somebody said so, and it is that nobody can.** No word she said on that recording is quoted anywhere as if she had said it in a room.
17. **THE RELATIONSHIP IS NOT RESOLVED AND WAS NOT TOUCHED AS ONE.** Talia Venn is named in the body prose of Chapters 292, 295, 298, 299 and 300 and in the load books of 292, 295, 298 and 299, and in nobody's mouth in any of the ten chapters, and she did not give her name to anybody in any room in any of them. **Four of the four load-book names were added by the review repair pass on this batch and were not in the first draft, although three state files asserted them; the fifth body name, Chapter 299, was in the first draft and two state files did not assert it.** She described the room in Crown Terrace and he did not speak and she did not thank him, and a woman of about thirty in the back said nothing. **There was no scene, no declaration and no kiss, and the words *we*, *our* and *us* do not appear in a sentence about the two of them anywhere in these ten days, which has been walked and not asserted: there are thirteen instances in the ten files and all thirteen are about a room, a company, a reader, a group of people in a building or a set of heads, and none is about the two of them.** Volume 08 and Volume 15 are where this is and the outline says so.
18. **NOTHING ARRIVED FROM ANYBODY AND NOTHING IS TO ARRIVE.** The licensor is unnamed and the field is blank. The plate in a service cupboard on a landing in Nine Bridges is not explained, not removed and not blamed on anybody, and the man of thirty-four who found it did not touch it. The box in pencil in Bower Street is untouched and holds one name. **And the forward question is left open, and it is the next volume's first page: can a city of this size survive a cascade without the single response a woman said on a recording fourteen years ago that it needs. It is stated once, in his own hand, on a service road, and it is not answered, and a movement that answers it has spent the volume's stone.**

# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT IV (Chapters 281–290) — THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS, AND WHAT IT DID TO THIS LIST

**A REVIEW REPAIR PASS RAN OVER MOVEMENT IV AFTER THE BATCH WAS WRITTEN AND ALL FIVE OF ITS FINDINGS ARE APPLIED. It is written up in full at section 8 of `workspace/volume-06/batch-0004/SUMMARY.md`, which is the dated account and which also carries the corrected measurement table. Nothing was restarted: no chapter was replaced, no scene rewritten, no beat moved, no day of the day map moved, and the load-book run is still 284 to 293 with no duplicate and no gap.**

**No thread on the list above is resolved by the pass, closed by it, or weakened by it. The pass removed a duplicated draft, corrected a figure's noun, and removed a manuscript-structural unit from the narration. None of that is a plot event and none of it moves a debt.**

**What it did change, in the list's own terms:**

- **The word was said out loud once, to one person, and that stands exactly as the list above has it.** Chapter 289's prose is unchanged in that scene, in that room, in that exchange, and in what she said and did not say afterwards. **The one line the pass re-worded in that chapter is the narrator's sentence about why he said it on that afternoon, and it now says *in about four years* where it said *in six volumes*.** The reason it was a valve is the reason it was a valve.
- **The heating is on and it costs more, and the fortnight is still unpaid.** Nothing in the pass touched the heating, the four names, the notice, or the money. **The pass corrected the one sentence in these ten files that attached the room's age to the heating, which is a bookkeeping repair and not an event, and the series of days it was off still ended on the Wednesday of week ninety-nine and is still not an interval.**
- **The standing question is still not stated, still carries no number, and is still owed to Movement V.** The pass added no question and answered none.

**One new thread is added by the pass and it is owed to the volume close, not to Movement V:**

- **THE DRAFTING-REFERENCE RULE WAS A PHRASE LIST, AND IT WAS FIXED FOR THE PROSE AHEAD OF THIS VOLUME AND NOT FOR THE PROSE BEHIND IT.** The word *volume* was counting things in six of this batch's ten files, ten times, and is now in none of them. **The same construction is in the chapter prose of Chapters 251, 257, 273, 276, 277 and 278, in the summaries of Movements I, II and III, and in this batch's own prompt, which is where the prose got it.** Those chapters are closed and this phase did not and may not reopen them. **The rule in the Movement V prompt is now a rule about the unit rather than the phrase, so the prose ahead of the volume is protected and the prose behind it is not.** **Owner: the Volume 06 close, or a pass over Volumes 04 and 05. Movement V must not re-import it and its own prompt now says so in the terms that would have caught it.**

# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT IV (Chapters 281–290) — OPEN, UNPAID, AND CARRIED

**These sit above the `VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT III` block and are the live thread list. Nothing in this list is resolved. Five of them are carried whole from Movement III and the loudest thing in this movement is that the heating is on and it costs more.**

1. **THE HEATING IS ON AND IT COSTS MORE, AND NOBODY PAID FOR THE FORTNIGHT AND NOBODY WILL.** It was restored on the Wednesday of week ninety-nine by a landlord with four units who came to that one last and did not knock, and it took him about four minutes, and **no fund, collection, subscription, grant, list, minute, notice, form, leaflet, registration or appeal was put anywhere near it and no conversation about money took place in that room.** A landlord's notice arrived nine days later, signed by a managing agent and not by him, and **the number of names on the lease is the number of names that now cost more: four, and each of the four named the cost out loud before signing in a week in the spring before last.** Nobody has asked any of the four to name it again.
2. **THE WORD HAS BEEN SAID OUT LOUD ONCE, TO ONE PERSON, AND IT CANNOT BE TAKEN BACK.** Nine, ten and eleven a minute, uncorrected, in a warm room off a service road on the Thursday of week one hundred, addressed to a woman of twenty-four, with about nine people in the room. **It is not an apology and it is not a request and it is not a declaration and there was no scene.** She is not reporting it, the not reporting is her decision and not his, he cannot ask her to and cannot ask her not to, and **about four people were close enough to hear it and he did not check which four, and the not checking was the decision, and he has written down that he made it in about four seconds and would make it again.** He has not said it to the officer of sixty, has not said it to a man of fifty-eight with a bad hip, and has not said it on a service road.
3. **THE SECONDMENT IS UNCHANGED, THE CHAIR IS STILL NOT TOLD, AND THE CLERK HAS NOW SAID THE NAME OF HER OWN BUILDING OUT LOUD.** A clerk of forty-four, in a secretariat in a building in Crown Terrace, not known to be in it by anybody in that building, has checked three times that a secondment cannot be ended by anybody in this case. The second fault in the item is on a record and has not moved. **She said the name of that building out loud in a room off a service road on the Friday of week one hundred, in front of about nine people of whom about four have never been in it, and she signed the seventeenth line of the wall, and the wall is seventeen lines in eleven hands and twelve names. A person with an ordinary job is not a post and a clerk is not a mechanism and she holds nothing. Whether she says anything further on the record is still hers and is not to be arranged for her.**
4. **THE CONTINUITY OFFICE HAS SUSPENDED A LOCAL COUNCIL, IN WRITING, WITH A COUNT, ON THE GROUND OF AN EMERGENCY, AND IS NOT DEFEATED AND NOT TURNED AND AGREED TO NOTHING.** About eleven hundred premises, about nine hundred people on a list at a school hall kept by three people on a rota written on the back of a card, a pump running since the water went down, a standpipe not running, and a fortnight. **He is right about the water and wrong about the room and he said so himself, unasked, in one sentence.** The boundary is drawn around premises because a thing that cannot be counted is not a boundary, and that room is inside it. **It cost this case four people and a day room, and that is the first time in this manuscript that a room has lost something to a decision made in a building it has never been inside, and the day room next door is still shut.**
5. **THE WALL IS SEVENTEEN LINES IN ELEVEN HANDS AND TWELVE NAMES.** The sixteenth is a woman of about sixty-eight's and it is a refusal. **The seventeenth is a clerk of forty-four's, it is signed, it is about findability, and the cost of it is that in about four years about four people will be able to find her at a building she was never told she was in.** No line has been removed in these ten days and none has been rubbed off, the first line is a cook's and is still the first line, and **a later writer may carry six figures for that board: the twelve, the thirteen, the fourteen, the fifteen, the sixteen and the seventeen.**
6. **A SIGN CAME OFF A DOOR IN NINE BRIDGES AND NOBODY KNOWS WHOSE, AND SOMEBODY DELIBERATELY DID NOT FIND OUT.** A woman of thirty-four who checks about nine hundred doors on her own time went up four flights, found the sign gone, and did not put it back, did not knock, did not ask anybody on the landing, did not write it down and did not telephone. She said on the stairs that she is not going to find out who, because in about four years that person would have a person coming to their door about a sign. **Four words in chalk on a glass door in that street are still up and are still hers; a sign on a second-floor door in a district this case has not been in is still up and is still not true; this is a third state; and none of the three is the hardboard.**
7. **THE NAME OF THE PRINTED RULE WAS SAID OUT LOUD ONCE, IN A CLERK'S MOUTH, IN A ROOM IN CROWN TERRACE, AND ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE IN THAT ROOM HAD NEVER HEARD IT.** It is in no narrator's sentence, no heading, no load book and no document of this case, and **the number of that rule is in none of these ten files at all.** The rule is not hidden, has never been withdrawn, and about four hundred competent people in this city can invoke it. The hearing that read the nine sentences declined to decide the item, and the item stands open and will be on the list again.
8. **THE EXCHANGE DID NOT OPEN AT THE FOURTEENTH SITTING AND THE REASON IS NINETEEN YEARS OLD AND IS NOT NEW, AND A LINE FROM THAT BOOK HAS NOW BEEN NAMED AS HAVING LEFT THE ROOM.** The ninth of about nine lines she let be heard in nineteen years came back out of that room in a building in Crown Terrace, by a person doing their job, in a sentence carrying four words that were not the four words, and **she has never been told which line it was, has not asked, and is not going to.** The book is at forty-seven lines, the telephone is at fifty-two, the direction is stated and the difference is not, the count has been announced nineteen times, and the ninth chair was against the wall at the close and was not moved back. **A Movement V writer must read the calendar's table before writing the fifteenth sitting: it says the book opens there and goes to forty-eight lines and the telephone to fifty-four, and this movement has touched neither.**
9. **THE ROTAS, THE GAP, AND THE FRIDAY THAT RAN SHORT.** Three Fridays with a gap of about four seconds, then two with none, then a gap again, and a man of twenty-two told the instructor of thirty-six once, in a doorway, that it was not her, and she said she knows, and that is the whole of what that question has produced in about four months. **The man of about forty-four is still short of the reason and is not to be asked a third time.** On the Friday of week one hundred **the instructor of thirty-six was not there, there is no rota in this city that says who stands in that room when she is not, a man of fifty-eight with a bad hip said out loud that he is not the person who says the word and would like it written down, and the second part of the run did not happen and nobody in that room said the word.** A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts was on that week's list and was not asked to stand in for anybody.
10. **NO ROTA WAS MADE AND NO POST WAS INVENTED AND NO LIST WAS WRITTEN, AND TWO PEOPLE SAID OUT LOUD IN A FORTNIGHT THAT THEY ARE NOT THE PERSON.** A first-year of nineteen said in a doorway that she would like it noticed before somebody in that building decides that she has, and said in a room that there are more people in this city who would be told about a valve before they were told about a person than there are who would, and that she has no idea what to do with that and is not going to invent a use for it this week. **Neither has been acted on. The sentence a first-year gave in an earlier movement about there being no post in this city whose business it is to ring a person is a different object and the two are about different objects and nobody in that room is going to be told they are the same.**
11. **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY IS STILL IN THE CHAIR NEAREST THE DOOR AND HAS STILL NOT BEEN ASKED ANYTHING ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS.** She was not asked about the day room that shut, was not asked about the heat that came back, was not asked why she comes, was not thanked and was not apologised to. **About four people who had been coming for the warmth of the day room next door have not been back, and one of the four is her and she was not asked about that either.** Her page is the eighth thing in a ring binder and is unread and eight people have now decided not to read it.
12. **OREN VEY IS OWED NOTHING FURTHER, CAME BACK ONCE, AND GAVE NO SECOND FACT.** A card went back into an inside pocket and nothing was left on a counter four feet wide. **Asked who is at the end of the number in about four months he did not answer and gave the reason, and that reason is in the chapter and is not to be asked for again.** A hundred and forty-one pounds in an envelope under that till has not moved since the spring.
13. **NOTHING ARRIVED FROM ANYBODY.** The recording of eleven minutes was not copied, not read out and not destroyed, the person on it is not met, nobody went looking and nothing will arrive from her. The plate in a service cupboard in Nine Bridges is not explained, not removed and not blamed on anybody. The master's fifth line is still blank in the fourth pocket of a tool roll. The licensor is unnamed and the field is blank. The first key cannot be taken back by anybody.
14. **THE PERSON OF SIXTY-ONE IS BEA NUNN AND NOT IANTHE ROKE, and Ianthe Roke has not been asked in three movements, is one of two keys, holds the answer given aloud in an earlier volume, and is owed nothing.** Bea Nunn was not thanked in the fourteenth sitting and has not been thanked in nineteen years and is not going to be asked again about the ninth line.
15. **THE FIGURE OF ABOUT ELEVEN A WEEK IS ON THE BACK OF A WOMAN'S OWN CARD AND IS ON NO FORM AND IS NOT A RATE, and it is quoted in this movement four times, word for word, unabridged, in four load books and once in the narrator's prose, and no variant of it that drops *a release* appears anywhere in the ten chapters. The number has still not been said out loud to anybody in this case in six volumes.**
16. **THE RELATIONSHIP IS NOT RESOLVED AND WAS NOT TOUCHED AS ONE.** Talia Venn is named in the body prose of Chapters 281, 288 and 289 and in the load books of 281 and 288, and in nobody's mouth in any of the ten chapters, and she did not give her name to anybody in any room in any of them. She is the person the word was said to, and she said the sentence about not handing it over, and he asked her neither to keep it nor to let it go. **There was no scene, no declaration and no kiss, and the words *we*, *our* and *us* do not appear in a sentence about the two of them anywhere in this movement, which has been checked rather than asserted.** Volume 08 and Volume 15 are where this is, and the outline says so.

# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT III (Chapters 271–280) — OPEN, UNPAID, AND CARRIED

**These sit above the `VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT II` block and are the live thread list. Nothing in this list is resolved. Two of them were opened by this movement and are the loudest things in it.**

1. **THE SECOND FAULT IN THE ITEM IS ON A RECORD, AND THE ONLY THING KEEPING IT THERE IS A SECONDMENT NOBODY HAS BEEN TOLD ABOUT.** A clerk of forty-four put it there on the Friday before the Monday of week ninety-five, and said so out loud, once, and said that if it comes out the note comes off and that a secondment cannot be ended by anybody in this case. **The man of twenty-two is the only person in this case who could report the mechanism and he did not, on the stated ground that a person who has checked twice is not somebody who is going to be caught by accident — and then, on a service road, that he has been findable since the spring before the one before last, that this is the first time he has been glad of it, and that he does not know what to do with that.** Nobody thanked anybody. She is still seconded and the chair was still not told.
2. **A RECORDING OF ELEVEN MINUTES EXISTS, WAS HEARD ONCE, AND WAS NOT COPIED, NOT READ OUT AND NOT DESTROYED.** The author of the clause is identified once, on that tape, by herself, and by nobody else. **Iona Sorn is in the prose of this manuscript for the first time as a voice and nothing else. She is right about one of the four things she says and it is not the one she says it for, and the thing she is right about is the argument this case has been making the other way round for four years: that refusing to run the last approved sequence is a decision to let a pump run until somebody comes back, made about a stranger by somebody who will never have to look at them.** Nobody in this case has an answer to her, nobody was given one, she is not refuted and she is not defeated. **She is not met, nobody went looking for her, nothing arrived from her and nothing will, and a recording of a room in which a person was asked a question is not a message and may never be written as one.** Movement IV is where a room in Crown Terrace hears about a rule, and a rule with a name in it is a different object from a rule on a wall.
3. **HE SAID NO IN A CORRIDOR, AND THE VERSION OF THE PROCEDURE THAT WENT INTO THE RECORD IS SOMEBODY ELSE'S, AND IT IS BETTER THAN HIS.** Paid in Chapter 278 and **not to be re-argued in any later chapter.** The nine sentences are set out in the load book of that chapter in full and are deliberately verbatim, because the defect this repository owns is a load book that gets its own chapter's dialogue wrong. The cost is that he read nine sentences, found nothing in them to argue with, and said so, and that is a thing he now knows about himself and cannot un-know. Nobody thanked him and nobody was angry.
4. **THE ROTAS THE ANSWER, AND NO ROTA WAS MADE.** A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip said out loud, to be the one who says it, that none of them are going to write it, and said he is not going to say it again. A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts has said for the first time in this case that he is on a rota and that it has been a Tuesday for about nine years. A woman of thirty-four has said in one room that nobody has ever asked her whether she would rather it was a card. **A first-year of nineteen has said to a room that there is no post in this city whose business it is to ring a person, that about four rooms full of people a year could hold that job and none of them do, and that every one of about four of them has said in that room that somebody should. It was not acted on in this run and it is not to be acted on in the next.**
5. **THE WALL IS SIXTEEN LINES IN TEN HANDS AND ELEVEN NAMES, AND THE SIXTEENTH IS A REFUSAL.** A woman of about sixty-eight was asked to take down a sign that is not true and did not, on the ground that she does not know who is behind that door, and the cost is the opposite of the fifteenth line's: the fifteenth made a man findable and he hated it, and this makes a woman the person who is asked and gives her the only thing she has, which is the power to say no, at the price of about four people in about four years deciding she could not. **No line has been removed and none has been rubbed off, the first line is a cook's and is still the first line, and a later writer may carry five figures for that board: the twelve, the thirteen, the fourteen, the fifteen and the sixteen.**
6. **A SIGN ON A SECOND-FLOOR DOOR IN A DISTRICT THIS CASE HAS NOT BEEN IN IS STILL UP AND IS STILL NOT TRUE,** and there is a person behind it that nobody in this case knows about, and a man of thirty-eight was told nothing and wrote nothing and is not going to be rung. **Four words in chalk on a glass door in Nine Bridges are hers and are still up: *Nobody answers. Ring again.*** About four people in this city will, in about four years, know that she can take a sign down, and one of them has already been told she did not.
7. **THE FAMILY AND THE CARE-LINK ARE RIGHT AND IT IS THE THING NOBODY CAN END, AND THE QUESTION OF WHO RINGS A PERSON WHOSE ARRANGEMENT HAS STOPPED WAS NOT ASKED BY ANYBODY IN THIS RUN.** The mother can cancel it from her own chair. What cannot be cancelled is what follows, and nobody in this city has the job of finding that out. **The family is not a case, is not a resource, is not counted, was not thanked, and nobody is to go and get them.**
8. **THE REASON A BOOK OF WHAT A CALLER SAID CANNOT BE WRITTEN BY THE PEOPLE WHO DID NOT CALL IS NINETEEN YEARS OLD AND IS NOT BEA NUNN'S TO GIVE, and the only person who could say it out loud with any right to is not findable and has not been for nineteen years.** She was asked where it came from, once, and refused before she was told what she had asked, and nobody asked a second question and nobody asked who the person was. **It is not to be put in a building in Crown Terrace, it is not to go on a page, and the ground is in the load book of Chapter 274 in her own words.**
9. **THE BOOK OF WHAT A CALLER SAID IS AT FORTY-SEVEN LINES AND THE TELEPHONE COUNT ANNOUNCED AT THE THIRTEENTH SITTING IS FORTY-NINE,** and the count has now been announced eighteen times, thirteen of them on the thirteen Wednesdays this book holds and five predating it on a sheet not shown to anybody. **The next sitting is the Wednesday of week one hundred, Chapter 288, where the book does not open and the count goes to fifty-two.** The ninth chair is against the wall, its mover is not named, and the young woman of twenty-one who put it there is not named and is not asked anything.
10. **THE FOUR SECONDS ARE NOT SETTLED AND ARE NOT TO BE SETTLED FOR A TIDY REASON.** About four seconds on two consecutive Fridays, and no gap at all on the third, and a man of twenty-two and a man of about forty-four both short of the reason and both knowing it. Nobody was thanked and nobody was asked a second time. **A later writer may settle it or may not.**
11. **THE HEATING IS ON ITS HUNDRED AND FIFTH DAY AND IS UNPAID AND UNRESOLVED.** It comes back in Chapter 285, the Wednesday of week ninety-nine, because a landlord put it on, and the notice arrives in Chapter 290. **No fund, collection, subscription, grant, list, minute, notice, form, leaflet, registration or appeal was put anywhere near it in this movement and none may be in the next, and the same week a company offers to pay for the next winter and the two things are indistinguishable from inside the room.**
12. **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY IS STILL IN THE CHAIR NEAREST THE DOOR AND HAS STILL NOT BEEN ASKED ANYTHING.** She is the person a fortnight of unpaid heating was paid for, and her page is in the ring binder and eight people have now decided not to read it, and it is still there. **Nobody apologises to her, because an apology would have been a question, and she is not to be named, counted as a resource, described at the public body, or put in any photograph.**
13. **THE WORD HAS NOT BEEN SAID OUT LOUD TO ANYBODY IN SIX VOLUMES, and the rate held at nine and ten on all ten days of this movement and reached eleven on none of them.** It is said out loud once, to one person, in Movement IV, and nothing in this movement rehearses it, prices it or prepares it.
14. **THE MASTER'S FIFTH LINE IS STILL BLANK AND NOBODY HAS EVER ASKED HIM TO FILL IT IN, and it is still in the fourth pocket of a tool roll in a repair shop in Lattice Ward.** The licensor is unnamed and the field is blank. The first key cannot be taken back by anybody. The nine words wrong in a minute. The four hundred and one replies and the nine who got the company answer. A hundred and forty-one pounds in an envelope that cannot move. Three photographs in a drawer in Nine Bridges. A company's photograph on page two and a shutter speed of about a fortieth of a second. A prospectus in another city read by about four hundred people. A girl's description on a board in a public corridor and not her name. Nine Fridays and about ninety pounds. The second visitor column's five other words. The bit of pale card in a gate hut. The six lines in a register in Crown Terrace, one of which has an author still in this city. Steve Akers's nine words. The old man of sixty-one who was given an answer about four years ago and has gone quiet and cannot be found; he is not named, nothing has been sent to him, and nothing will be.
15. **THE FIGURE OF ABOUT ELEVEN A WEEK IS ON THE BACK OF A WOMAN'S OWN CARD AND IS ON NO FORM AND IS NOT A RATE,** and it is quoted in this movement three times, word for word, in three load books, and no variant of it that drops *a release* appears anywhere in the ten chapters. **The number has still not been said out loud to anybody in this case in six volumes.**

# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT II (Chapters 261–270) — OPEN, UNPAID, AND CARRIED

**These sit above the `VOLUME 06 MOVEMENT I` blocks and are the live thread list. Nothing in this list is resolved. Two of the six were opened by this movement and are the loudest things in it.**

1. **A ROOM CANNOT ASK TO BE HEARD AGAIN AND A PERSON HAS TO.** Said at about eleven twenty-five on the Monday morning of week ninety-two, by a chair of forty-nine with an ordinary job, to a room and not to any person in it, and it is twelve words, and it is owed to nobody and it is on nobody, and the record that holds it belongs to a committee. **Nothing in this city acts on it and nothing can, because the thing it names is the absence of a mechanism, and the volume's remaining forty chapters are about the absence of a mechanism.** It is not to be restated as a line on the hardboard, and it is not to be improved on, and nobody is to be thanked for it.
2. **THE SECOND FAULT IN THE ITEM IS OPEN AND WAS RAISED IN THE ROOM AND MET WITH A SMALLER QUESTION.** That a practice cannot show that anybody may stop at any time, because the only evidence is a record of who stopped, that a record of who stopped is a column, and that about eleven thousand premises have no column and three were asked and one of them said the word budget out loud first. **It was found a fortnight before the hearing by the person whose job it is to ask the questions, she was right, she did nothing about it, the hearing went ahead anyway, and the item is now with her.**
3. **THE CLERK OF FORTY-FOUR IS SECONDED TO THE SECRETARIAT OF THE COMMITTEE THAT HEARD THE ITEM AND THE CHAIR OF FORTY-NINE WAS NOT TOLD.** A secondment is not a conspiracy and nobody in this case can end it. **The chair may be named in Movement III and was not named here.** The man of twenty-two saw the shape of it and did not raise it. Whether she says anything on the record is hers and is not to be arranged for her.
4. **A PLATE IN A SERVICE CUPBOARD ON A FOURTH LANDING IN NINE BRIDGES, not explained, not removed, not blamed on anybody, with four things on its face and no fifth, carrying the block's own number, and not there in the spring.** Found by a man of thirty-four who works nights, who did not touch it, did not open the box behind it, did not photograph it and did not report it, and who told one person about it by telephone at ten past five on a Friday morning. **He wrote nothing down. The only record of the plate in this case is one man keeping a book against himself on a service road, the finder asked for it to be left off, and he was told he would rather be written down than asked twice; a repairer in Lattice Ward did write a different thing on the back of his own hand in an earlier volume and that is a different man and a different object, and no writer may merge the two. It is not explained in this volume, it is not removed, and it is not blamed on anybody. The next writer may not explain it, remove it or blame anybody for it, and the word that must not appear is *carrier*.**
5. **A MAN OF THIRTY-FOUR WHO WORKS NIGHTS IS NOW THE ONLY PERSON IN THIS CASE WHO CAN BE FOUND AT A DOOR, and that is the last thing he wanted and he has wanted it not to be true for about four years, and he wrote the volume's fifteenth line about it.** He is still a count and has no name in this manuscript. **The volume close owns his naming, and whether he is ever named is a live question rather than an oversight.** He has said in a room that not going back into that cupboard is a decision and not a thing that lasts, and that nobody is going to write that down.
6. **THE COOK OF TWENTY-SEVEN'S FOUR WORDS ARE ON ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED LINES IN FOUR YEARS IN ABOUT NINE BUILDINGS THE MAN OF FIFTY-ONE HAS WORKED IN, in a different hand every time, of which two hundred and forty are on one sheet in one service corridor and the rest are in eight more buildings, and she does not know and is not going to be told.** The ground for not telling her is on the page and is not manners: four hundred lines is a bigger surface than a hardboard in a building with a telephone number and no name on it. **A later writer must not tell her for a tidy reason.**
7. **A MAN OF SIXTY-ONE WAS GIVEN AN ANSWER BY A STRANGER ABOUT FOUR YEARS AGO AND HAS GONE QUIET, and a room of about forty people in Crown Terrace knows he still has nine words of his own, and nobody in this case can hand him four words because he cannot be found.** He is not named, nothing has been sent to him, nothing will be, and there is no mechanism in this city for arriving at a person. He is not to be given the four words in Movement III either.
8. **A PAGE OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE PEOPLE HAS NOW BEEN READ ALOUD ONCE, IN ABOUT NINE MINUTES, TO ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, BY A CLERK OF FIFTY-THREE WHO HAD NEVER READ IT OUT.** It changed nothing, she said so before she started, and a district officer who asked whether it could be recorded properly with a reference was refused on the ground that it would work. **The pencil box in Bower Street is untouched, holds one name, takes no second name, has no reference number, is not on a system and has been adopted by no district, and the day any of that changes the volume's argument is over.**
9. **THE EXCHANGE'S BOOK IS AT FORTY-SIX LINES, THE TELEPHONE COUNT ANNOUNCED AT THE TWELFTH SITTING IS FORTY-SEVEN, AND THE SIGN HAS CHANGED** from the book being two lines ahead to the telephone being one line ahead. She said the direction out loud once and did not look into it. **The next sitting is the Wednesday of week ninety-six: the book opens, goes to forty-seven lines, and the count goes to forty-nine.** The ninth chair is against the wall, its mover is not named, and the young woman of twenty-one who put it there is not named and is not asked anything.
10. **A DELIVERY STOPPAGE WAS SETTLED IN ABOUT FOUR MINUTES BY A COMPANY AND ITS DRIVERS, and no person in this case was in that room and nobody in this case heard the terms.** The woman of forty-four has not come back to the room off the service road and is owed no explanation by anybody and was given none. **Asha Reed is not going to be her friend for about a term, and that is the price of a room that is open.**
11. **THE COMPANY THAT WAS REFUSED IN WRITING IN MOVEMENT I IS OWED NOTHING FURTHER AND HAS SENT NOTHING THIS TERM. It comes back in Movement IV with a product, and nothing in Movement II staged it, hinted at it or named it.**
12. **THE HEATING IS ON ITS EIGHTIETH DAY AT CHAPTER 270 AND IS UNPAID AND UNRESOLVED.** It comes back in Chapter 285, the Wednesday of week ninety-nine, because a landlord put it on, and the notice arrives in Chapter 290. **No fund, collection, subscription, grant, list, minute, notice, report, form, leaflet, registration or appeal was put anywhere near it in Movement II and none may be in Movement III. The same week a company offers to pay for the next winter, and the two things are indistinguishable from inside the room.**
13. **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY IS STILL IN THE CHAIR NEAREST THE DOOR AND HAS STILL NOT BEEN ASKED ANYTHING.** She is the person a fortnight of unpaid heating was paid for, and in nineteen weeks she has said two sentences and both of them were about her own position. **Nobody apologises to her, because an apology would have been a question, and she is not to be named, counted as a resource, described at the public body, or put in any photograph.**
14. **THE WORD HAS NOT BEEN SAID OUT LOUD TO ANYBODY IN SIX VOLUMES, and the rate held at nine and ten on all ten days of this movement and reached eleven on none of them.** It is said out loud once, to one person, in Movement IV, and nothing in this movement rehearses it, hinted at it or priced it.
15. **THE MASTER'S FIFTH LINE IS STILL BLANK AND NOBODY HAS EVER ASKED HIM TO FILL IT IN. The licensor is unnamed and the field is blank on three documents in three buildings and on a company's stock docket. The first key cannot be taken back by anybody. The nine words wrong in a minute. The four hundred and one replies and the nine who got the company answer. A hundred and forty-one pounds in an envelope that cannot move. Three photographs in a drawer in Nine Bridges that cannot be handed back. A company's photograph on page two and a shutter speed of about a fortieth of a second. A prospectus in another city read by about four hundred people. A girl's description on a board in a public corridor and not her name. Nine Fridays and about ninety pounds. The second visitor column's five other words. The bit of pale card in a gate hut. A hundred and forty-one pounds. The six lines in a register in Crown Terrace, one of which has an author still in this city. Steve Akers's nine words. A machine in a basement in Nine Bridges stuck for about four years.**
16. **THE FIGURE OF ABOUT ELEVEN A WEEK IS ON THE BACK OF A WOMAN'S OWN CARD AND IS ON NO FORM AND IS NOT A RATE,** and it is quoted in this movement six times, word for word, in three chapters, in two of them in a load book and one in the body.

## ARCHIVE — the Volume 01, 02 and 03 thread lists, compacted in the review repair pass of Volume 04 batch 0001

**The reasoning under each of these threads was removed from this file in the review repair pass of Volume 04 batch 0001, because this file had reached 223 KB and the next writer could not fit its own mandated read list. What is kept here is every thread, in order, with the status word it was closed with — which is the part that must never be lost, because a thread marked SPENT may not be reopened, re-proved or re-argued. The full text of every thread is in the git history of this file, and the dated hand-offs that carry the reasoning forward are `workspace/volume-01/batch-0006/VOLUME-CLOSE.md`, `workspace/volume-02/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md` and `workspace/volume-03/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md`.**

**Nothing in this section is live state. The live threads are the `VOLUME 03 CLOSED` block and the `VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT I` blocks below, and those are what a Volume 04 writer reads.**

### The nine rules that govern the whole list, and they are the reason the list is kept in this form

1. **The licensor is unnamed and the field is blank, and no volume may name the Crown Clause in prose or identify an authorship.** The names Iona and Sorn appear nowhere in Volume 04.
2. **Threadline is the contracted assembler and the seller and is never the author, and it is never defeated.** Oren Vey is owed nothing further and gives no second fact. Petar Vance gave one fact and can deny it.
3. **The seven are still seven, the four things that are not the seven are still four, and the word *pattern* does not appear anywhere.**
4. **Asha Reed's sentence has been said seven times and is never said again, and it is not said in Volume 04.**
5. **The Exchange's book does not go to forty-five. Bea Nunn will not open it on a Friday, and a room of people who did not call is not nine callers.**
6. **The relay was staged once, in Chapter 136, and cost a carrier who was not asked first. It may not be staged again in any form and no page may imply it got easier.**
7. **Nell Senn is unwritten and is not a device, and no volume may write about her or resolve her cheaply.**
8. **There is no post in this city whose business it is to look at a stranger's wrist, and none may be invented. A named person with an ordinary job is not a post.**
9. **Rafi Pell is not forgiven the word *variant* and nobody took a vote about him.**

### The thread list itself, Volumes 01 to 03, as each was closed

- 1. The missing beat — PROVED DESIGNED IN CHAPTER 25 AND SPENT. DO NOT RE-PROVE IT IN ANY VOLUME.
- 2. The Solo Seal — REFUSED AND FINAL.
- 3. The licensor — BLANK, STILL BLANK, AND NOW IN TWO PLACES.

- 5. Evan's scratched card — USED AS A TOOL AND REFUSED AS A KEY, AND STILL NOT EXPLAINED.
- 6. Marek and Talia — MILESTONE 5 ACHIEVED, AND IT WAS NOT A RESOLUTION.
- 7. The public institution — ONE LEDGER IN A REPAIR SHOP, AND IT IS NOT ONE.  **THE COUNT IS FIVE, NOT FOUR, AND MUST NOT BE WRITTEN DOWN AS FOUR AGAIN**
- 8. Marek's counterpoint scar — STAGE 1, FIRST KNOT, WITH ONE CLEAR FAILURE.
- 9. The unresolved consequence — WORSE, AND PERMANENT, AND NOT RESET.
- 10. The records request — NOT FILED, AND THE CLOCK IS STILL RUNNING.
- 11. The second dependence — NOT CURED AND NOT TO BE CURED BY A DEMONSTRATION.
- 12. "A telephone number a stranger can ring in a crisis is the whole of it, and I have been an idiot for a term."
- 13. THE NEW THREAD, AND IT IS THE VOLUME'S LAST DISCOVERY: A RULE THAT GOVERNS A PAGE CANNOT GOVERN A MOUTH.
- 14. THE FIFTH ROOM IS SHUT AND IS STILL A QUESTION.
- 15. THE THIRD THING ON A RELAY PLATE.
- 16. The service bulletin — THE NUMBER GOES UPWARD AND SOMEBODY HAS BEEN IN THE DEPOT.
- 17. Oren Vey's trial is declined and the job is at the start of next term, and he is owed one more thing.
- 18. Rafi says yes too early — SLOWED, AND STILL NOT FORGIVEN.
- 19. Nell Senn — UNRESOLVED, UNWRITTEN, AND NOT A DEVICE.
- 20. The three independent lines — BUILT, DEMONSTRATED, AND NOT CONVERTED.
- 21. Consent posture — DEMONSTRATED FOUR MORE TIMES AND ONE CONSENT UNSPENT.
- 22. The Choir of Practice — CAPABLE OF BEING WRONG, UNKIND ON PURPOSE, AND THE ONLY BODY THAT PRINTED THE CASE.
- 23. The Institute's manual is still 3-08 and Rusk still requisitioned the reprint himself in March.
- 24. The three accounts of the case do not match, and there is no office whose job it is to say which one is the account.
- 25. The March second page.
- 26. The teacher who will not be taught.
- 27. The unnamed member of the Choir is still unnamed and is still not to be made findable.
- 28. A card in a goods yard off the Meridian is still not held.
- 29. Delia Frost's two times, and her sentence.
- 30. The person whose job it is.  **THE THING THAT WAS BUILT INSTEAD IS A LEDGER, AND IT IS NOT THE SAME THING, AND NKECHI OSEI SAYS SO IN A REPAIR SHOP**

- 31. What a failed condition is. SPENT as a question and expensive as an answer.
- 32. The reason he was handed, and did not take. OPEN AND DELIBERATELY UNRESOLVED.
- 33. Ruslan Petric and the box at the Weaver Street post office. HALF SPENT, AND THE HALF IS WORSE.

- 39. The hinge. DETECTED, NOT NAMED, AND IT RELEASES NOBODY.
- 40. A boy of twenty is going to buy a card off the company that made the room. OPEN, AND IT IS THE MORAL CENTRE.

- 42. Aurel Kosta's yard. OPEN, AND IT IS NOW THE VOLUME'S ARGUMENT IN A WORKING MAN'S MOUTH.  **MAREK DID NOT SPEAK AND THE REASON HE DID NOT IS THAT ANYTHING HE SAID WOULD MAKE HIM THE FOURTH THING IN A YARD**
- 43. A row removed. NEW, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME ANYTHING IN THIS CASE WAS ACTUALLY TAKEN OUT OF ANYTHING.

- 52. The job. TAKEN, SIGNED IN A SHOP, AND NOT UNDONE.
- 53. THE FIFTH CLAUSE. NOT SATISFIED. NEW, AND IT IS THE BATCH'S DECISION.  **MOVEMENT III MUST ACCOUNT FOR WHAT**
- 54. A SENTENCE IN CAPITALS IS NOW IN A COLLEGE'S FILE. NEW, AND IT IS THE WORST OF THE TEN DOCUMENTS.  **RELEASE IS GIVEN BY ANOTHER VOICE**

- 64. THE COURSE FORM, POSTED, CARRIED ON FOOT, WITH TWO EMPTY BOXES ON PURPOSE.
- 65. REUBEN DAW'S SIX WEEKS OF SATURDAYS. RESOLVED AS ARITHMETIC AND NOT AS A DISCOVERY.
- 66. The chairs have gone, and the office that could not take a complaint moved them.
- 67. THE SECOND DEPENDENCE, AND IT IS NOT CURED AND IT IS NOT A CURE.
- 68. TALIA VENN'S COUNT AND HER REFUSAL, AND NOW A REGIONAL RETURN.
- 69. THE REFUSALS IN THIS MOVEMENT, ALL THREE OF WHICH COST SOMEBODY.  **NOBODY IN THIS MOVEMENT IS THANKED AND NOBODY IS FORGIVEN, AND FENWICK**

**What Movement III closed, and what it closed *against***

- 70. THE FIFTH CLAUSE IS TOLD ABOUT, AND THE ANSWER IS SURVIVABLE AND COSTS SOMETHING.
- 71. THE PRICE MINUTED ON THE THURSDAY OF WEEK FOURTEEN IS PAID IN FULL, BY TAM ARRICK, AND BY NOBODY ELSE.
- 72. THE NUMBER IS SPOKEN, IN CHAPTER 75, AND ONLY THERE. SPENT. Do not spend it again in any form.
- 73. THE COURIER'S FIFTEEN BOXES, READ OUT ALOUD, AND THE TWENTY-FIVE THAT WILL NOT GO IN.

- 77. VEY LOSES THE ONLY THING HE HAS WANTED SINCE THE EIGHTH WEEK OF THE SUMMER, AND THE REASON WAS FOUR SENTENCES A THIRD-YEAR IS BEING PAID FOR.
- 78. THREADLINE IS PROFITABLE FROM THE SHAPE AND IS NOT THE AUTHOR OF IT, AND IT IS NOW CANON IN Oren Vey's OWN MOUTH.  **THE LOT LEADS TO A BUILDING**
- 79. THE BATCH'S DECISION, MADE AND UNSPENT: HE IS GOING TO ASK, ON THE MONDAY OF WEEK TWENTY-TWO, TO BE PUT ON A SERVICE CALLOUT FOR MERIDIAN CONSOLIDATION POINT, LOT ONWARD.
- 80. THE REFUSALS IN THIS MOVEMENT, ALL SEVEN, AND ALL SEVEN COST SOMEBODY.

**What Movement IV inherits, dated — with everything reset**


**VOLUME 02 — Movement IV closed (Chapters 81–90), and what Movement V inherits**


**What Movement IV closed, and what it closed *against***

- 81. HE ASKED FOR A DOOR AND GOT ONE IN THREE SECONDS, AND THE COST OF IT IS A REFUSAL HE DID NOT EXPECT.
- 82. THE REVIEW HAS BEEN CALLED, AND IT WAS CALLED BY A FORM.  **THE SITTING IS IN MOVEMENT V**
- 83. THE BATCH'S TURN, AND IT IS NOT A TRAGY. Rafi Pell asked a company for a thing and he got it, in four seconds, off a number on a card, and he is delighted.
- 84. THE BATCH'S STRUCTURAL FINDING, which is not a discovery and is his, and which reframes the whole case: THE CARD IS NOT WHAT IS DOING THE WORK. THE CARD IS THE RECEIPT, AND A RECEIPT IS WHAT YOU SHOW A COMPANY THAT HAS STOPPED ANSWERING ITS TELEPHONE, AND THE ENTIRE EDIFICE IS STANDING ON ABOUT NINETEEN MEN IN THIS CITY WHO HAVE NEVER BEEN ISSUED ANYTHING AND HAVE NEVER ONCE BEEN ASKED.
- 85. A SLOT SHAPED LIKE A PERSON, MADE BY A WOMAN OF THIRTY-ONE AT HER OWN KITCHEN TABLE.
- 86. A LAMINATED CARD ON A CONTROL POST AT A SITE OFF THE MERIDIAN, WITH NO NAME, NO DATE, NO COMPANY AND NO VERSION ON IT, AND IT HAS BEEN THERE AT LEAST TWENTY YEARS.
- 87. THE ONLY ADDRESS LEFT IN THE CASE WAS IN A TRAY IN A PREFAB OFFICE, AND HE DID NOT WRITE IT DOWN.
- 88. THE COST OF NOT WRITING THE BOX NUMBER DOWN, and it is the first thing in this case he could be called for.
- 89. YOU LOT ARE QUICK THIS YEAR.
- 90. THE LEAK'S CONSEQUENCES, TWO MORE OF THEM, AND THEY ARE PERSONAL.
- 91. THE DRAWER UNDER THE BENCH HAS THREE THINGS IN IT AND NONE OF THEM CAN BE PRODUCED TO ANYBODY.
- 92. TALIA VENN ANSWERED THE REGIONAL OFFICE ON THE TUESDAY OF WEEK TWENTY-TWO.
- 93. HE LIED TO HER ONCE, AND HE TOLD HER ELEVEN DAYS LATER, AND IT WAS ABOUT A PERSON.
- 94. SEVEN PEOPLE HAVE INDEPENDENTLY INVENTED THE SAME PROTECTION AND NONE OF THEM HAS MET ANOTHER, AND HE HAS REFUSED TO CALL IT A PATTERN.

# VOLUME 03 CLOSED. Batch 0005 (Chapters 141–150) closed *The Taker*, and what Volume 04 inherits, dated

**Volume 03 is CLOSED at Chapter 150. The load-book run ends at entry 153 and Volume 04's first entry is 154.** The full hand-off is `workspace/volume-03/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md`; this block is the thread list and it is not a summary of the volume.

## What Chapters 149 and 150 closed, and what they closed *against*

1. **THE VOLUME'S RESOLUTION, AND IT IS A BOX IN PENCIL ON A FORM ON A COUNTER, WITH NO REFERENCE NUMBER AND NO POST.** A date, a form, a person whose job it is to telephone, and a box for the person who removed a share, ruled by hand in pencil about the size of a stamp under the fourth field of a district page that has four fields and one of them is a tick, with three words over it in a clerk's hand — *who ended it*. **It is a form and not a system, nothing was typed, nothing was copied, nothing was entered anywhere, it is empty on the day it was made, and the woman of thirty-one who put it there is not thanked and the clerk has not mentioned her since.** Closed against: any reading in which the box is a mechanism, a policy, a form change or a post. **The answer to who takes the name out is still nobody. The answer to who telephones a care-link review is still a person with a diary.**
2. **THE NURSE'S ONE CLEAN CALL A WEEK WAS SPENT, FOUR WEEKS LATER, IN NINE SECONDS, AND NEITHER WOMAN EVER SAYS SO.** A district nurse of twenty-four rang the office in Bower Street on the Thursday of week forty-four, about her own reasons, as she has for four years, and said the name of a district and nothing else. **It is in a diary, the page has not been shown, and Marek Senn does not know.** Closed against: any reading in which the Chapter 145 promise is paid as a scene, a thank you, or a phone call anybody makes to anybody about.
3. **THE PLATE'S ELEVEN WEEKS ARE PAID AND THE WALL IS STILL WRONG.** Somebody came in the week of week thirty-seven with a fault ticket that carried no name, read it against the wall, wrote 2-14 on it again in the building's own typeface and closed the ticket. **That was the correct action eight weeks before Chapter 149 and the room is still on a shelf, about four hundred consent leaflets in the rack are all printed 2-07, and about forty-one people in that district are still on a link nobody can end.** Closed against: any reading in which a clock expiring repairs anything.
4. **THE PAGE THE BOX IS FOR ALREADY EXISTS AND CANNOT BE FIXED.** Four fields filled in and the tick and a date and a time in a hand that is not hers, from the Tuesday of week thirty-eighth, when a care-link came off a woman of sixty-eight in four seconds and a bay went off eleven minutes later and nobody was hurt. **No box on that page because there was no box to rule, and no person anywhere on it, and there is not going to be.** Closed against: any reading in which the new box retroactively fills the old form or gets anybody's name onto it.
5. **ROY AMISS IS FIFTY-ONE AND HE IS ON THE PAGE IN THE CLOSE AND HE IS NOT PAID.** A frame and a stick, the leaflet on his own table at home, one question, nine true words that do not answer it, and *nobody rang me before or after and I am not saying that to anybody.* **Nobody rang him before or after that either, and he is owed a thing that is not a leaflet and there is no form for it.** Closed against: any reading in which he is comforted, thanked or found.
6. **RAFI PELL GAVE THE WORSE ANSWER, NOT THE BETTER ONE.** Not being the reason it got a name on it is not a thing you can hand to somebody else at a door, and nine people in this city have said that and it did not do anything. **The third practice is still his, he has still not signed it, the grey card has two names on it and neither of them is Marek Senn, the word *variant* is not forgiven, and nobody took a vote about him and nobody asked him to stop.** Closed against: any reading in which he is promoted, forgiven or made a lieutenant.
7. **THE VOLUME'S LAST SENTENCE IS THE PRICE, AND IT IS NOT A VICTORY.** About eleven people a week are still going to be taught that a release waits for a man in a lanyard that this case has just made unnecessary, and the line under it is that nobody says so. **The nine words — nobody is going to say whose practice this is — are said out loud once in Chapter 150, in a car park, to nobody, and not said again.** Closed against: any reading in which the close converts the cost into progress.

## What Volume 04 inherits, dated — with everything reset

- **Volume 04 is Chapters 151 to 200 and its series title is *Civic Weave*.** Its central pressure, per `outline/series.md`: the team enters the old network archives while Iven Sore's office seals records connected to Marrow Falls. Its midpoint: the Civic Compact originally required every technique to show its return path, and an unauthorised working-copy amendment inserted during Marrow Falls overrode that visible exit without announcing the change. Its climax: a partial maintenance ledger comes out of the Crown Vault. Its resolution: the ledger survives in public custody, Marek becomes a recognised researcher and not a public hero, and the Continuity Office keeps its emergency authority.
- **TWO THINGS IN THAT SKETCH ARE CONTRADICTED BY THE PAGE AND HAVE TO BE RE-BASED BEFORE IT IS DRAFTED, and both are recorded in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md` section 10.** (1) **The power state.** The sketch says *Stage 2, with the first sustained relay*, and the relay was staged once in Chapter 136, cost a person who was not asked first and who stopped it, and may not be staged again. The correct Volume 04 state is **Stage 2 Counterpoint with a permanent sensitivity, an uncorrectable word, a demonstrated-but-unrepeatable relay, and a fourth condition that stops him doing the thing and does not stop him being asked about it.** Growth has to be earned somewhere other than the relay. (2) **The recognition.** *A recognised researcher* is the right line and the only usable version of it, **because the fourth condition makes him the most findable person in the city and findability is the hazard.** A recognition that arrives with a title is a name on the front of a document, and a man of forty-four has already written *no name* in two boxes in his own hand.
- **The first question of Volume 04, taken from the ending of this volume and not from the series sketch:** *what is the difference between a form that has one box in it and a form that has none — and who is supposed to be the difference, given that the person who drew the box was told twice that there was no box for it.*
- **Dated, fixed, and not to be reset. Week 46 = Mon 208 – Sun 214. The Exchange runs on four-week spacing: 16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, and therefore 48. The next Exchange is the Wednesday of week 48, and Bea Nunn will not open the book on a Friday.** The book is at forty-four lines, the fortieth line is three years old, line forty-four has a hole in it where a woman of sixty-eight in bay four should have been, and it is not a register. **A line in `state/current.md` saying the next Exchange was the Wednesday of week forty-five was wrong and has been corrected.**
- **No month-name, no month-date, no day-date, no year and no day number in the prose**, and the interval map's two-day offset from the Monday of week thirty-one is inherited, is recorded for the fourth time, and is not to be re-derived. **No chapter in fifty printed a day number.**
- **The specification runs to four sections and had two lines and a funded third line of nine words, and the fund's committee does not meet again and nothing may be settled by a committee, a minute, a notice, a report, a leaflet or a box that a system produced.**
- **The master is in an inside pocket on a chair in a repair shop in Lattice Ward, and the decision is unmade after a term and a half. The original is in the fourth pocket of the tool roll with the fifth line still blank. The drawer under the bench holds five things, four of them other people's sentences.**
- **The box in pencil may take a name in Volume 04 and may not take a reference number, a system, a post or a district.**
- **The relay may not be staged again in any form, and the carrier may not be asked again.** Marta Selby's round was re-cut on the Monday of week forty and she is not going to do it again for him, for a district, or for a woman in Crown Terrace with a clipboard.
- **Series milestone 3 is spent and may not be spent again. Milestone 4 belongs to Volumes 05 and 06.** The bench is not reopened, Chapter 102's Tuesday is not revisited, the Chapter 7 page stays closed, and the volume's resolution was done by a woman of thirty-one who is not Talia Venn.
- **The seven is still seven, the four things that are not the seven are still four, and the word *pattern* does not appear in Volume 03 and may not appear in Volume 04.** Asha Reed's sentence is not said again. Steve Akers is not given to anybody. A woman of thirty-four with a hip is not found. Nell Senn is unwritten. The Crown Clause is never named and the names Iona and Sorn appear nowhere.
- **Open, unpaid, and not to be closed by a document:** the licensor, still blank; the second visitor column's five other words; the bit of pale card in a gate hut, and the fact that a room in Crown Terrace knows a man of sixty-one has it; the unsigned sheet and its four correct steps; the printer's customer who paid eleven pounds eleven times; the woman of thirty-nine and her page of her own; the four hundred and one replies and the nine who got the company answer; the nine words wrong in a minute; the fifth room and who takes the name out; the unlabelled bay, the list of named holders, the boy at the control post, Delia Frost's landing, Nkechi Osei's Mondays; **Roy Amiss's unringed telephone**; **the hundred and forty-one pounds in a bag under a till with no name on it**; **and the box number and the four-letter post town, in two hands, one of them a telephone on a wall that nobody has rung.**
