# VOLUME 06 MOVEMENT I — A THIRD PASS ON CHAPTERS 251 TO 260, AND WHAT IT CHANGED

**This block is the live state. It sits above the `VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT I` block, which is correct in substance and is superseded on the three points named in it. Nothing below it was deleted and the full record is section 7 of `workspace/volume-06/batch-0001/SUMMARY.md`. The next phase is Volume 06 batch 0002, Movement II, Chapters 261 to 270, and its prompt is `workspace/volume-06/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`.**

**1. THE NAME. Talia Venn is now named in the prose of Chapters 252, 253, 255, 258, 259 and 260, and in the load books of FIVE OF THOSE SIX — 253, 255, 258, 259 and 260 — and Chapter 252's entry does not name her, because her only appearance in that chapter is in the body at line 21 and the entry records a day that did not turn on her. The earlier figure of six was wrong and was corrected at the fourth pass. She is also named in the opening paragraph of 259 and of 260 so that the milestone chapter and the close each stand on their own. She was named nowhere before this pass, and the batch's own hard stop required it, and the second pass over Chapters 251 to 260 did not catch it. THE ARRANGEMENT INSIDE THE ARRANGEMENT, WHICH A LATER WRITER MUST NOT UNDO: no character in any of the ten chapters says her name out loud, and she does not give it to anybody. The narrator may name her and nobody in a room may.** The reason is the volume's own. Her stated position is that she has been asked to be findable three times in a term and a half and has said no three times, and Chapter 260 turns on the cost of a line whose handwriting is findable in about four years. A name in a book and a name on a surface in a building with a plate on the outside of it are two different objects, and this movement has spent itself proving that a rule and a line are two different objects, so the distinction is now load-bearing for a person as well. Chapter 252 states the arrangement in one sentence and gives no reason: the woman of sixty knew her name, did not use it once in that room, and the reason is not on anything. **The defect to look for in a later pass is a scene in which she hands her name to somebody who did not have it. That is not a thing this pass introduced and it is not a thing a later pass should add.**

**2. THE MEASUREMENT. Both bold ceilings are now met and this is the first time either has been true. The batch is at 9.20 bold spans per thousand against a ceiling of 10.75, and the worst chapter is 11.60 against a ceiling of 12.4, down from 11.47 and 19.97. The whole reduction is `**` delimiters taken off speech, and NOT ONE WORD OF PROSE CHANGED WHILE IT CAME DOWN, which is verified by stripping every `**` from the current files and from the files as they stood and comparing byte for byte. The rule applied was to move the emphasis rather than thin the page: the interrogative frame and the connective replies go plain, the payload keeps the span.** Nine lines that carried a bold span on both sides of one speech tag are fixed, and one whole bold paragraph that was in the body narration of Chapter 251 rather than in speech or in a load-book entry is fixed, and the count of the markup defect is nine and not the eleven an earlier pass printed for it.

**3. WHAT IS STILL OPEN AND IS NOT OWNED BY A WRITING PASS.** The hedge rate is unchanged at **481 true hedges in 27,269 words, which is 17.64 per thousand**, and the prompt caps no hedge figure anywhere. **The figure this line used to carry, 468, was wrong twice over — 468 is a count and not a rate, and it was not the count: the printed rule gives 481 at 17.64, and the 468 and the 463 that preceded it were one number counted twice inside a single pass and published as a delta. The raw count of 515 is right and did not move when the name went in.** The drafting-reference sentences in the narrator's voice in Chapter 252 and in three load-book frames stand. `state/phase-ledger.json` still reads `phase-000-bootstrap` and `planned` at Chapter 260, the reviewer agent still cannot be invoked as it is configured, and the five state files are still append-only and still growing by an order of magnitude depending on the pass — between 1.3 KB and 139 KB across the last ten commits that touched them, a median of about 12.5 KB, so the *about four kilobytes a batch* this line used to carry described the small repair passes and nothing else. All four are carried forward with owners named in the batch summary and none is fixed by hand.

# VOLUME 06 — MOVEMENT I (Chapters 251–260), "A Rule In Print" — OPEN, AND ALL OF IT IS UNPAID

**`outline/volume-06.md` governs. Movement I opened eighteen threads, paid none of them, and the standing problem list below the Volume 05 blocks is unchanged in wording. Anything marked SPENT in the archive may not be reopened, re-proved or re-argued.**

1. **A PRINTED RULE WITH A NUMBER AND A DATE THAT NOBODY HAS WITHDRAWN, AND FOUR HUNDRED COMPETENT PEOPLE WHO CAN INVOKE IT.** PS-3-11(c), dated eleven years, on a wall in a binder in a district office in District 3, requiring a practice card to carry a beat that performs the boundary before issue. **THE RULE ON WHERE THE NUMBER MAY APPEAR IS THE NARROWER ONE AND THE OLD WORDING OF THIS LINE WAS WRONG: the number is spoken aloud, once, by the clerk of forty-four in Chapter 256, because reading a number off a wall in about nine seconds is that chapter's event and the day map asks for it, and it also stands in the load books of Chapters 252 and 256. WHAT IS FORBIDDEN, and is the rule a later writer must hold, is that the NARRATOR carries it: it may not appear in a narrator's sentence, in a heading, or in any document inside the story that is not the district's own wall binder. No character identifies the name of the clause and no character identifies its author. It is not hidden, it is not withdrawn, and there is nobody to catch.** OPEN.
2. **NINE HUNDRED CARDS ACROSS ABOUT ELEVEN SITES WENT OUT OF USE IN ABOUT FOUR DAYS AND IT CHANGED NOTHING.** Correct, unpaid and unrepeated by anybody. The only continuing work is one day a week of pulling, done by a clerk of forty-four, in nobody's job, that nobody is paying for and that nobody has asked her to stop. **If she stops then in about four years the standard will still be on that wall and nobody will be pulling cards.** OPEN.
3. **A CARD WITH FIVE PARTS NAMED AND THE BOUNDARY BEAT MISSING, IN CIRCULATION ABOUT ELEVEN YEARS, IN FIVE PRINTINGS, TWO OF THEM WITH A DIFFERENT TYPEFACE ON THE BOUNDARY BOX, AND NO LINE ON THE REVISION SHEET THAT SAYS WITHDRAWN, AMENDED, REASONED OR REVIEWED.** The company is not the author and the card is not hidden. **Nobody has asked who changed the typeface, and the only fact anybody has is a column and a date.** OPEN.
4. **THE ENDING OF A PRACTICE IS ON NO CARD AND IS A SPOKEN WORD SAID BY A PERSON WHOSE JOB IT IS.** Nine company training rooms, a laminated sheet with nine words and no name, a man of fifty-one with a bad wrist who has said that a practitioner will go home and wait about four times and that the fourth will be in a stairwell on her own. **The withdrawal of the cards removed the paper and did not remove the wait, and about four people in a room in Crown Terrace understood that on the Friday of week ninety and did not do anything about it.** OPEN.
5. **ABOUT NINE PEOPLE ON NO ROTA ACROSS FOUR SITES, TWO OF WHOM HAVE NEVER BEEN ASKED WHETHER THEY WANT TO BE THERE.** One is a man of fifty-one with a bad wrist who attends on a Friday and is not on the list. The only way to reach them is to telephone about eleven people a week, and the person who would have to make that telephone call has said he has stopped asking and has not stopped noticing. OPEN.
6. **A HEARING SET FOR THE MONDAY OF WEEK NINETY-TWO IN A ROOM IN CROWN TERRACE, AND NOT HELD.** One morning, about nine chairs, an item asking whether a practice can be required to show that any person in it may stop at any time, and **Talia Venn, who is twenty-four, has refused to be the person who says the standard out loud, in public, in a room, in Chapter 259, and the refusal is on the page and is paid for.** OPEN, and it belongs to Movement II.
7. **THE TWO FAULTS IN THAT ITEM, AND THE ONE THAT CANNOT BE UNDONE.** The clerk of forty-four who invoked the standard is now seconded to the secretariat of the standing committee that is to hear the case and the chair has not been told. **A secondment is not a conspiracy, no person in this case can end it, and a hearing where the only available answer is no is a way of producing a finding.** OPEN.
8. **A DECISION A DISTRICT OFFICER MADE, ALONE, IN A CORRIDOR, AND DID NOT WRITE DOWN.** She is right, she is thanked by nobody, she has said once in nineteen years that she is right every time she opens her mouth and that this is the difficulty and not the qualification, and she has now said in about nine seconds that she will not say either thing a second time. OPEN.
9. **A REFUSAL IN WRITING, SIGNED BY THE ONE PERSON IN THAT ROOM WHO CAN BE NAMED TO A STRANGER, NOW IN A COMPANY IN ANOTHER DISTRICT.** About four sentences on somebody else's notepaper, read back to the room before it was sealed. **He cannot take it back because he wrote it, and the first line on that hardboard does not apply to it and he did not claim that it does. About nine people in a company now know that a man of twenty-two refused them in writing and nobody in that room thanked anybody.** OPEN.
10. **A COMPANY THAT WAS REFUSED, IS OWED NOTHING, IS NOT A VILLAIN, AND WAS TOLD IN THAT LETTER WHAT IT COULD USEFULLY DO.** Go and find the about nine people in four of its own sites who are not on anybody's rota. **It sends nothing further this term and it comes back in Movement IV with a product, and nothing in this batch may stage that.** OPEN.
11. **A WOMAN OF ABOUT FORTY-THREE WHO HAS TO WRITE A FILE NOTE ABOUT HER OWN VISIT.** A declined approach is a thing she can be found about inside her own employer, and she chose to write it herself rather than have it written about her by the person above her. She is owed nothing and is thanked by nobody. OPEN.
12. **A COOK OF TWENTY-SEVEN WHO HAS SAID THE SHAPE OF A THING FOUR TIMES AGAINST A PROMISE NOT TO SAY A FIFTH, AND DID NOT SAY A FIFTH.** She has asked about the heating three times and will not ask a fourth, she put the kettle and the gas ring on at about half past eight every morning for about fourteen weeks and told nobody either time, and she is standing in a cold room that refused money. **She is not a mechanism, holds no post, and the room does not depend on her being in it.** OPEN.
13. **THE HEATING, ON ITS FIFTY-NINTH DAY, UNPAID AND UNRESOLVED, AND NOT RESOLVABLE BY A DOCUMENT.** No fund, collection, subscription, grant, list, minute, notice or appeal exists and none is proposed. It comes back in Chapter 285 by a landlord and the notice arrives in Chapter 290. OPEN.
14. **A PAGE IN A RING BINDER THAT NINE PEOPLE HAVE NOW AGREED NOT TO READ, ADDED BY A PERSON WHO WAS NOT ASKED.** Folded once, half a sheet of typing paper, the corners gone soft. It is not read out, not summarised, not photographed and not made a thing, and **nobody in this volume is going to read it.** OPEN.
15. **TWO FIGURES OF ABOUT NINE HUNDRED IN ONE CASE, AND NOBODY WHO CAN TELL THEM APART IN UNDER ABOUT FOUR MINUTES.** About nine hundred cards and about nine hundred doors, four buildings in common, and a field service engineer of fifty-one who has wanted the fact written down for about four years and has never once wanted it written more than he wanted it not to be true. **Nobody wrote it down.** OPEN.
16. **ELEVEN BUILDINGS AND A MAN OF TWENTY-NINE WHO CANNOT PRICE THEM.** A page of his own in the ring binder, a refusal given in nine words, and a standing reason that a list of buildings is a list of people, which he has given and will not give again. **Nine of the eleven sites had a card pulled out of them and he has gone to none of them.** OPEN.
17. **A HEARING ROOM THAT GETS TWO MORE PEOPLE THROUGH THE DOOR WHEN NINE HUNDRED PEOPLE STOP BEING HANDED A PIECE OF PAPER, AND NOBODY KNOWS WHY.** Stated by a man of twenty-nine to the floor, and he will not ask them, and he said it would not be knowable for about four years. OPEN.
18. **THE RATE AT ELEVEN FOR THE SECOND TIME IN ABOUT A TERM AND A HALF, TOLD TO NOBODY.** He has now said the number out loud to an empty service road twice in six volumes and to no person twice, and the permanent sensitivity is unchanged and still told to nobody, and **it is said out loud once, to one person, in Movement IV, and nothing in this batch may rehearse that.** OPEN.



## The review repair pass on batch 0001: it paid none of the eighteen, and it opened two that are not about the book

**All eighteen threads above are still open, unchanged in wording, and that is the correct state and not an omission. The review of Chapters 251 to 260 returned ten findings and every one of them was a figure in a document, a delimiter in the markup, or a measurement rule that did not re-run. A repair pass that paid a story thread would have had to invent something to pay it with, and inventing a payment is how a thread gets closed by a document rather than by a scene. The corrected card series, the batch's bold figure and the now-printed matcher are at `state/continuity.md` under *The review repair pass on batch 0001*; the full finding list is section 6 of `workspace/volume-06/batch-0001/SUMMARY.md`.**

**TWO PROCESS THREADS OPENED BY THAT REVIEW. THEY ARE NOT STORY THREADS, NOBODY IN THE BOOK OWNS THEM, AND NO CHAPTER OWNS THEM EITHER, AND THEY ARE LISTED HERE ONLY SO THAT THE DEBT IS IN THE MANUSCRIPT AND NOT ONLY IN A LOG.**

- **P1. THE REVIEWER AGENT CANNOT BE INVOKED AS IT IS CONFIGURED, AND A REVIEW THEREFORE RUNS WITH WRITE PERMISSION.** `.opencode/agent/novel-reviewer.md` declares `mode: subagent`; the workflow calls `opencode run --agent novel-reviewer`; the platform reports the fallback to the default agent at the top of the review log, and the default agent is the writer, which holds `edit: allow` and `bash: allow` — the inverse of what the reviewer declares. **The review that produced the ten findings honoured its instruction not to edit files and left the tree clean, and that instruction is the only guard. The fix is three files — the agent definition's `mode`, the workflow's invocation, and the agent list — and all three are controller-owned and none is a fiction, bible, outline, chapter, summary, continuity, character or open-thread file, so no writing or review pass may apply it. OWNER: whoever owns the controller. THIS IS THE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL FINDING OF THE REVIEW.**
- **P2. `state/phase-ledger.json` HAS NEVER BEEN WRITTEN BY A PHASE.** It has two commits, both scaffold, and it still reads `phase-000-bootstrap` and `planned` at Chapter 260. **The manuscript has flagged the disagreement in `state/current.md` and in every batch summary in this volume rather than fixing it by hand, and that is still the right call. What is new is the count: the disagreement is nine phases old, so the ledger is not functioning as a completion tracker, and whoever owns the controller should decide whether it is repaired or removed.** A tracker reading `planned` at Chapter 260 is worse than no tracker.

A FIGURE THAT IS RE-RUN AND MOVES IS A FIGURE THAT WAS MEASURED, AND A FIGURE THAT IS RE-RUN AND DOES NOT MOVE IS A FIGURE THAT WAS COPIED, AND THE WHOLE OF THE MOVEMENT IN THE HEDGE FIGURE IS IN SENTENCES THAT WERE CORRECTED.**

**SIX PASSES RAN OVER THIS BATCH AND NOT ONE OF THEIR FINDINGS TOUCHED A SCENE, A BEAT, A DAY, A NAME, A CHARACTER OR AN ENTRY: THE FIRST FOUR FOUND FIGURES AND DOCUMENTS ONLY, THE FIFTH WAS THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS AND ITS THIRTEEN FINDINGS INCLUDED FIVE CANON ERRORS INSIDE THE TEN CHAPTERS, EVERY ONE OF THOSE A FIGURE OR A YEAR, AND THE SIXTH FOUND TWENTY-TWO FIGURES AND ONE CLAUSE IN NINE CHAPTERS. NOTHING WAS RESTARTED AND NOTHING WAS MOVED. A WRITER MUST NOT RE-DERIVE ANY OF THEM.** The card in a rail is 109 days old at Chapter 241 and 113, 116, 118, 119, 120, 123, 133, 141 and 146 at Chapters 242 to 250. **The room is 105 days old at 241, 109 at 242, 112 at 243, 114 at 244, 115 at 245, 116 at 246, 119 at 247, 129 at 248, 137 at 249 and 142 at 250, continued from Movement IV's eighty-one to a hundred and two, and the 151 this line used to give at 250 was nine days high, as the card's 155 was, because the span from the Monday of week eighty-three to the Wednesday of week eighty-eight is thirty-seven days and the batch prompt's endpoints assumed forty-six.** The twelve lines on the hardboard are twenty-five days old at 241 and sixty-two at 250; the thirteenth line was written on the Thursday of week eighty-six and is thirteen days old at 250. **The heating went off at seven in the morning on the Monday of week eighty-three, was not restored when the water went down on the Friday of week eighty-four, and is off at the close of the volume, thirty-seven days in.** The Exchange's last open sitting before this movement was eight weeks before Chapter 244 and four weeks before Chapter 250. **The condition is quoted in NONE of the ten chapters and paraphrased in NONE of them. The standing problem is NOT SAID in any of the ten and is referred to in ten load books. The words *carrier* and *pattern* appear in none, and no month-name, day-date or year appears in any of the ten. The name Talia Venn appears in Chapters 249 and 250 and in no other of the ten, and the woman of twenty-four of Chapters 245, 246, 249 and 250 is the same woman and the prose says so on the Friday of week eighty-seven, when she gives her name at the door.**

**And the fifteen threads this movement opened, and everything it did not close, are listed below, dated, and the volume close and Volume 06 inherit every one of them without amendment.**

1. **THE HEATING IS OFF AND UNPAID AND UNRESOLVED, THIRTY-SEVEN DAYS IN AT THE CLOSE OF THE VOLUME, AND NO DOCUMENT IN THIS CITY CAN RESOLVE IT AND NONE IS PROPOSED.** The supply went off at seven in the morning on the Monday of week eighty-three; a landlord's message of the Tuesday said it would be restored when the water went down; the water went down on the Friday of week eighty-four; nothing was restored. **A cook of twenty-seven asked three times and was told three times that the supply is restored when the water goes down and has said she is not asking a fourth time. A man of thirty-eight in a district jacket said there was a form for it and nobody in that room wanted a form. The heat is the cost of the refusal in Chapter 240 and the cost has now been paid for thirty-seven days and nobody has paid for it.** Three of the four people who came for the temperature did not attend again. **THE PERSON IT COSTS IS A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY WHO HAS ATTENDED ON EVERY WORKING DAY SINCE THE FRIDAY OF WEEK SIXTY-NINE, WHO HAS SAID TWO SENTENCES IN EIGHTEEN WEEKS AND BOTH WERE ABOUT HER OWN POSITION, WHO HAS NOT BEEN ASKED ANYTHING, TO WHOM NOBODY HAS APOLOGISED, AND AN APOLOGY WOULD HAVE BEEN A QUESTION.** No fund, collection, subscription, grant, list, minute, notice or appeal exists, none was proposed, and the man who said no to the money does not pay it and is owed nothing.
2. **NINE KEYS ARE NEEDED AND THERE ARE TWO, AND ONE OF THEM CANNOT BE ASKED BACK BY ANYBODY, AND THE OTHER CAN BE ASKED BACK IN ABOUT NINE SECONDS, AND THE ANSWER WAS GIVEN ALOUD IN A ROOM AND WAS DELIBERATELY NOT WRITTEN DOWN.** The count was said aloud in one sentence for the first time in the volume on the Friday of week eighty-seven, in front of the two people who own the other two, and he added unprompted that the other seven do not exist and that he has never met a person holding one. **The question on the back of the docket — who can be asked to take this key back — is about sixteen weeks old, was asked aloud in a room for the first time in this case, and was answered aloud: by her, in about nine seconds, without thinking about it, in her own coat, with nothing.** A first-year of nineteen asked whether any of it could be written down and was refused in about four seconds, on the ground that a written answer would be a rule about who may be asked, and that a rule about who may be asked is a rule about who is findable, and that there is no such rule in that room. **The binder holds seven things and did not gain an eighth. The first key is untouched and unreachable and this volume did not fix it and invented nothing.**
3. **THE STANDING QUESTION IS NOT STATED IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS, IS DECLINED ONCE IN A ROOM IN THE SHAPE OF A PRACTICAL ANSWER, AND CARRIES NO NUMBER, AND IT IS SHARPENED RATHER THAN ANSWERED.** The refusal to write down an answer about who may be asked is the first time in five volumes that the question has been raised in a room in a shape that could have been answered. **VOLUME 06'S FIRST PAGE IS THE QUESTION THE VOLUME OUTLINE NAMES: WHO CAN STILL INVOKE A RULE THAT IS IN PRINT IN A PUBLIC ROOM AND HAS NEVER BEEN WITHDRAWN, NOW THAT ABOUT NINE PEOPLE HAVE LEARNED TO ASK FOR THE BINDER NOBODY TOLD THEM ABOUT.** This batch sharpened it and did not answer it, and no message arrived from anybody, and the message the series sketch reserved for this volume was not delivered.
4. **ABOUT NINE PEOPLE HAVE LEARNED THE ADDRESS OF A RING BINDER IN FOURTEEN DAYS AND NOBODY TOLD THEM TO, AND THERE IS NO PAPER ANYWHERE IN THIS CITY THAT SAYS WHO MAY ASK FOR IT, AND THAT IS NOT AN OVERSIGHT.** A man of about thirty-one took it off a shelf and read it standing up for about nine minutes and put it back. Asked who says he is allowed to read it, the answer was: nobody, come and read it. Asked who is not allowed to know that he read it, the answer was that it is a different question, that nobody in that room has an answer, that about nine people have been asked it in the last fortnight, and that the absence of paper was decided in about nine days and nobody in the city agreed to it. **A ring binder is a person's book and is not a system, it cannot be emptied, and nobody who wrote in it can be found in it. What it holds is seven things in it, and the one way any of the seven is findable in a document is to be the person who wrote one of them and take that page out, which nobody will stop and nobody will be told.** **THIS SENTENCE IS REVERSED FROM WHAT CHAPTER 250 SAYS, TWICE, IN ITS PROSE AND IN ITS LOAD BOOK, AND AN EARLIER VERSION OF THIS LINE PRINTED THE REVERSAL — *seven of the seven people who wrote in it can be found in it, which is worse than being one of nine*. It was corrected here by the batch-0006 review repair pass, and `state/continuity.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` already carried the true version, so the error was in this file alone. The chapter is the authority and this line is the second time in this repository that a state file has reversed a sentence the prose settles.**
5. **A WOMAN OF THIRTY-ONE WITH A DIARY HAS BEEN ASKED TO BE CARRIED SOMEWHERE BY A STRANGER AND HAS NOT BEEN ASKED AGAIN, AND SHE HAS SAID THAT IF IT HAPPENS WITHOUT BEING ASKED THEN A BOOK IS A THING BEING MOVED, AND THAT SHE HAS WATCHED THAT HAPPEN ABOUT FOUR TIMES IN HER TIME IN THIS CITY WITHOUT NOTICING WHILE IT WAS HAPPENING.** There are about nine rooms it could be carried to and she has been to none. **He did not ask her and said why: because she made a statement in that room sixteen weeks ago that she has not repeated since and he will not be the reason anybody asks her again. She said that was the first correct thing he had said about it in a fortnight, that she was not going to do anything with it, and that if he tells anybody she will deny it and he will not be able to prove it, and that she has wanted to be able to do that for about a year and it has not come up.** Nobody in this case has asked whether the binder should travel and the question is not closed.
6. **THE COMMONS IS STILL VULNERABLE TO CAPTURE AND CANNOT YET CROSS A CITY LINE, AND BOTH WERE SAID OUT LOUD BY A WOMAN OF TWENTY-FOUR WHO IS NOT THE PROTAGONIST AND WHO HOLDS NO POST AND WAS NOT THANKED.** The capture will not be taken by a villain, because there is no villain in it: there is a company, a district and a woman with a clipboard and all three are right, and what takes a thing of this kind is a Tuesday, a tired person, and a version of it that is obviously better for about nine people. The city line is not stopped by a rule, because there is no rule in this city about a room, and not by money, because a room in a hall with a hatch in it has been doing it since the flood with a cook's numbers and a man's list of buildings. **What is stopping it is two keys where nine are needed, and a woman of twenty-four put that number in the room and not the man of twenty-two.** Two other rooms are now doing what that room does and neither is in a district the room can reach, and no arrangement between them exists.
7. **A FLOOD CAME INTO THREE DISTRICTS ON THE THURSDAY OF WEEK EIGHTY-FOUR AND FOUR PEOPLE EACH RAN ONE PIECE, AND THE EVACUATION STILL WORKED, AND THE FOUR PIECES ARE NOT RUNNING NOW AND NOBODY PROPOSED THAT THEY SHOULD.** About eleven people came out of about eleven buildings they could not have left on their own; about nine came down about nine stairs into a dry hall; about four hundred and eleven cups went out in two days and the room has no record of a single one of them and could not produce one. **Nobody died. Three of the four people are still doing their ordinary work, one of them has given the numbers of her piece to another room and kept the four hundred and eleven, and the other room will count its own, wrongly, in the correct order of wrong. The fourth is a woman of twenty-four who will not be on a telephone and has not been asked again.** A man of thirty-four who works nights accepted a night of door-opening work on a sheet of about nine addresses for the first time in about nine years and a cook of twenty-seven has offered him a standing Tuesday at about ten and refuses thanks for it.
8. **A PAGE OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE PEOPLE WAS IN A PERSON'S HAND AT THE MOMENT IT WAS USELESS, AND ONE TELEPHONE CALL WAS MADE, AND NOTHING CHANGED.** A woman of about thirty-four with a district jacket rang a man of fifty-one named Amiss, who has stood on that page since the Tuesday of week thirty-eight and is the one of that page that was never rung. He had left his flat at about seven on the Friday with a bag and a neighbour's dog, reached a sister's in another district, was not hurt, is fifty-one with a bad chest, and did not answer a door for two days. **She also established that she could have rung him on the Friday and did not, because the number in front of her was a man's list of buildings and not a page, and that by the Tuesday it was not useful. The page is the most useful object in this city and the one thing that cannot be used. There is no job in this city whose work it is to ring people, and about four rooms full of people this year could have held that job and none of them did.** No letter was written, no record was corrected or closed, and nothing was referred onward. **The thread is not closed by the call and the call is not evidence that the call would have mattered.**
9. **A MAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT WITH A BAD HIP CARRIED A BAG OF SHOES OUT OF THAT ROOM BY HAND, BECAUSE THE ROOM COULD RETURN IT AND COULD NOT TRANSFER IT.** Eleven times on the left and not on the right, cut by hand. He was offered no help and asked for none, and a man whose size it was and who had no shoes now has them, and the man of fifty-eight is not going to say where, and the room does not need to know. **This is the only object this manuscript has ever moved through that room and it is the only one that could not be handled in a room at all, and a first-year of nineteen said so first.**
10. **A WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-EIGHT TOOK A THUMB TO FOUR WORDS IN A SIGN-WRITER'S HAND AND PUT FOUR WORDS OF HER OWN IN CHALK, AND SAID OUT LOUD THAT SHE HAS BECOME A PERSON WHO CAN TAKE A SIGN DOWN.** A grey ghost of the first word remains and cannot be got off that board. **Her words are that anybody wet may come in, which are true about eleven days a year and false the other three hundred and fifty-four, and that a sign does not have to be true all year and has to be true on the day somebody is standing in the rain with nothing. In about four years somebody will stand in that doorway and ask her to put a different one up, and she will be the one who knows where the chalk is, and she said that out loud in a room with about nine people in it.** The sign-writer of about sixty, who was paid four pounds, has not been thanked, is not a character in that room and is not going to be, and is not named in any of the ten chapters.
11. **THE WALL IS THIRTEEN LINES AND THE THIRTEENTH IS AN OPT-OUT, AND IT WAS WRITTEN BY A FIRST-YEAR OF NINETEEN WITHOUT ASKING THE PERSON IT IS MOSTLY ABOUT, AND THE COST WAS SAID OUT LOUD BEFORE THE MARK WAS MADE.** Thirteen words: that a person may say they were never here and the room will not argue. **The first line of the wall is unchanged and is still a cook's and is still the first line, and the twelve lines above the new one are sixty-two days old at the close of the volume. The wall is a hardboard on two nails, it is not a form, a minute, a notice, a report, a committee paper, a leaflet, a registration or a constitution, it is not enforceable against anybody who did not write it, it can be rubbed off, and it is evidence of nothing in any proceeding.** A man of about fifty-two proposed minutes at the same meeting and was refused in about nine seconds and told in advance that no record would exist.
12. **THE EXCHANGE'S BOOK IS AT FORTY-SIX LINES AND IT OPENED FOR ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS AT ITS ELEVENTH SITTING, AND THE REASON IT OPENED IS THE SAME RULE THAT HAS KEPT IT SHUT, READ IN THE OTHER DIRECTION, AND THE REASON IT TOOK FOUR WEEKS LAST TIME WAS THAT ITS HOLDER ASKED A CALLER WHETHER SHE WAS SURE.** A book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call; the condition for writing anything in that book is that the caller said it to her face, which she has never once said aloud in nineteen years and said on the Wednesday of week eighty-eight. **Nobody was described, nobody was counted, and the number of words was refused and not asked for. The fortieth line is three years old. Line forty-four has a hole in it where a woman of sixty-eight in bay four should have been, and it is B. Nunn's name in the fourth column. The second line went in on a Monday in the week of seventy-one and has not been looked at since. The thirteen words on line forty-five are not quoted, paraphrased or counted anywhere in this batch or in the four before it.** The telephone count is forty-four, announced sixteen times in all, eleven of which correspond to the eleven Wednesdays this book holds and five of which predate it on a separate sheet she has never shown anybody; the highest four-week movement was four, at the tenth sitting; the book is two lines ahead of the telephone and she does not know what makes the gap and will not look in front of nine people. **She will not open it on a Friday. The ninth chair has been reported moved twice by a young man of twenty-one who keeps a count of how often she has said that a chair is a chair; she has never confirmed the count and has now told him not to stop; the chair is against the wall; its mover has not been in that room since she was fifteen, is not Asha Reed, may not be named, and has given no reason.**
13. **THE NINTH CHAIR, THE KEY QUESTION, THE PAGE OF FORTY-ONE PEOPLE, THE REASON A UNIT'S FIRST YEAR IS FREE, AND THE BINDER ARE FIVE THINGS NOBODY IN THIS CASE CAN CARRY, AND THE MOVEMENT NAMED ALL FIVE OUT LOUD IN FIVE DIFFERENT ROOMS.** A letting clerk of thirty-nine with a ring file has now said the sentence about a vacant frontage six times, has said *hold on* for the sixth time in about twelve weeks, has refused a man in her own building for the first time in nine years, has a sheet of her own paper with six calls on it that she will not bring to that room, and has volunteered that the landlord has not asked about that unit once since the water and that the reason the first year is free is in no file and never has been and that she will not be asked about it by anybody in this city. **The reason remains unrecorded in this case after a seventh refusal of the question.**
14. **AND THE STANDING FIGURES, WHICH A LATER WRITER QUOTES AND DOES NOT RE-DERIVE: about eleven people a week in this city are still going to be taught that a release waits for a man in a lanyard, and about four of the eleven are on no rota at all, and the number is on the back of a woman's own card and is on no form and is not a rate. The city's standing weekly figure is unchanged. The word was at nine and ten all through Movement V and did not reach eleven for the first time in about a term and a half, and it has still not been spoken in front of another person in six volumes.** The man of fifty-one with a tool bag said on the Friday of week eighty-three that in about four years somebody will put a number on that door that finds a person, and asked for it to be written down, and a cook of twenty-seven said one word and would not say which word. A man of fifty-one with a tool bag and a man of twenty-two are both on a company's list, and about eleven people a week are on it, and a first-year of nineteen said once in Movement IV that the eleven are the same eleven and the list is not the same list and that she cannot prove it and named the only authority she has as borrowed.
15. **AND, STILL OPEN AND UNCHANGED, FROM FOUR MOVEMENTS AND FOUR VOLUMES: the licensor, still unnamed and the field still blank; the six lines in the register, one of which has an author still in this city; the four hundred and one replies and the nine who got the company answer; the nine words wrong in a minute; the woman of thirty-four with a hip; the nine words a man of sixty-one was given; the second visitor column's five other words; the bit of pale card in a gate hut; the unsigned sheet and its four correct steps; a hundred and forty-one pounds under a till that cannot move; three photographs in a drawer in Nine Bridges; a company that can establish that room exists; a prospectus in another city that about four hundred people have read; a company pack with a photograph of a counter on page two; about nine hundred leaflets with a company's own method under a picture of a page; a shutter speed of about a fortieth of a second; a letter on a board in a clinic that has a girl's description in it and not her name; nine Fridays and about ninety pounds; the box in pencil in Bower Street with one name in it; and the master, in the fourth pocket of a tool roll, with its fifth line blank and nobody having ever asked him to fill it in.**

The two ceilings in the batch prompt are the bold ceilings and only the bold figure is measured against them; it is inside both. Full log in `workspace/volume-05/batch-0004/SUMMARY.md`.**

**A REPAIR PASS RAN ON THIS BATCH AFTER IT WAS WRITTEN AND ITS FINDINGS ARE IN THE SAME CATEGORY AS EVERY OTHER PASS IN THIS REPOSITORY: NOT ONE OF THIRTY WAS IN A SCENE, TWENTY-SIX WERE DATE INTERVALS IN PROSE OR IN DOCUMENTS, AND TWO CHECKS WERE ADDED THAT THE EXISTING CHECKS HAD PASSED WITHOUT COMPLAINT. A WRITER MUST NOT RE-DERIVE ANY OF THEM.** The card in a rail is eighty-five days old at Chapter 231 and a hundred and six at Chapter 240. **The room is eighty-one days old at 231, eighty-four at 232, eighty-six at 233, eighty-seven at 234, eighty-eight at 235, NINETY-ONE AT 236, WHICH IS A QUARTER OF A YEAR AND WHICH A FIRST-YEAR OF NINETEEN SAID OUT LOAD WHILE A COOK OF TWENTY-SEVEN CONFIRMED THE FIGURE AND DECLINED TO SAY WHAT IT MEANS, ninety-four at 237, ninety-eight at 238, a hundred and one at 239 and a hundred and two at 240**, and the series is continued from Movement I's eleven at 207 through Movement III's eighty at 230. The two newest lines on the hardboard are one day old at 231, four at 232, seven at 234, eleven at 236, fourteen at 237, eighteen at 238, twenty-one at 239 and twenty-two at 240, and **nothing was added to that wall and nothing was taken off it in this movement.** The company's page was left face down on a counter on the Monday of week eighty, turned face up on the Thursday, and is four days old at 235, eleven at 238, fourteen at 239 and fifteen at 240. The Exchange's last open sitting is exactly eight weeks before Chapter 233. **The condition is quoted in NONE of the ten chapters and paraphrased in NONE of them. The standing problem is NOT SAID in any of the ten and is referred to in ten load books. The words *carrier* and *pattern* appear in none, the name Talia Venn appears in none, and no month-name, day-date or year appears in any of the ten.**

**And the six threads this movement opened, and the seven it did not close, are listed below, dated, and a Movement V writer inherits every one of them without amendment.**

1. **A FORTNIGHT OF HEATING IN A ROOM OFF A SERVICE ROAD IS NOT PAID FOR BY ANYBODY IN THIS VOLUME AND IS NOT RESOLVED, AND THE PERSON IT COSTS IS A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY WHO HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED WHY SHE COMES.** This is the movement's outstanding debt and it is now a physical fact with a date. **The heating goes off on the Monday after the Friday of week eighty-two, which is the Monday of week eighty-three, and it is off for a fortnight, and no fund, collection, subscription, grant, list, minute, notice or appeal exists and none was proposed by anybody.** A cook of twenty-seven gave the figure and gave the reason, which is that the only route by which money is found for a room like this is a person who wants something, that every version of that has been refused for about fourteen weeks, and that the heat is what the refusing costs. **A first-year of nineteen said out loud on the Friday of the close that from the Monday the eight words over the door are going to be the only line in that room that is not true, and that nobody in that room is going to be able to fix it.** Nobody apologised to the woman and that was correct, and she is owed nothing by anybody, and she is on no object in that room and in no file in this case. **A Movement V writer must not resolve this with a document and should look before looking for anything else.**
2. **A SHEET OF KITCHEN PAPER WITH ELEVEN LINES IN SEVEN HANDS AND EIGHT NAMES IS IN A DRAWER IN A KITCHEN THAT OPENS IN THE AFTERNOON, AND ONE OF THE ELEVEN HAS A THUMB'S WORTH OF DAYLIGHT THROUGH IT.** Eleven lines, about nine of them argued about, one rubbed off with its own writer's thumb, one written last and signed separately. It is not a document, it is not a constitution, it is not on the wall and it is not in the ring binder, and a first-year of nineteen said in that room that anybody describing it as a set of rules in four years is describing nine people sitting in a room on a Monday afternoon. **Its eleventh line is that a woman of thirty-one may be telephoned about any of it and will come and reverse it, and that is the same eleven words she put on a sheet of typing paper about a key, and she is now findable about two things instead of one.**
3. **THE WALL IS TWELVE LINES IN SEVEN HANDS AND EIGHT NAMES AND THE FIRST LINE IS STILL THE FIRST LINE AND IS A COOK'S, AND A BOARD OVER THE DOOR NOW CARRIES EIGHT MORE WORDS ABOVE THE SIGN-WRITER'S FOUR IN A DIFFERENT HAND AND A DIFFERENT INK: *it is warmer in here than out there*.** The two boards are not one object and a first-year of nineteen said so out loud. **The top line of that board is not the top line that was written there first, and the sign-writer is not a character and is not thanked and is not referred to again in this movement, and the eight words can be rubbed off with a thumb like anything else on that wall.** No hand and no name was added to the hardboard and none was taken off it.
4. **THE SECOND KEY IS IN THE POCKET OF A WOMAN OF THIRTY-ONE WHO WROTE ELEVEN WORDS ON A SHEET OF TYPING PAPER, AND THE FIRST KEY CANNOT BE TAKEN BACK BY ANYBODY, AND CHAPTER 239 DID NOT FIX THAT AND INVENTED NOTHING.** The question on the back of the docket — *who can be asked to take this key back* — is about eleven weeks old, four people answered it, nobody wrote the answers down, and the answer about the key that is still in his pocket is still nobody. **A Movement V writer may not fix it, and the volume's own argument is that this is what a key is worth: a person who can be relieved of one thing and not of the other is a man who has to be the one who decides whether a room is a room, and he said that out loud in a room with about eleven people in it and nobody told him it was all right.**
5. **A COMPANY CAN NOW PROVE THAT ROOM EXISTS, AND ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE A WEEK IN THIS CITY ARE ON A LIST IN THAT COMPANY'S BUILDING, AND A MAN OF FIFTY-ONE WITH A TOOL BAG HAS SAID OUT LOAD FOR THE FIRST TIME THAT HIS OWN NAME IS ON IT AND THAT THE MAN WITH THE DOCKET IS ON IT TOO.** The list is a rota with a box that reads that no reason is given by the holder; the man of fifty-one entered his own name into it about four years ago and has never wanted to be questioned about it; the man of twenty-two is on it on an exception recorded and renewed for the term, processed four times by the same engineer without a reason being asked. **A first-year of nineteen said once that the eleven are the same eleven and the list is not the same list, said she cannot prove it, and named the only authority she has, which is a borrowed one.** Nobody in that room thanked anybody and a person of thirty-four who buys training refused to put that room on a board in a corridor in her own building.
6. **A MAN OF FORTY-ONE MADE AN OFFER OF MONEY FOR AN EXCLUSIVE TRAINING CHANNEL IN ABOUT NINE MINUTES, THE TERMS ARE GOOD, THE REASONING IS BETTER, HE WAS RIGHT THAT THERE IS NO MONEY IN THE ROOM, AND HE WAS REFUSED IN ABOUT FOUR MINUTES THREE DAYS LATER.** His name was given at the door and was Vey and nobody wrote it down, and he is not owed anything further and does not appear again. **A man of thirty-seven signed the paper he read out, came into the room the Monday beforehand to stand in it before moving any money, said the true thing about money first, collected the page on the Friday in his own coat without sitting down and without apology, and said he has been told he may not record why and will not, and that he has been given an instruction about his own handwriting and has obeyed it.** The line in that company's file reads that the offer was declined and nobody was permitted to write why, and the counter has carried nothing since the Friday of week eighty-two. **A person of thirty-four who buys training is on the record twice, was refused a board, and is the only figure in this movement who has spoken to a man in a lanyard in this movement, and she has not been in that room.**
7. **THE EXCHANGE'S BOOK IS AT FORTY-FIVE LINES AT ITS NINTH SITTING AND DID NOT OPEN, AND THE REASON IS ABOUT ONE PERSON AND NOT ABOUT A COUNT, AND NOBODY SIGNED ANYTHING IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS MOVEMENT.** Nine sittings, eight gaps of four weeks, the tally of sittings at which nothing has been added is eight and a half, the telephone count is thirty-seven, and it was announced unasked. **She has announced it fourteen times, nine of which correspond to the nine Wednesdays this book holds and the first five predate it, and she performed the subtraction aloud and refused to repeat it and said the figure is about her and has never been about the room and that the room is older than the book.** A woman who telephoned in the spring about something she had said, and telephoned again to ask whether it had been written down, cannot be answered without opening the book in front of nine people. A caller came, was not asked to sign, and said no, because nobody asked her, and was told she would have said yes, and agreed. **The rule was given whole and unimproved and appears once in this movement, and the caller's thirteen words on line forty-five are not quoted, paraphrased or counted anywhere in it.**
8. **A LETTING CLERK OF THIRTY-NINE WITH A RING FILE HAS NOW SAID THE WORD *VACANT* ABOUT A UNIT THAT HAS BEEN OPEN EVERY WORKING DAY FOR TWELVE WEEKS, TO A COMPANY, AND HAS ASKED FOR NO NAME, AND HAS SAID *HOLD ON* FOUR TIMES, AND HAS REFUSED A MAN IN HER OWN BUILDING FOR THE FIRST TIME IN NINE YEARS, AND IS NOT THANKED.** All four of those calls are on a sheet of paper of her own that she has said three times she will not bring to that room, and it was not asked for in this movement and she was not thanked in any of the four.
9. **A MAN OF THIRTY-FOUR WHO WORKS NIGHTS HAS SAID TWO SENTENCES TWICE AND IS NOT ASKING ANYBODY TO ACT, AND HE IS THE MOVEMENT'S MOST CAREFUL SPEAKER AND IS NOT A PRACTITIONER, IS NOT ON A ROTA, AND HAS NEVER ASKED TO BE TAUGHT ANYTHING.** The first is that at about four in the morning he is alone with a pump in a building feeding about nine hundred doors, that at some point in every shift he takes his hand off it and says a thing out loud, that in about nine years nothing has ever happened, and that he has told nobody. The second is that he would like somebody to watch him do it, has wanted that for about two terms and about four hundred times in a corridor, and that there is nobody to ask. **He distinguished the two himself, unprompted, saying that his is a wish to be watched and the sentence going round this city is a need for a person to be present, that one of the two is about him and the other about a stranger, and that he has never been in a position to tell the difference and neither has anybody in a lanyard. A cook of twenty-seven told him it was not nothing and he replied that it is not nothing and is not a yes, and that he has been able to tell the two apart since about the spring and had told nobody until that night.** He asked in Chapter 238 what the heat in that room costs to run and nobody could answer him.
10. **THE REASON THAT UNIT'S FIRST YEAR IS FREE IS IN NO FILE IN THIS CASE AND HAS NOW BEEN RECORDED AS UNRESOLVABLE SIX TIMES, AND A MAN OF THIRTY-SEVEN WHO SIGNS THE MONEY HAS ASKED TWICE AND BEEN REFUSED TWICE WITH THE SAME TRUE SENTENCE, AND IN CHAPTER 232 HE SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE WILL NOT BE THE PERSON ASKED AND THAT HE DOES NOT KNOW WHAT HE WOULD SAY.** Nobody in this movement went to the woman with the ring file and nobody has ever gone to her about a year.
11. **THE HEAT, THE MONEY AND THE ANSWER ARE THE SAME QUESTION, AND A FIRST-YEAR OF NINETEEN SAID IN CHAPTER 235 THAT A LINE ABOUT MONEY WRITTEN BY A PERSON FRIGHTENED ABOUT MONEY IS THE WORST THING THAT BOARD COULD CARRY, AND SPENT A MAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT'S ARGUMENT ABOUT A PAIR OF SHOES ON A COMPANY, AND ADDED A TWELFTH LINE TO NOTHING.** The wall is unchanged and the sheet is in a kitchen drawer and the two are not one object and a Movement V writer should not put a line about money on that board.
12. **AND THE STANDING FINDING, WHICH A MOVEMENT V WRITER SHOULD CARRY AS A PRESSURE AND NOT AS A TASK: money is a name, it is the only name on a sheet like that one and the only one that cannot be refused, and a number is findable where a leaflet, a photograph and a lease are not. A first-year of nineteen found that in about four minutes in Chapter 234 and a man of forty-one accepted it as a description rather than an objection and had no answer to it after eleven weeks, and no chapter of this movement settles it and no chapter should pretend to.**

---
