## What Chapters 221–230 added to the record, in the order it is established

1. **Ch 221, the Friday of week 75 — eight printed pages with a photograph of that room in them, and a first-year who said yes in four seconds.** Eleven jobs, eight in date, a bin store where a man of thirty-eight has told three callers in a month they were right, the word from nine to ten at about one, and nobody in that yard asking where a release would come from. **A district nurse of thirty-four with a district jacket and a clipboard, who put a trestle in a market about five weeks earlier and has not been thanked for that either, laid a certificate prospectus on the counter: about four hundred readers in another city, and on the inside cover a photograph taken from that room's doorway with the shutter up, the nine chairs, the window and the wall about four feet behind it, the hardboard with four words in a sign-writer's hand, a piece of sandpaper, the ring binder standing open with its pages above the sleeve, and about six unresolvable figures behind the counter.** Asha Reed said two things and then the third: **that she said yes on a Tuesday about three weeks earlier in about four seconds to a woman who had telephoned the number on the plate because she had taken one of about eleven hundred leaflets out of her bag in a market, and that six people were standing in that room when the picture was taken and she asked not one of them.** She named the cost of her own two-year sentence — that nobody in a room has to say anything to be described by the person who has the room — and said she has said it on a floor in week sixty-eight and at a market table in week seventy and has not been careful with it. A cook of twenty-seven said one word from a sink. **A man of twenty-two said the photograph is not a room described by somebody who was not in it, and was told in front of about nine people that it is a room described by a person who was in it and did not ask six other people, which is worse and is a different thing.**
2. **Ch 222, the Wednesday of week 76 — the eighth sitting, the book shut, and a print run found in a shop four feet wide.** Eight sittings, seven gaps of four weeks, the tin into a drawer with no lock on it, the rule given whole, and the telephone count moved from thirty-three to thirty-five and announced unasked; she has now said it thirteen times, eight of which are the eight Wednesdays this book holds, and she performed that subtraction aloud, refused to repeat it, has not rung the caller who is not coming, and has refused her reason a third time in the same words. **The book did not open, and the reason is new: at the seventh sitting it grew, and since then the question asked of her most often is when it will grow again, and she cannot answer it, and a woman who cannot answer the only question there is does not open a book on a table in front of about nine people.** Two items at four minutes each, neither written down; a man of twenty-two in a corner for two hours, not thanked. **Then four feet of shop: a printer of fifty-eight and a clerk of forty-four, neither in the room and neither thanked, and a page with a photograph on it, and about nine hundred leaflets off one counter in one morning carrying the same picture and the words *our method* beneath it — and the top sheet in that sleeve legible, and the hand on it the printer's own nine words.**
3. **Ch 223, the Thursday of week 76 — nine steps that are a woman's, a refusal, and a telephone call to a plate.** A man of fifty-eight with a hip that did not go right, a bus and one stop too far, and nine steps for getting out of the front seat of a car when one hip will not take weight, written by his sister of sixty-four on the back of an envelope in a car park about four years ago because he asked her to. **He said before anybody asked that the words are not his, that he did not learn them, and that the difference between performing a thing and holding it is the whole of that room.** A first-year of nineteen refused, without walking to the wall, because the only person who could take a page back is a woman in another district who has not been asked — **and said the offer was right and the refusal was right in the same breath.** He named two failures, the second on a pavement outside a shop in front of about nine people, and said the sequence therefore qualifies as a technique by that room's own definition, which he can still remember from the wall in its first week. He named the price: four years in a drawer, and a sister he has not spoken to in about nine years. **The room then did nothing for four days, which is the instrument it has; and at about seven that evening the sister telephoned the number on a plate with a company's name on it, a letting clerk of thirty-nine gave her the sentence about a vacant frontage, and the clerk said hold on.** The sister said the words herself, faster, with a different word for the count, adding two things he had not said: that the chair is not optional, and that where another person is helping the count is not taken by the person being helped. **The page went into the binder as the seventh thing; the first-year announced out loud that she was about to break the other room's rule, was told to go ahead, and gave her whole justification in about nine words — she said it to a person, and not to a tin — and added that she cannot prove the difference and has decided it.** He read it, found a number in it he has asked her for about two hundred times in nine years and never heard, and said she was wrong, and was told that that is also correct.
4. **Ch 224, the Monday of week 77 — a room that is open, and what the openness cost.** A man of thirty-one in the fourth week of a stoppage over the removal of a four-minute torch walk-round of the rear of a loaded vehicle, at a depot of about ninety vehicles and about four hundred loaders; not a supervisor, has never wanted to be, and said twice unprompted that it is not about money. **Nobody asked which side he was on, nobody asked whether the check should be reinstated, no line of the wall was read at him, and about four people watched a first-year of nineteen decide in front of everybody not to reach for the wall.** A woman of thirty-one with a key and a diary did the door, the sounder and the outside of the shutter with nine people present where she expects four, unaided, and the shutter was the same in the morning. He asked her one question; they talked for about two hours; she walked him to a stop that does not come to a service road. **At about four o'clock, at the sink, she said she was going to have to stop: one of her four employers draws about nine people a week from an agency, that depot is on the same agency, a woman with a clipboard asked her in a corridor about three weeks ago whether she was friendly with anybody on strike, she said no and it was true, and there is nothing in writing anywhere, which is the whole difficulty and the whole of why it works.** Nineteen, nine Fridays a week across four employers, about ninety pounds, about two hundred in the account. **A man of twenty-two said the correct thing about the room and was told it was not enough: the silence is right and is not a kindness, and being the one who has to be the friend is a job nobody there is paid for and she is the only person who cannot afford it.**
5. **Ch 225, the Thursday of week 78 — THE MIDPOINT. Four lines in a company's typeface that use no pronoun at all, and the two words *our method* set above them.** A pack of about forty pages, page two carrying the photograph, and under it: a working method, in a real room; the room asks a person what they do and records it in their own words; nothing is claimed, nothing is standardised, and the practitioner can withdraw at any time. **The printer of fifty-eight read them aloud twice from the counter, unasked, and then finished a sentence the man of twenty-two had started and could not finish, which is the finding: ten lines on a hardboard in six hands — the first a cook's about withdrawal, the second a first-year's about claims of improvement — printed in a company's voice under a company's possessive on page two of a pack with about nine hundred impressions of a leaflet off one counter in one morning.** The clerk of forty-four said the other half, including that she took the pack off an open shelf without leaving a note and chose in about four seconds to be the askable party. **A man of fifty-one with a tool bag, on the record about about nine hundred wrong numbers on about nine hundred doors and unable to change one, said a plate is a service item inside a building and a leaflet is not, and that recovery would need about nine hundred visits to a service road that four buses a day serve, and that he has not been thanked and is not going to be.** **A first-year of nineteen stood up and said she was not taking the leaflet down — no mechanism exists, and a public withdrawal would make her the only person anybody could consult about how she did it — and then said out loud that she agreed to the photograph in week seventy-two in about four seconds, that six people were in the room, that she asked none of them, and that she does not know whether the six in the picture are the six in that room; and was told that she does not, and that this is worse.** A nurse of twenty-four said that a person can agree to a photograph of a room and be in the photograph. **The room decided in about four minutes not to write to the company, because a letter from four signatories in a building with a company's number on the outside is a document about that room; nothing was sent, drafted or minuted.**
6. **Ch 226, the Friday of week 78 — a company asks the right question and the answer is that five of six cannot be found.** Eleven jobs, nine in date, and an emergency lighting pack that passed its test with cells two years out of date and was replaced anyway, which is now the reason that stair will never be examined again. **A call to a company's number on a plate with no name on it, answered by a letting clerk of thirty-nine who said hold on; about nine minutes in all, none of it anybody's fault.** A woman asked who was in the photograph and whether they had agreed to it, and a first-year of nineteen counted for four minutes and got six: a cook of twenty-seven, herself, a printer of fifty-eight, a man of twenty-nine on an employer's Friday list, a woman of thirty-one with a key and a diary, and a man of twenty-two. **The six figures in the picture are those six and the count closes, and five of the six cannot be located by anybody outside that room and the one who can be located is the man with the docket; the seventh person in that room, a woman of about thirty with a bag on her lap, is not in the picture and is the only one of the seven whose face is not in it, and nobody asked her and about four people wanted to.** The caller withdrew the pack from sale, from the next printing and from the course, and the room said why that was worth nothing before she could, and she said her own part and asked for it to be attributed to her and not to a room. **A woman of twenty-six who had taken a leaflet off a market table in week seventy asked whether what people say in that room stays in that room, and got nine words counted on the page — *That photograph is not of a room any more* — and he said out loud first that it is the third such answer and is not an improvement on the two before it.** The clerk of thirty-nine came back, said this is the third time in about two months the plate has been how somebody found that room, that she holds a sheet with all three calls on it, that it is hers and she is not bringing it here, was asked for it, and refused in one word.
7. **Ch 227, the Monday of week 79 — a piece of hardboard bought for two pounds, a corridor, eleven repetitions, and nine Fridays.** **A first-year of nineteen stood eleven feet from a desk in a ground-floor clinic corridor in Lattice Ward, where about nine people an hour hand something to a stranger, and said the first line of that room's wall eleven times, and wrote one line and not the other nine, giving the reason standing up: the other nine are about what a room will not do and this one is about what it will not keep, and a person about to hand something over at a desk needs the second kind.** Nine of about eleven stopped. **It went wrong twice and she corrected neither — once by saying it too fast, and once when a man of about sixty returned it with the sense inverted, and she refused to correct him because correcting a stranger creates a person holding a sentence in the wrong order with no means of finding out, and no form anywhere takes a sentence off a person. At about eleven a woman wrote to the clinic about a girl shouting in a corridor, the letter went on a board by the desk where that clinic displays anything anybody has written, a woman behind the desk read it out to about four people in a queue, nobody rang the first-year, and one of her four employers — a Friday at that clinic — was covered by somebody else at about eleven that morning. About ninety pounds out of an account of about two hundred.** A man of twenty-two said the thing would be on a wall within a month and photographed, and was told he was about eleven miles away on a docket; he then said she should have asked somebody first, and was told that there is nobody to ask and that this is the entire content of the wall the board was bought for. **And she named the sentence she did not invent: a nurse of twenty-four, whom she has never spoken to, said in a corridor outside a clinic a few months back that a clinic asks a person for nine things at a desk before it asks one question about any of it — and that both sentences are true, and that the nurse's is the one that would get a person arrested and hers is the one a person can act on at a desk.** The letter is not wrong and she put her there.
8. **Ch 228, the Tuesday of week 79 — a pair of shoes, and the first thing the room can hand straight back.** **A man of fifty-eight with the same bad hip came back nineteen days after he was refused, said he had not brought the envelope, and put a pair of shoes on the counter: resoled eleven times on the left and not on the right, cut by hand because a machine does not do that shape, wearable for about an hour and a half.** He said it was not a practice, not a method, not his sister's and not his; that a man who cuts soles by hand had asked him a question about the heel, the answer was his own and is on the back of a receipt; and that he would like to leave them where somebody could see them, the reason being that he wanted to be able to pick them up and put them down again without asking anybody. **Told they can stay until the shutter comes down and then he takes them home, which is neither a favour nor a rule and is the only thing in that building that has ever worked.** Then the wall, line by line: a thing liftable in about nine seconds satisfies the first line and cannot satisfy a binder or a page, and one page has been struck through once and cannot be restored; a shoe admits of no copy; a shoe has never been printed; the tenth line was not invoked. **A cook of twenty-seven said a kettle, a sheet of cloth and a pair of shoes are none of them a technique, and a record of any of them would in about four years put a card in front of somebody saying they had failed, and in this case the card would be about a man's feet — but an object may stay on a counter where it is visible and be taken away at the end of a day, and that is a different class of event from a page; and she refused to say it twice.** He read his sister's page, found a number in it he has asked her for about two hundred times in nine years and never heard, and established that it exists because a woman telephoned a company's number and was written down by somebody who has never met her, and said she was wrong. **At the door he said four words to a man of twenty-two who had said nothing all afternoon: that this was the first time in eleven weeks that somebody had brought him something he could hand back in a minute. He was told the shoes were handable because of what he had done and not because of the room.**
9. **Ch 229, the Wednesday of week 79 — who held the camera, and why the hand is moving.** Four feet of shop in the Quiet Quarter, about four hundred leaflets left out of nine hundred, a quarterly return on a card of the shop's own, and a hundred and forty-one pounds under a till in an envelope with no name on it that cannot be sent back and has not moved since the spring. **A person of forty-one with a first floor in Nine Bridges and a van, a customer of that counter for about four years, said they had known since the Wednesday and did not pretend otherwise, and then said the thing nobody in that shop had understood: a binder open on a counter with a sheet proud of the sleeve reads in a still exposure as a certificate, and at about a fortieth of a second the six people in it went, and a hand going is what reads as a room, and the exposure was set deliberately in about nine seconds to stop the picture looking like a document; the page did not go, because that sheet stands in a sleeve and nobody was holding it, and a person does not hold still for a fortieth of a second while a page nobody is holding is exactly as still — so the legibility of six people's handwriting is the half of the craft decision that was not noticed. Their summary, said once, without heat: no conspiracy and no fault is involved and the whole of the accident is a shutter speed.** Four exposures in about eleven minutes; a brief reading *a working method, in a real room*, *with people in it if possible*, *not posed*; one paid for and three not. **A person of thirty-four who buys training, who wrote the brief, said she has never once asked to see a person in a photograph, gave the reason — the room is somebody else's, they would not identify their own occupants, and the exchange would have run about nine weeks and she had a course to write — and said the part she could not improve: a young woman answered a telephone in about four seconds and said yes, she wrote that down in her own book as a yes, and three colleagues that week used the same word about a supplier.** **The printer of fifty-eight said she has been wrong about printed matter four times in forty years and right once, and the once is her own handwriting nine inches tall on the top of page two, and that she is not a mechanism, has never asked to be on that wall, and that the name a reader arrives at is a shop with a telephone number on the outside of it, and no number on the pack is hers.** And the person of forty-one said they will not destroy the three uncommissioned photographs in a drawer, because a person paid for a job and the job was done, and because in about four years that drawer would be the only evidence in this city that anybody stood in that room who is not in the pack, and there is no mechanism for handing three photographs back.
10. **Ch 230, the Thursday of week 79 — the close: twelve lines in seven hands, seven things, and a corridor.** About eleven people, four standing; a first-year of nineteen stating for the third time that the room is busier and smaller at once. **A man of twenty-two stood in the floor rather than at the back for the first time in eleven weeks, unremarked and observed by about nine people.** The wall went to **twelve lines in seven hands and eight names**. **The eleventh line was written first, by the man of fifty-eight, standing up, and read back to the room before he let anybody know he wanted it there: that it will not keep a thing whose words are somebody else's — eleven words, the only thing he has learned in nineteen days, and he did not have it on the afternoon the room refused his sister's sequence. Asha Reed said out loud that he is the second man of fifty-eight to write in that room and not the first, that the first was the Monday of week seventy and refused to write anything, and that she thinks about him about twice a week.** The twelfth line was hers: that it will tell you what it will not hold before you hand it over. **She said the room had begun four days earlier to do this in a corridor in about ninety minutes with a board bought for about two pounds, had it go wrong twice, once in her mouth and once in a man's, and fixed neither, lost nine Fridays and about ninety pounds, and would do it again; that doing it again is worse than being told; and that both versions are on the table.** A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts asked what the corridor is for and was told it is for the people who are not here, and that it cannot be done every day, or on a Friday, or indoors. **A nurse of twenty-four gave the finding once: saying it badly is the only shape available, because doing it well would produce a question about how, and there is no how, and the how is a person, and the person is findable; and that she has never been in a photograph, that this is not modesty and not a rule, and that it is the only arrangement in that building she has been able to rely on.** The binder came off the shelf, was opened on the counter and not on the floor, and has **seven things**; the first-year read the number and the hands and the contents of none, as she has done once before, and said **six of the seven carry his name, that he is the only one of the seven anybody can go and find, and that he and she are both going to do nothing about it**, and that a man of fifty-one with a tool bag has said so to her privately. **A woman of thirty-one with a key and a diary found the sounder loose where a trolley had struck it, put two screws into it in about four minutes, and it rang and nothing happened, because a sound at a man on a step does not make anybody come.** And a woman of about thirty was in the third chair for the whole evening and is not in the photograph and has never been asked why she comes, and about four people in that room wanted to and did not.

## What Movement III of Volume 05 did not do, and what it leaves for Movement IV

**It did not teach, demonstrate, assess or assist anything; nobody was shown anything; and the condition is quoted in none of the ten chapters and paraphrased in none of them. It did not stage the relay, ask the woman who carried it, or name her. It put no second panel and no `* * *` marker in the volume. It did not put a name over the door, and it put no title, office, post, committee, minute, notice, report, form, registration or constitution on anybody, and the only documents this movement produced are a company's pack, about nine hundred leaflets, a prospectus, a piece of hardboard in a corridor, a letter on a clinic board, and two new lines on a wall that is not screwed to anything. It did not open the Exchange book anywhere but Chapter 222, and the book is at forty-five lines and did not open at its eighth sitting, and nobody signed anything in any chapter but the woman of sixty-four on a telephone, which is not a signature. It did not resolve the standing problem, invented no rule about who may be asked, and attached no number to one. It did not get to nine keys, and two exist. It did not empty the box in pencil and did not touch it. It did not let the master leave the fourth pocket of the tool roll and nobody decided anything about its fifth line. It did not name the licensor, name the person who inserted the page, name the clause behind the public version, put Iona or Sorn in any file, or let any message arrive from anybody. It did not put Rafi Pell in a room, name Steve Akers, find the woman of thirty-four with a hip, or say Asha Reed's retired sentence. It did not give Oren Vey a line, and it did not let the man of twenty-two be the person who saw the photograph. It did not settle anything: a company withdrew a print run and a course, a room decided in four minutes not to write a letter, a company answered a correct question correctly, a strike went on, a sister telephoned a plate, and none of it changed a thing anybody can put back. And it did not resolve the relationship: there is no scene, no declaration and no kiss, the two of them are in the same building in one chapter and do not speak, and he is addressed in Chapter 224 by a woman of nineteen and not by anybody else.**

## What Movement III closed, and what it closed *against*

1. **A ROOM CAN CONSENT TO A PICTURE OF A ROOM AND THE PEOPLE IN IT CANNOT, AND THE PERSON WHO CONSENTED IS THE ONE WHO HAD THE ROOM.** Closed against any reading in which the movement's villain is a person. **The chain is four strangers: a woman took a leaflet out of her bag in a market, a clerk answered a company's number on a plate, a first-year of nineteen said yes in about four seconds, a photographer came for about eleven minutes, and a company printed a page. Nobody in it planned anything and nobody in it is a villain, and six people were standing in a room when the shutter went because a room agreed to a picture of a table.**
2. **A PHOTOGRAPH OF A BINDER IS NOT A COPY OF THE BINDER, AND THE ONLY THING IN IT A READER CAN RECOGNISE IS A HAND.** Closed against any reading in which a photograph is a neutral fact. **A sheet proud of a sleeve reads in a still exposure as a certificate; at about a fortieth of a second the six people moved, and that movement is what reads as a room, and it was set deliberately to stop the document reading, and the sheet in the sleeve did not move because nobody was holding it. The whole of how six people's handwriting became printable nine inches tall is one shutter speed, and the reason is on the page in the mouth of a person of forty-one who is not a villain.**
3. **THE ONLY THING A ROOM HAS EVER BEEN ABLE TO HOLD IS A PAIR OF SHOES, BECAUSE A THING WITH NO WORDS ON IT CAN BE PICKED UP AND PUT DOWN.** Closed against any reading in which the room's difficulty is a shortage of things. **Every other offering in eleven weeks has been a person or a person's account of a thing they do, and every difficulty the room has had has been about whose words those are, and a cook of twenty-seven said that a kettle, a sheet of cloth and a pair of shoes are none of them a technique and that an object may stay on a counter and be taken away at the end of a day, and that this is a different class of event from a page.**
4. **THE STANDING PROBLEM IS UNDER ITS HEAVIEST PRESSURE IN THIS VOLUME AND IS NOT SAID.** Closed against any reading in which a photograph gives an occasion for saying it. **A photograph makes a room findable without anybody in it agreeing, the man the photograph is of is the man who cannot be asked about the room, and a member of the public asked him about the room in Chapter 226 and his answer was nine words on the page that do not answer her, and he said out loud, first, that it is the third such answer and is not an improvement on either of the first two.**

## What Movement III did NOT close, and the four questions it opened

- **THE NINE HUNDRED ARE IN ABOUT NINE HUNDRED POCKETS AND THERE IS NO MECHANISM TO GET THEM OUT, AND THE ONLY PERSON WHO CAN PROVE THAT IS A PRINTER OF FIFTY-EIGHT WHO HAS BEEN WRONG FOUR TIMES IN FORTY YEARS.** A company withdrew the pack, the prospectus page and the run, correctly, in about nine seconds, on a Friday, at its own initiative, and a field service engineer of fifty-one said in the room that a plate is a service item inside a building and a leaflet is not, and that recovery would need about nine hundred visits to a service road that four buses a day serve. **A Movement IV writer may not find a way to get them back, because there is not one, and the temptation will be strong, because a company offering to buy them back would be an obvious scene.**
- **A FIRST-YEAR OF NINETEEN LOST NINE FRIDAYS AND ABOUT NINETY POUNDS FOR SAYING ONE SENTENCE ELEVEN TIMES IN A CORRIDOR, AND THE THING THAT COST HER WAS A PIECE OF PAPER WITH HER DESCRIPTION ON IT AND NOT HER NAME, ON A BOARD IN A PUBLIC CORRIDOR.** A piece of paper in a public place with a person's description on it is the same mechanism that put about nine hundred leaflets into about nine hundred pockets, and nobody in this case has yet asked whether a room can be found by a person describing it. **This is the sharpest instrument Movement IV inherits and it has never been used.**
- **THERE IS A DRAWER IN NINE BRIDGES WITH THREE UNCOMMISSIONED PHOTOGRAPHS IN IT THAT CANNOT BE HANDED BACK, AND THE PERSON WHO PUT THEM THERE WILL NOT DESTROY THEM, AND THEY ARE THE ONLY EVIDENCE IN THIS CITY THAT ANYBODY STOOD IN THAT ROOM WHO IS NOT IN THE PACK.** Nobody has asked them for anything and nothing has been demanded of them, and a Movement IV writer who wants a thing that cannot be taken back has one already on the page and should not build a second.
- **AND THE WOMAN WHO HAS BEEN WAITING SINCE THE SPRING IS STILL NOT FOUND, STILL NOT NAMED, AND WAS NOT MENTIONED IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** The name a woman of about fifty-two refused twice in a market has not been refused a third time and has not been given. **She is the one thing in this case that a Movement IV writer could most easily make into a mystery and must not.**

## What Movement IV inherits, dated, with everything reset

- **Volume 04 is CLOSED at Chapter 200 and its run ended at entry 203. Volume 05 opened at Chapter 201 with entry 204; Movement I is 204 to 213, Movement II is 214 to 223, Movement III is 224 to 233. Movement IV is Chapters 231 to 240, weeks 79 to 82, and `workspace/volume-05/batch-0004/` DOES NOT EXIST and must be created by the next prompt. **The constraint the Movement IV day map has to satisfy is that the Exchange falls on the Wednesday of week eighty, four weeks after the Wednesday of week seventy-six, and that the book is at forty-five lines and Bea Nunn will not open it on a Friday, and that the volume's end figure of forty-six is Chapter 250's problem and is not Movement IV's. THIS BATCH SPENT TEN DAYS, ABOUT NINE HUNDRED LEAFLETS AND A COMPANY'S OWN PACK AND DID NOT MOVE THAT NUMBER BY ONE, which is the standing cost of the Exchange and is the point of it.**
- **THE COMPANY IS WITHDRAWN AND IS NOT DEFEATED, AND THE PERSON WHO WITHDREW IT MAY NOT APPEAR IN MOVEMENT IV.** The pack is off sale, off the course and out of the next printing, at the company's own initiative, in about nine seconds, and about nine hundred leaflets and an unknown number of packs are in about nine hundred pockets and in drawers. **Oren Vey returns in Movement IV with an offer and no second fact, and the temptation in a movement whose midpoint was a photograph will be to give him a line about the photograph, and he may not have one: the photograph, the pack, the nine hundred and the four lines are now a matter of record inside this case and he is owed nothing further. A man of fifty-one with a tool bag, who is on the record about about nine hundred wrong numbers on about nine hundred doors in four buildings and cannot change one of them, said the same about leaflets and worse, and is available to Movement IV, and is not a mechanism and is not a door into the company.**
- **THE ROOM CAN NOW TELL A STRANGER WHAT IT WILL NOT HOLD BEFORE THEY HAND ANYTHING OVER, AND THE WALL HAS A LINE THAT SAYS SO, AND NEITHER HAS BEEN TESTED BY ANYBODY WHO DISAGREED.** The instrument is a piece of hardboard, a corridor, a first-year of nineteen and about ninety pounds. The obvious Movement IV question is not what happens when the room is right and costs somebody a friend, because Chapter 224 answered that; **it is what happens when the room is right and it costs somebody a job, which is the Friday that was covered by somebody else at about eleven in the morning.**
- **THE BINDER HAS SEVEN THINGS AND HE IS ON SIX OF THEM, AND THE SEVENTH IS THE ONLY PAGE NOBODY IN THAT ROOM CAN TAKE BACK.** The first-year of nineteen said in the room on the last day of this movement that six of the seven carry his name, that he is the only one of the seven anybody can find, and that he and she are both going to do nothing about it, and a man of fifty-one with a tool bag has said the same to her privately. **A Movement IV writer may open the records, including the failures, as the plan says, and this batch's finding is that the failures are already on the page: a first-year said yes in four seconds, a room broke another room's rule on purpose, a corridor was tried and a letter was written, and none of the three was a villain's act.**
- **OPEN, UNPAID, AND NOT TO BE CLOSED BY A DOCUMENT:** the licensor, still blank; the six lines in the register, one of which has an author still in this city; the four hundred and one replies and the nine who got the company answer; the nine words wrong in a minute; the woman of thirty-four with a hip; the nine words a man of sixty-one was given; the woman who has been waiting since the spring; the second visitor column's five other words; the bit of pale card in a gate hut; the unsigned sheet and its four correct steps; and a hundred and forty-one pounds under a till. **NEW FROM MOVEMENT III:** a prospectus about four hundred people have read; a company pack with a photograph of a counter on page two; about nine hundred leaflets with a company's own method under a picture of a page; a shutter speed of about a fortieth of a second; three uncommissioned photographs in a drawer that cannot be handed back; a sheet of paper in a clerk's office with three telephone calls on it that she will not bring; a piece of hardboard in a corridor with one line on it; a letter on a board in a clinic with a girl's description in it and not her name; nine Fridays and about ninety pounds; a pair of shoes; a man of fifty-eight's envelope in a drawer in another district; and a woman of sixty-four's two sentences that are in a binder in her brother's room and are about his own body and are not his words.

## What Movement II closed, and what it closed *against*

1. **A KEY IS NOT A POST BECAUSE NOBODY CAN PRODUCE THE LIST, AND THE TEST OF THAT HAS NOW BEEN RUN AND IT FAILS FOR THE FIRST KEY.** Closed against: any reading in which a second key settles the question. **One question on the back of a docket — who can be asked to take this key back — was answered four times in about nine seconds, and three of the four answers were a person and the fourth is nobody, and a key nobody can take back is a key whose holder can never be relieved, so the first key is the one that makes him a holder.** Nobody invented a holder to fix it, no lock was fitted, nothing was posted, and the first key did not go back in an envelope. There are two keys and nine are needed.
2. **THE ROOM'S RULE HAS BEEN OBEYED TWICE UNDER PRESSURE, AND NEITHER TIME BY A VILLAIN.** Closed against: any reading in which the wall is an instrument, and against any reading in which it is not one. **In Chapter 216 the first line was read aloud before anything was taken and a man of forty-nine said at the door that he would have been within the law for four years and that he did not argue with a wall because he had watched a stranger take a page out of a binder. In Chapter 219 the first line is what makes a removal possible and the ninth line was enforced in that chapter by nobody at all.** The wall has no force, is not enforceable, can be rubbed off, and is not evidence of anything, and a first-year of nineteen said all of that out loud in the room.
3. **A PERSON CAN BE TAKEN OFF A PAGE IN THAT ROOM, ON PURPOSE, BY ASKING, AND IT WORKS, AND IT CANNOT BE UNDONE.** Closed against: any reading in which the removal is a decision by the man of twenty-two. **The instrument is the person who asked. His hands are the hands because there is exactly one person in that room anybody in this city can telephone and a first-year of nineteen said that out loud as an admission and not as a choice. Nobody stopped it, nobody was asked to, the reason was about the record and not about the person, and the only record of the asking is the stroke, so rubbing it out would destroy the only evidence that a person asked.**
4. **HE IS ON FIVE OF THE SIX PAGES AND IS THE ONLY ONE OF THE SIX ANYBODY CAN FIND, AND THAT IS WORSE THAN BEING ONE OF NINE.** Closed against: any reading in which the binder is a recognition, and against any reading in which the woman who says it becomes the room's voice. **A woman of about thirty said it once, to him and not to the room, and nobody wrote it down, it is not on the wall, and nobody asked her a question about why she was there. A man with one of nine keys is a man with a door; a man on five of six pages is a document; and there is no version of this that is not a trade.**
5. **A PAGE IN A PERSON'S OWN WORDS CAN BE RIGHT AND WRONG IN THE SAME FOUR MINUTES, AND IT IS NEVER BECAUSE THE PERSON LIED.** Closed against: any reading in which the room's method is accuracy, and against any reading in which it is the first-year's fault. **It is because they answered the question they were asked and the question had a use in it, and a person in a room is doing a thing and a person in a sentence has got a reason attached, and the reason cannot be taken off and the thing kept. A rule about which question gets asked is a rule about which people get asked, and it was refused before the sentence was finished.**
6. **A CUP OF TEA AND A CHAIR IS NOT A TECHNIQUE AND THE PERSON WHO MADE IT SAID SO OUT LOUD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE.** Closed against: any reading in which the movement has found a technique that works. **A technique in that room is a thing whose wrongness hurts a person; a person who has not done a kettle right is not a person who has failed; the binder stayed shut and the page is empty for the second time in a month for the second different reason.**

## What Movement II did NOT close, and the four questions it opened

- **THE BINDER IS FINDABLE AND THE PEOPLE WHOSE WORDS ARE ON IT ARE THE PAYERS.** Five of the six pages carry his name, one of them is a first-year's, one is a printer's, one is a lift engineer's and one is a woman of thirty-one's, and every one of the other five has said out loud, in this volume or in an earlier one, why they will not give theirs. **The mechanism that put a name on five of six is a question, and the question is asked by the only person in this city who can be asked to answer for the room, and nobody designed it. Movement III is where it is proved against the room's own heroes.**
- **THE PAGES ARE IN SOMEBODY'S HANDS AND A HAND CAN WALK OUT.** A page went into an inside pocket on the Thursday of week 71 and a page went into an inside pocket on the Thursday of week 72, and the two men who did it are not the same man and neither knows the other did it, and neither is a villain, and one of them still does the thing every working day in about four hundred houses and one of them will see about four hundred people this week. **Nobody in this city can produce the working copy of a page that a person wrote and then did not keep, and that is the same sentence as the one about the binder nobody told them about, and it is now about a room.**
- **THERE IS A MAN IN THIS CITY WHO HAS BEEN SAYING *THAT IS ALL RIGHT, WE ALL SIGN THEM* TO HIMSELF FOR FIVE NIGHTS BECAUSE A WOMAN AT WORK SAID IT ONCE, AND HE CAME TO A DOOR FOUR MILES FROM A WALL THAT SAYS THE ROOM WILL NOT PUT A PROMISE ON A WALL AND COULD NOT GET AN ANSWER.** Nobody wrote it down and it is a debt and not a mystery. He said that the man not coming over was the first useful thing anybody has done for him in about a month, and that is exactly what it was.
- **AND THE WOMAN WHO HAS BEEN WAITING SINCE THE SPRING IS STILL NOT FOUND, STILL NOT NAMED, AND STILL NOT WRITTEN DOWN.** She was not mentioned in any of these ten chapters and the name she refused twice in a market has not been refused a third time. **She is the one thing in this case that a Movement III writer could most easily make into a mystery and must not.**

## What Movement III inherits, dated, with everything reset

- **Volume 04 is CLOSED at Chapter 200 and its run ended at entry 203. Volume 05 opened at Chapter 201 with entry 204, Movement I is entries 204 to 213 and Movement II is entries 214 to 223. Movement III is Chapters 221 to 230, and its day map IS FIXED, in `workspace/volume-05/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, on ten days inside the outline's weeks 75 to 78 territory. **The constraint the day map has to satisfy is that the Exchange falls on the Wednesday of week 76, four weeks after the Wednesday of week 72, and that Bea Nunn will not open the book on a Friday. **Chapter 225 is the volume's midpoint reversal and it is fixed in the outline: a company's own training pack, its own photograph, and the words *our method* on about nine hundred leaflets, and the person who exposes it is a printer of fifty-eight and a clerk of forty-four and not the protagonist.
- **The Exchange is at forty-five lines, seven sittings, six gaps, six and a half shut, and the telephone count is thirty-three.** The only thing that has ever grown that book is a caller who came and signed in her own words, and the price was said out loud: a book that grows is a book she can no longer refuse, because anybody can now ask her when it will grow again. **The caller is not described in any file, has not been named anywhere, was asked no question about herself, and a line in that book reads *she said it in a car* and is the worst line in it. BEA NUNN SAYS SHE HAS SAID THE TELEPHONE NUMBER OUT LOUD IN THAT ROOM EVERY FOUR WEEKS AND TWELVE TIMES, THE SITTINGS ARE ON THE WEDNESDAYS OF WEEKS 48, 52, 56, 60, 64, 68 AND 72, SEVEN OF THOSE TWELVE ARE IN THE BOOK, AND SHE RECONCILED THAT HERSELF AND UNASKED IN CHAPTER 217 — seven of those are in this book, the first five are not and are not going to be. THE TWELVE IS A FIGURE ABOUT HER AND NOT A SITTING COUNT, THE ROOM EXISTED BEFORE THE BOOK DID, AND A MOVEMENT III WRITER MUST NOT READ IT AS ONE.**
- **The room: a year, nil rent for the first year, seven ordinary clauses and a ninth with no nine(b), four names on the lease, and about ten months left on it.** The freehold is an entry on a schedule of title and the person who owns the entry was never met. The plate outside carries a telephone number and no name. **Two keys exist and nine are needed; the first cannot be taken back by anybody and the second came from a person on a page in her own hand with a reason attached.**
- **The wall is ten lines in six hands and seven names and it is not screwed to anything.** The first line is still the first. The tenth is a cook's and it is four words and a conditional. **The list on the wall and the board above it are two objects with two owners and are not to be put in one sentence.**
- **The ring binder has six things in it: eleven items in a cook's hand with the eleventh struck through in a single stroke and not erased, four words in a first-year's hand, one sheet in four hands, and one page each from a printer of fifty-eight, a man of twenty-nine who works on lifts and a woman of thirty-one with a key and a diary.** A seventh thing was offered and refused in Chapter 216, a sixth went in and came out in Chapter 214, and a page went into a man's pocket in Chapter 218, and none of the three left a mark on the binder. **The card in a rail is forty-two days old at the end of Movement II, with his name on it and a week at the bottom left and nothing on the back. There is a master in an inside pocket on a chair and an original in the fourth pocket of the tool roll with the fifth line blank, and none of these objects may appear together in a single sentence, which is mechanically checked in every load book in this batch.**
- **The standing problem has been said eight times, the last of them in a market on the Wednesday of week 70, and it is said an eighth and last time in this volume. It was NOT said in any of the ten chapters of Movement II and is referred to in five load books.** He looked at a rule instead of a woman's face in Chapter 209 and nobody wrote down what she said the second time. **In Chapter 215 a member of the public asked him about the room and he gave nine words that are true and do not answer her, and he said out loud, before anybody else spoke, that it is not an improvement on the nine words he said in a market nine days before.**
- **The panel budget for the whole of Volume 05 is one and it has been spent, in Chapter 209. There is to be no second, and no `* * *` marker may appear in Chapter 221 or after.**
- **Series milestone 3 is spent and milestone 4 was paid in Chapter 195 of Volume 04 and is not re-paid. What Volume 05 is running on is the series' own version of it: she supports the room and challenges his certainty when it becomes a policy, and the three beats of Movement II are a sentence on a page, a rule about which question gets asked, and one word spoken to somebody else.** The words *we*, *our* and *us* are permitted in this volume in a person's mouth about a public thing and not in a sentence about the two of them, and the word *pattern* appears nowhere.
- **Open, unpaid, and not to be closed by a document:** the licensor, still blank; the six lines in the register, one of which has an author still in this city; the second visitor column's five other words; the bit of pale card in a gate hut; the unsigned sheet and its four correct steps; the four hundred and one replies and the nine who got the company answer; the nine words wrong in a minute; the fifth room and who takes the name out; the hundred and forty-one pounds under a till, and the printer of fifty-eight has *it will not be printed* on a wall; a name in a box in pencil that cannot come out; the woman of thirty-four with a hip; the nine words a man of sixty-one was given; the woman who has been waiting since the spring; **and about nine thousand job sheets in about nine hundred vans, on about eleven thousand pieces of paper, that carry four words in a box with a name under them, and which the man who wrote the four words has now told four people in a room in the Lower Wards are not what he does.**
- **NEW AND OPEN, FROM MOVEMENT II, AND NONE OF IT IS SETTLED BY A DOCUMENT: two keys and the reason the first one cannot be taken back; a sheet of typing paper with one reason in a woman's own hand in a folder on a shelf; a question written on the back of a docket that four people answered and nobody wrote down; a man who can hear a person not pretending, three seconds, and a page that was in his own words and was not what he said; a man whose nine minutes of a job are on eleven thousand sheets in about nine hundred vans with his name in a box; a man whose four hot plates are on nine canteen walls and cannot be taken back; a woman who folds a sheet and has taught about four hundred people and was frightened for every one of them; a page left empty twice in a month for two different reasons; a caller's **thirteen** words and a name in a fourth column and a book that can no longer be refused; a name struck through in a single stroke and not erased, and the smudge that is the only other record; a tenth line on a wall in six hands; and a man of thirty-eight who signed something at work and has not slept and could not be answered at a door.**

# VOLUME 05 — MOVEMENT I (Chapters 201–210), "What A Room Is For", and what MOVEMENT II INHERITS, dated

**Volume 05 is OPEN and this block is the live state at the end of Chapter 210. It supersedes nothing in the `VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT V` block below it and it inherits every line of it, and every line of the `workspace/volume-04/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md` hand-off, subject to the four re-bases in `outline/volume-05.md`. Load-book entries 204 to 213, continuous, one per chapter, and the run does not end here.**

**REPAIRED IN THE SECOND REVIEW PASS, AND THREE THINGS IN IT WERE WRONG AND ARE NOW RIGHT.** **ASHA REED IS THE FIRST-YEAR OF NINETEEN and there is no second nineteen-year-old in this case** — the batch used both names for one woman in fifteen chapters and never printed it, and Chapter 202 now names her where she first appears, so every thread below that says *a first-year of nineteen* means her. **The wall has nine lines in six hands, seven names and eight people in the room**, of whom one was not asked to sign. **The card in a rail is twenty-one days old at the end of Movement I and not eight days or about six weeks.** Full log in `workspace/volume-05/batch-0001/SUMMARY.md`. **A THIRD AND THEN A FOURTH REPAIR PASS RAN AFTER THAT ONE, AND THE FOURTH CORRECTED TWO INTERVALS AND ONE OBJECT WITHOUT TOUCHING A THREAD: the room has been open fourteen days at Chapter 208 and not eleven, which is Chapter 207's figure carried forward one chapter and which had been printed in this file's own header as well as in two others; Chapter 209's prose and its entry said *about eleven days* where the room was sixteen days old, and both now say about two weeks; and the sign-writer's letters are four inches high and not four letters high, which was Chapter 206's own prose and was copied into three state files and one load book, none of them this one.**

## What Movement I closed, and what it closed *against*

1. **A ROOM EXISTS AND IT IS THE FIRST THING IN THIS CASE THAT HAS EVER BEEN BUILT INSTEAD OF FOUND.** Closed against: any reading in which the room is a set, a symbol, a proof of anything, or a thing four people agree about. **Four rooms and a back one, nine chairs, a counter, a ring binder with nothing in it, a kettle, two plug sockets of which one works, a bell-push that is not connected to anything, a window onto a wall four feet away, and a back door that wanted a millimetre off the top edge and nothing else. Every one of those is an object with an owner and none of them is a metaphor.**
2. **NOBODY WROTE ANYTHING DOWN, AND THE REASON WAS GIVEN OUT LOUD, AND IT WAS A COST.** Closed against: any reading in which the room is documented, minuted, registered, catalogued or minuted of. **A letting clerk of thirty-nine stopped a pen with a sentence about a page being able to be carried to somebody who was not in the room, and he put it away in about four seconds, and the counter-argument was made by him in one sentence and she agreed with it.** The only writing in that room in ten chapters is nine lines on a piece of hardboard, a page in a ring binder with two things in it, and a first-year's private list of four words she will let anybody take off the wall.
3. **THE FOURTH CONDITION IS IN FORCE IN THAT ROOM AND WAS SAID WHOLE, ONCE, BY THE MAN IT IS ON, IN CHAPTER 203.** Closed against: any reading in which the room is a technicality, an exception, a doorway, or a place where he could have taught, demonstrated, assessed or assisted anything. **He taught, demonstrated, assessed and assisted nobody in any of the ten chapters, and the sentence he said was: he cannot run the practice he wants the room for, and somebody else must, and somebody else must be a person who can be asked whether they are competent and who can lose something when the answer is no.** No name, no title, no post and no deputyship was invented to fill the gap.
4. **THERE IS NO NAME OVER THE DOOR, AND THE ARGUMENT AT BUILDING SCALE WAS HAD IN CHAPTER 206 WITH TWO OF THREE AGAINST HIM.** Closed against: any reading in which the disagreement was resolved, or in which the person who said it was the strongest person in the room. **He lost.** A cook of twenty-seven refused on the ground that a kitchen of hers was found twice in nine years by a man with a clipboard and both times there was a name on the door and both times it was the owner's. A nurse of twenty-four refused on the ground that a room that cannot be found is a room for people who already know — having said the reverse on the Thursday six days earlier, so the two of them now have one position each. A first-year of nineteen agreed with him in one sentence and then said one sentence too many about people ringing him. **The one who agrees with him is the one proved right in Movement III and the batch says so in the entry.**
5. **A KEY IS A KEY AND NOT A POST, AND THE REASON HAS NOT BEEN TESTED.** Closed against: any reading in which the key settles the question of who the room belongs to. **There is one key and one holder and it is not a set, it came from a company with a ring file, and a company is not a person and cannot be asked to take anything back.** Nine are needed and there is one. This is the volume's spine and it is not solved in this movement.
6. **THE ROOM'S RULE ABOUT WHAT IT WILL NOT HOLD IS THE COOK'S AND IT IS THE FIRST LINE ON THE WALL.** Closed against: any reading in which the list is his, or is a constitution, or is enforceable, or is evidence of anything in any proceeding. **Six hands, nine lines, eleven proposed, and a person who had been in the room for six hours across three days was not asked to sign because she only came in to sit down, and the reason was given out loud in the room and given back by her.**
7. **HE SIGNED IT AND HE IS THE ONLY NAME ON IT ANYBODY CAN FIND, AND HE SAID OUT LOUD WHY.** Closed against: any reading in which this is a recognition, an honour, an appointment or a return of the rail. **It is the bill for a card in a rail, arriving in the Lower Wards instead of a corridor, and he did not choose which, and about eleven people a week are still going to be taught to wait for him and a name on a wall by a door is not a rota and nobody in that room said otherwise.**
8. **THE STANDING PROBLEM WAS SAID AN EIGHTH TIME, IN A MARKET, IN FRONT OF ABOUT FORTY PEOPLE, AND IT IS NOT RESOLVED.** Closed against: any reading in which the eighth saying is a step towards a rule about who may be asked, and against any reading in which a woman in a market is given an answer. **His answer was nine words and they did not answer her. He looked at the rule instead of at her face. The one panel in Volume 05 so far is in that chapter and it is right and it answered nothing.** No rule was invented and no number is attached to it anywhere else in the volume.
9. **A BUS INSPECTOR'S FOUR STEPS WERE REFUSED IN NINE WORDS AND A PAGE WITH THREE QUARTERS OF A THING ON IT IS THE LEAFLET.** Closed against: any reading in which the refusal was about him, and against any reading in which three of the four steps went into a book. **They did not. The person who refused it put a knife down, said it once, did not improve it, and did not explain it, and the man agreed in about four seconds and said he was not going to argue with a cook about a door.**

## What Movement I did NOT close, and the four questions it opened

- **THE ROOM HAS A FINDABILITY PROBLEM AND IT HAS NOT BEEN TOUCHED.** A description in public is a description of the people in it; a name at the bottom of a leaflet is worse and cannot be taken off; a room that cannot be found is a room for people who already know; and he believes the third of those and has been argued out of it by two other people and has not changed his mind. **That argument is not resolved in this volume and Movement III is where the room's own method is proved against the room's own heroes.**
- **THE KEY SYSTEM IS ONE KEY DEEP AND THE FINDING IS A ROOM THAT NEEDS A MAN TO STOP IT IS A ROOM WITH A KEY-HOLDER IN IT.** Nobody has tested whether a holder can be removed by a person who can be asked to do it. **The mechanism exists in this batch and it is nine seconds and a person with another key, and it has not happened yet, and the last entry of the volume says so.**
- **THE PHONE IS A COMPETENT IDEA HELD BY SOMEBODY WHO HAS NOT USED IT.** Two people rang a woman of sixty-one in four weeks and neither came, and one of the two wants the room to be findable, and neither of them has said out loud that a findable room and a findable person are the same sentence. **Movement II's first day is the first time that has to be said by somebody with a leaflet.**
- **THERE IS A WOMAN IN THIS CITY WHO HAS BEEN WAITING SINCE THE SPRING BECAUSE NOBODY TOLD HER A MAN COULD REFUSE TO COME, AND HER NAME WAS REFUSED IN A MARKET BY THE PERSON WHO HAD IT.** Nobody wrote it down. **It is the sharpest thing in the batch that is not resolved and it is not a mystery anybody is going to spend anything on; it is a debt.**

Chapter 210 does not touch the relationship at all.** The words *we*, *our* and *us* are permitted in this volume in a person's mouth about a public thing and not in a sentence about the two of them, and the word *pattern* appears nowhere.

# VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT V (Chapters 191–200), "The Ledger", and what VOLUME 05 INHERITS, dated

**Volume 04 is CLOSED at Chapter 200 and the load-book run ends at entry 203. This block is the state at the end of the fifth and last movement. It supersedes nothing in the `VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT IV` block below it and it inherits every line of it. The dated hand-off is `workspace/volume-04/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md`, which supersedes this block for anything about what Volume 05 inherits.**

## What Movement V closed, and what it closed *against*

1. **THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX IS DELIVERED AND IT IS A CORRECT ACTION.** Closed against: any reading in which the purge was a raid, a vendetta, a defeat, a turn or a triumph; in which Iven Sore is caught, confronted, converted or removed; in which the ledger is read by him; in which the sheet is carried by him; and in which a form, a minute, an order in a minute, a committee, a notice, a report or a leaflet is the instrument. **Eleven weeks of work on a list, a strip of tape across a corner, and a line in a disposal book in a hut, with a job title in an authority column — the mechanism was spent correctly and publicly in Chapter 191, five days' worth of ordinary work before the room was even found, and it runs again on the Tuesday of week sixty-six and does not notice the one sheet that mattered.**
2. **THE VAULT APPEARED ONCE, IN CHAPTER 195, AND IT IS A MAINTENANCE CHAMBER.** Closed against: any reading in which it is a tower, a vault with a door, a hidden room, a set-piece, or a place a person is let into. **Eleven feet by nine, a low ceiling, a steel stair, a painted grey cabinet with a keyhole that has no key in it, a hatch with nothing to lock, and a drawer in a wall.**
3. **HE WAS ON THE FLOOR AND HE DID NOT READ IT FIRST, AND TWO PEOPLE HE DID NOT CHOOSE READ IT.** Closed against: any reading in which he reads it, translates it, summarises it, recognises the hand, or identifies anybody. **Sera Quill and Halla Rennick read it between them. The fifth column has a word at the top of it that means who gave the word and it is blank in every line, and about nine of the forty lines on a folded run sheet are the same eleven words in the same order, and the authorship is not identified and the name is not in this volume.**
4. **HE ASKED HER TO CARRY THE PAGE AND SHE REFUSED, AND THE REFUSAL IS ABOUT THE RECORD.** Closed against: any reading in which the refusal is about the two of them, in which it is a scene, a declaration, a kiss, or a reconciliation, and in which either of them says *we*, *our* or *us*. **Her nine words are countable: *I will not carry what I cannot answer for.* She picked up her bag on the stair and went to a counter that was opening at eleven. Series milestone 4 is PAID and is not re-paid.**
5. **THE CARRIER MOTIF BELONGED TO A MECHANIC AND A RECORDS CLERK, AND ONE OF THEM STOPPED HALFWAY, AND IT WAS SAID ONCE.** Closed against: any reading in which the relay is staged, in which the carrier is him, in which Marta Selby is asked again, named, thanked, or told she was right, or in which the carrying is a favour, a request, or a rescue. **Five words — *because the last one did* — said once, not argued with, nobody asked her who, and the field service engineer said nothing about it then or afterwards. The bag was not theirs, both of them knew by the second landing that they were not going to ask whose it was, and nobody thanked anybody.**
6. **THE CONTINUITY OFFICE AGREED TO NOTHING AND REFUSED NOTHING, AND EMERGENCY AUTHORITY WAS RETAINED AND STATED AS RETAINED BY A PERSON, OUT LOUD, IN A CORRIDOR, IN THE FIRST PERSON.** Closed against: any reading in which he is defeated, turned, exposed as the author, or made to hand anything over. **The one new sentence in Chapter 199 is one with a count in it, and the new act is that he does not ask for the page and has known where it is since the Wednesday.**
7. **THE CITY BEGAN TO INVESTIGATE THE UNAUTHORISED AMENDMENT AND IT IS NINE SLIPS IN A DRAWER.** Closed against: any reading in which a committee is formed, a minute is taken, a notice is issued, a report is written, a leaflet is printed, a question is answered, or the nine are adopted, distributed, minuted or noticed. **Thirty-one slips on the Friday of week fifty-nine; fifty-eight on the Thursday of week sixty-six; nine of the fifty-eight have a person's name at the bottom of them; the first arrived about a fortnight before; none has been answered.**
8. **THE CHOICE IS SPENT AND IT IS THE VOLUME'S SECOND AND LAST.** A card in a rail in a reading room in Lattice Ward, in his own hand, on purpose, renewable, removable by a clerk, no title, no office, no second name, nothing on the back. **The reason was said out loud in a room with five people in it, once, and the reason does not help. The price is that he is findable in a building with a plate on the outside of it. A rack is not a rota and nobody in that room said otherwise.**

## What the review repair pass on batch 0005 changed about these threads, in five lines

1. **NOTHING ON THIS LIST IS CLOSED BY THE REPAIR AND NOTHING ON IT IS OPENED BY IT.** The repair found six defects in the batch — a paragraph Chapter 200 had copied from Chapter 196 at 319 characters, three more paragraph pairs at 66 to 84 characters, Iven Sore's coat on the wrong man in Chapter 197, the Exchange's gaps miscounted in Chapter 192's prose, a sentence that contradicted itself about Sera Quill's name, and three stale figures in the batch's own summary. **None of the six is a thread and none of the six closed one.**
2. **THE EXCHANGE THREAD IS THE ONE THAT MOVED, BY THREE WORDS.** **The run is five sittings — the Wednesdays of weeks forty-eight, fifty-two, fifty-six, sixty and sixty-four — three Wednesdays in between them and four gaps, four weeks apart, and the next sitting is the Wednesday of week sixty-eight.** The number was never in doubt; the sentence around it in Chapter 192 was, and it is now right in the prose and in the load book.
3. **THE AUTHORSHIP THREAD IS UNCHANGED AND IS NOW PHRASED SO IT CANNOT BE MISREAD.** **The name not said in Chapter 195 is the name on the page. Sera Quill is named in the chapter and always has been, and the woman who recognised the hand is not the woman the hand belongs to.** A later writer quoting this thread must not turn it into Sera Quill's name being withheld.
4. **THE HAND-OFF THREAD.** `workspace/volume-04/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md` is an account and not a dispatchable prompt, the runner cannot see it, and the hand-off rests on `state/current.md` naming it. **A Volume 05 writer arriving through the runner's own generic continuation prompt must read that file before writing anything, and this is the first time in this repository that the next phase cannot be handed the volume close by the dispatcher alone.**
5. **A METHOD THREAD FOR EVERY LATER BATCH.** **A backwards-only overlap sweep will report a clean batch over a dirty one. Run a within-batch pass as well, pair every file with every other file, and re-measure every figure after the last edit rather than before it.** This repository has now published a stale word total, a stale per-chapter series and a false compliance claim in three consecutive batches, and the cause is the same in all three.
