- **Open, unpaid, and not to be closed by a document:** the licensor, still blank, behind a postbox in Brin that nobody will open for a stranger; the six lines in the register, of which one has an author still in this city and the man upstairs will not say which; the second visitor column's five other words; the bit of pale card in a gate hut; the unsigned sheet and its four correct steps; the woman of thirty-nine and her page of her own; the four hundred and one replies and the nine who got the company answer; the nine words wrong in a minute; the fifth room and who takes the name out; the unlabelled bay, the list of named holders, the boy at the control post, Delia Frost's landing, Nkechi Osei's Mondays; the hundred and forty-one pounds under a till.
- **NEW AND OPEN, FROM MOVEMENT V, AND NONE OF IT IS SETTLED BY A DOCUMENT:** **a partial maintenance ledger in five columns whose fifth column is blank in every line, of which one folded run sheet is on a high shelf in a room off a street in Lattice Ward that anybody may walk into, held by nobody, in a folder, with no card and no slip on it; about thirty-one other lines of it are in a maintenance chamber under a building with no lock on it, and the chamber is opened by anybody with a torch and a reason, and the next round is eleven weeks from the Tuesday of week sixty-six; nine lines out of forty on the one sheet that survive are the same eleven words in the same order and nobody has identified a hand; nine request slips with names on them in a tray in a room off a street and none of the nine has been answered; the reasoning that a person cannot ask for a binder nobody has told them about, which is the strongest instrument this volume has built and is inert; the requirement that every technique must show its exit, in print, in a public room, never withdrawn, with fourteen years of quiet non-compliance behind it and no authority in this city that can repeal it; a canvas bag on a hook behind a door in a plant room belonging to a man who left in the spring whose locker nobody has cleared; a foreman of fifty-eight who has asked whether he is allowed to keep a diary and has been told the answer is in a document behind a counter; a woman of twenty-nine who has sixteen Fridays on the back of a card and has still not written down what they are for; a care worker of thirty-eight who has asked whether a person can find out who has read a page and was told no; a printer of fifty-eight who has asked whether a person can print something and then not be the person who decides about it and was told that nobody knows; a woman of forty-six with fourteen lines in pencil on the back of a condition sheet who will not be asked to give a man's name; a woman of twenty-six with eleven letters, nine answers written, none sent and two left; a man of twenty-one with three practices in three hands who was in a room on the last day of the volume and was not asked anything; a woman of thirty-four with a diary and nine lines in it who has still not torn them out; a man of fifty-eight who authorises correct things in writing and retains an authority out loud in a corridor and has never been asked whether he is it; and a man of twenty-two who is findable.**
- **THREE THINGS A VOLUME 05 WRITER INHERITS AS SETTLED: that a person in this city can be removed from a share, a name and a room by the people who put them there, and that the volume's resolution is that same operation turned on its own hero, and it is spent; that a rule is only as real as the copy people open, and the requirement has never been withdrawn and the illegality is fourteen years of quiet non-compliance; and that a correct action that changes nothing is cheaper and more common than a wrong one and this volume has nine of them on the page.**

# VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT IV (Chapters 181–190), "What People Are Owed", and what Movement V inherits, dated

**Volume 04 is open at Chapter 190. This block is the state at the end of Movement IV. It supersedes nothing in the `VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT III` block below it and it inherits every line of it. The load-book run is 184 to 193. Batch 0005 is Chapters 191–200, Movement V, *The Ledger*, whose ten days fall in weeks 64 to 67, and its first load-book entry is 194.**

## What Movement IV closed, and what it closed *against*

1. **THE MOVEMENT'S EVENT IS DELIVERED: A PERSON IN THIS CITY CAN BE REMOVED FROM A SHARE, A NAME AND A ROOM, ONE AT A TIME, BY THE PEOPLE WHO PUT THEM THERE.** Closed against: any reading in which a district, a company or an office does any of the three, in which the removal is a ban, an insult or a sanction, in which anybody is thanked for a removal, or in which the three are the same operation. **A share can only be ended by a person in it; a room can only be closed by the person holding its key; a name in pencil on a page in a stack of about two hundred can be removed by nobody at all, including the woman it is for.** A share and a room can be put back by the same person who took them out. A name cannot, and the reason is structural and not moral: an object with no system behind it has no record in it that could be amended. **Nobody stopped any of the three and nobody was asked to, and that is not a failure of nerve: stopping any of them would have meant becoming the person who does it, and there is no such person in this city and there is not going to be one.**
2. **HE WAS ASKED TO LEAVE A ROOM AND HE LEFT IT AND THE ROOM DID NOT STOP, AND FOUR PEOPLE IN A CORRIDOR NOTICED THAT HE WENT.** Closed against: any reading in which the request was a ban, in which she asked him to leave, in which he argued, in which he was thanked for going, in which the corridor's four said anything to him about it, or in which the room stopped. **She asked him to be somewhere else for eleven minutes and said out loud that she wanted to know which of the two she had done before he went through the door, and the reason was findability in a working form: with a man in the room the answer to her one question is given in a voice people use on a man they have been told about.** Five of the eleven were asked: four yes, one no, six not asked, and the no was not followed by a reason.
3. **THE BOX IN PENCIL IS DEALT WITH AND IT CANNOT COME OUT, AND A SECOND NAME WAS NOT PUT IN IT.** Closed against: any reading in which the box is emptied, corrected, opened, adopted, given a reference number, a system, a post or a district, and against any reading in which a second name goes in by anybody but the person it is for. **Nobody took it from her. She gave it, another person wrote it, a clerk ruled the box, and all three did the right thing. The count is one name in about two hundred pages.** The reason no second name went in is a reason and not a mood, and it is the movement's sharpest: the only mechanism that put the first name there was a person ringing a counter, the moment the woman of thirty-one asks a second person she is a person who asks people for names, and in about four years she is a woman at a counter with a diary and a list who does that, **and that is not a post, it is a habit of hers, and a habit of hers is the most dangerous thing in this case because she is the only one holding it.** What she was given instead is true and is not a correction, and she said so unprompted and is keeping it. **There is exactly one box in this manuscript and nobody has ever been asked to empty it.**
4. **A SHARE ENDED BY ITS OWNER, CARRIED OUT OF A BUILDING BY SOMEBODY ELSE'S HANDS, AND NEITHER WAS THANKED.** Closed against: any reading in which the ending is the work of a form, in which a district was involved, in which a carrier was thanked or volunteered, or in which the ending solved anything. **There is no form, because a form wants a reason and he has not got a reason. Forty words in biro on the back of a work card, carried out in a lift engineer's tool bag through a fire door, and the warning that ending a share does not un-teach four people that a release waits for a man is on the card.** A lift engineer of twenty-nine said yes because nobody asked her to and said that if he thanked her she would have to think about a favour in about four years and she would rather it was a lift.
5. **A ROOM WAS CLOSED BY THE PERSON WHO HOLDS THE KEY AND THE ONLY INSTRUMENT ANYBODY HAD WAS A DIARY.** Closed against: any reading in which a company, a district, a landlord or a form closed it, in which the diary became a register, in which the clerk at the counter became an instrument, or in which anybody was thanked. **She gave the reason before it was asked for and the half of it that costs her is that after the week he can ask and she will say yes, and that version is the one where she is a woman with a key, and in about four years a woman with a key is a post, and there is no post in this city whose job it is to decide who goes in a room.** The clerk refused to be told and said why. **The only two people who know are the two of them.**
6. **THE SECOND OF THE TWO PUBLIC METHOD DISAGREEMENTS IS SPENT, IN CHAPTER 184, AND IT IS THE LAST OF THE TWO IN THIS VOLUME.** Closed against: any reading in which the outline's *twice* is quietly satisfied by Chapter 172, in which Chapter 172's corridor is restaged, or in which either of the two is wrong. **A registrar of forty-one against an officer of a fund, in a room with a counter in it, in front of four other people, about method.** The words *we*, *our* and *us* appear nowhere in any of the ten chapters of the movement. **There is no third one available in this volume and a later writer must not manufacture one.**
7. **THE STANDING PROBLEM WAS NOT SAID IN THIS MOVEMENT, AND NO RULE ABOUT WHO MAY BE ASKED WAS INVENTED.** Closed against: any reading in which it is said an eighth time, and against any reading in which a number is attached to it in Chapters 181 to 190. **He sat in the fourth row of the tenth session for fifty minutes and said nothing, because he has said it twice in that room, it did not get better the second time, and he is not going to say it a third time in a better sentence because there is not one.** A second condition, that he can say what he heard in a corridor and cannot hear a corridor, is on no sheet and was said in Chapter 177 and is not said again here.
8. **THE EXCHANGE'S BOOK IS AT FORTY-FOUR LINES AND HAS NOW NOT BEEN OPENED FOR FOUR SITTINGS, AND THE NUMBER OF PEOPLE WHO HAVE BEEN TOLD YES BY TELEPHONE IS TWENTY-NINE.** **The four are the Wednesdays of weeks forty-eight, fifty-two, fifty-six and sixty, counting the sittings in the shut run and not the intervals since week forty-eight, and the Wednesday of week sixty-four is the fifth. A writer who carries *four* forward to the week-64 sitting has not incremented it.** Closed against: any reading in which a caller signs, in which the book grows, or in which the reason is a mood. **The rule was given in one go and not shortened: a book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call.** A man of sixty-ish came to that room, was not a caller, said so in the first nine seconds, and was given four minutes. **The next Exchange is the Wednesday of week sixty-four and it is the last one in this volume.**
9. **EVERYBODY IN THE MOVEMENT PAID SOMETHING AND NONE OF THEM WAS PAID BACK.** Closed against: any reading in which a paying is settled, forgiven, refunded or noticed by the person paying it. **A boy of nineteen's handwriting is on about four hundred pieces of paper and a no of his is now written down; a man of thirty-four's nine words are on the back of a leaflet in a bin; a woman of twenty-six has five answers written and none sent and nine more to write; a printer of fifty-eight is still the person who decides what a card is for and has a hundred and forty-one pounds on a coat hook in an envelope she has not opened.** A man of twenty-two stood behind a hatch and heard all five and wrote down five numbers and no names.
10. **A MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-ONE ASKED FOR A MICROPHONE TO BE SWITCHED OFF AND WAS NOT ANSWED, AND NOBODY TOLD HIM THAT ASKING IS THE PART THAT LASTS.** Closed against: any reading in which he is given a way out, in which he is rescued, thanked, found or asked to stop, and in which anybody tells him on the page what asking does. **A man of forty-four said it to a man of twenty-two on the far side of a trestle and not to him, and it was not told to him, and the man of fifty-one looked at the man of twenty-two twice in about four minutes and asked him nothing.** He is still coming.

## What Movement V inherits, dated, with everything reset

- **Volume 04 is Chapters 151 to 200 and its working title is *Civic Weave*. Movement V is *The Ledger*, Chapters 191–200, whose ten days fall in weeks 64 to 67, with weeks 62, 63 and 68 unmapped.** Its pressure is physical crisis, proof, choice, aftermath. **Its job: a partial maintenance ledger comes out of a maintenance chamber, nobody reads it first, two people he did not choose read it between them, a man of fifty-eight does the correct thing and the correct thing is a purge, a mechanic and a records clerk carry one page out of a building in a bag that is not theirs, and one of them stops halfway because that is what the last carrier did.** And the choice, which is the second and last of the volume: **a card goes into a rack in a reading room with his name on it, in his own hand, on purpose, and the reason is that a ledger nobody may read is a ledger a person owns, and a ledger a person owns dies with the person.** **The words *rack* and *ledger* appear nowhere in Chapters 181 to 190, and a Volume 04 writer who finds either one in Movement IV has written the volume's own resolution by accident.**
- **TWO RE-BASES ARE IN FORCE AND NEITHER HAS BEEN SPENT.** The power state, and the recognition re-based into a card in a rack, in his own hand, renewable, removable by a clerk, no title, no office, which costs him not being findable. **No page of Movements I, II, III or IV anticipates one and no name is on the front of anything.**
- **Dated, fixed and not to be reset. Week 58 = Mon 292 – Sun 298. Week 59 = Mon 299 – Sun 305. Week 60 = Mon 306 – Sun 312. Week 61 = Mon 313 – Sun 319. Week 62 = Mon 320 – Sun 326. Week 63 = Mon 327 – Sun 333. Week 64 = Mon 334 – Sun 340. Week 65 = Mon 341 – Sun 347. Week 66 = Mon 348 – Sun 354. Week 67 = Mon 355 – Sun 361. Week 68 = Mon 362 – Sun 368.** The Exchange runs on four-week spacing: 16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 48, 52, 56, and therefore **60, 64 and 68. THE WEDNESDAY OF WEEK SIXTY IS CHAPTER 185 AND THE BOOK WAS NOT OPENED FOR THE FOURTH TIME RUNNING. THE NEXT ONE IS THE WEDNESDAY OF WEEK SIXTY-FOUR AND IT IS THE LAST EXCHANGE IN THIS VOLUME. Bea Nunn will not open the book on a Friday. The book is at forty-four lines, the fortieth is three years old, line forty-four has a hole in it, and it does not go to forty-five in this volume. Asha Reed's sentence is retired.**
- **The interval map's two-day offset from the Monday of week thirty-one is inherited, is recorded for the ninth time, and is not to be re-derived. No chapter in Volume 04 printed a day number, and no month-name, month-date, day-date or year. Three month-names were drafted in Chapter 185 and Chapter 186 and cut before the files were finished.**
- **The specification runs to four sections and three lines and the funded third line is minuted, and the fund's committee does not meet again and nothing may be settled by a committee, a minute, a notice, a report or a leaflet.** The seventh session was Chapter 158, the eighth Chapter 167, the ninth Chapter 177, **the tenth is Chapter 187 and the eleventh falls in Movement V.** **An officer of a fund has decided not to read five jobs off the front of a leaflet an eleventh time and gave the reason out loud in a room, and nobody stopped him.**
- **The Friday rota: four days a week at grade two and two Fridays at grade three, on a card in date or an exception recorded and renewed in a box that says *No reason given by holder*.** About eleven people a week, seven on the rota and about four not on it. **Seven Fridays of this volume have gone by with nobody asking him where a release is going to come from. A man of thirty-four on nights is allowed each of two sentences once a week, used one on the Monday of week fifty-nine, the other on the Tuesday, and had nothing at all to say on the Friday.**
- **The master is in an inside pocket on a chair, the decision is unmade after a term and a half, the original is in the fourth pocket of the tool roll with the fifth line blank, and the drawer under the bench holds five things, four of them other people's sentences. The master did not move in Movements I, II, III or IV and may not move before the volume's own choice.**
- **The relay may not be staged again in any form and the carrier may not be asked again. There is no panel in this volume and there was none in Movement IV.**
- **Series milestone 3's first half is spent and may not be spent again; its public half is spent once, in Chapter 172; and the second of the two public method disagreements is spent, in Chapter 184. Milestone 4 belongs to Chapters 195 and later and nothing in Movement IV pre-empts it. Milestones 1 and 2 were both paid in Chapter 163 and are not re-paid. Talia Venn appears in the prose of none of Chapters 181 to 190.**
- **The seven is still seven, the four things that are not the seven are still four, and the word *pattern* does not appear in Volume 04. Asha Reed's sentence is not said again. Steve Akers is not given to anybody. A woman of thirty-four with a hip is not found. Nell Senn is unwritten. The Crown Clause is never named and the names Iona and Sorn appear nowhere. Nobody identifies an authorship and the name of the person who inserted the page is planted and stays planted.**
- **Open, unpaid, and not to be closed by a document:** the licensor, still blank, behind a postbox in Brin that nobody will open for a stranger; the six lines in the register, of which one has an author still in this city and the man upstairs will not say which; the second visitor column's five other words; the bit of pale card in a gate hut; the unsigned sheet and its four correct steps; the woman of thirty-nine and her page of her own; the four hundred and one replies and the nine who got the company answer; the nine words wrong in a minute; the fifth room and who takes the name out; the unlabelled bay, the list of named holders, the boy at the control post, Delia Frost's landing, Nkechi Osei's Mondays; the hundred and forty-one pounds on a coat hook in an unopened envelope; **the name in the pencil box, which nobody took, which cannot come out, and which one name in about two hundred pages; and the second of the two public method disagreements, which is now spent and is closed.**
- **NEW AND OPEN, FROM MOVEMENT IV:** **three people who can each remove one other person from one thing, and two of the three things can be put back by the same person and one cannot be. A district nurse who asks a man to be somewhere else rather than to leave, and who got five answers out of eleven in eleven minutes and has six to go back to. A work card in biro on the back of which is the finding that ending a share does not un-teach a room. A key and a diary with nine lines in it. A sheet of paper with a woman's own name at the bottom of it in her own hand, in a handbag, going to a shift. A leaflet with a man's nine words on the back of it in a bin and then in a skip. A stamped envelope in a coat. Twenty-nine telephone calls to a woman of sixty-one, three of which became a room. A man of thirty-four who wrote the truth on the back of an offensive card and cannot unring it. A man of thirty-four who used one sentence on a Monday and the other on a Tuesday and had nothing to say on the Friday. An officer of a fund who has begun removing things out loud with reasons attached. A man of fifty-two who has stopped asking to be checked. And a man of twenty-two who sat in a room for fifty minutes and said nothing, and wrote down five numbers and no names.**
- **THREE THINGS A MOVEMENT V WRITER INHERITS AS SETTLED: that a person can be removed from a share, a name and a room by the people who put them there; that the Exchange's book is nearly empty and the emptiness is the same rule as the safety; and that the officer of the fund has started removing things out loud with reasons attached, which is the volume's habit arriving in a person who is not him.**
- **AND ONE THING A MOVEMENT V WRITER MUST FIND UNWRITTEN: the corollary of the movement's event, which is in the close's load book and not in its prose. The same three people can put a share, a name and a room back, one at a time, and nobody has done it, and the reason nobody has done it is that a share has a party and a room has a key and a name in pencil has neither. The volume's resolution is a thing somebody else can hold and he can be removed from, and a card in a rack is a removal of the last kind: a man of twenty-two removed from not being findable, by himself, in a room, in his own hand.**
- **MOVEMENT IV CHECKED THE SEVEN DEFECT CLASSES BEFORE EACH FILE WAS FINISHED RATHER THAN AFTER, AND FOUND SEVEN INTERVAL ERRORS, THREE WRONG COUNTS OF WORDS, TWO WRONG COUNTS OF PEOPLE AND CHAIRS, A FIVE-HUNDRED-AND-FIFTY-EIGHT-CHARACTER RUN AGAINST CHAPTER 180 AND FOUR NEAR-DUPLICATE BENCH PASSAGES, ALL IN ITS OWN FILES.** The record is `workspace/volume-04/batch-0004/SUMMARY.md`. **AND IT RESOLVED FLAG 1 OF THE PREVIOUS BATCH: the printed hedge rule, implemented correctly, reproduces all three of this volume's finished movements exactly — 218, 206 and 184 — and the discrepancy that pass recorded was an implementation fault in its own matcher and not an error in the prose. The corrected rule is printed in full in that summary and nowhere else. Three figures in the finished movements do not reproduce under it and all three are recorded there rather than patched: Movement I's word total, Volume 03 batch 0005's word total, and Volume 03 batch 0005's true-hedge count.**
- **AND ONE DEFECT IN A CLOSED BATCH THAT THIS PASS FOUND AND DID NOT PATCH: the load book of `workspace/volume-04/batch-0003/chapter-0177.md` says the five jobs on the leaflet were chosen by software out of two job sheets, and the canon sentence in `batch-0001/chapter-0158.md` is *A system chose them out*. The prose of Chapter 177 has it right and the load book does not, and batch 0003's summary claims it was restored. A closed batch is not this pass's to edit and the discrepancy is recorded in the batch 0004 summary and here.**

# VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT III (Chapters 171–180), "A List And A Lock", and what Movement IV inherits, dated

**Volume 04 is open at Chapter 180. This block is the state at the end of Movement III. It supersedes nothing in the `VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT II` block below it and it inherits every line of it. The load-book run is 174 to 183. Batch 0004 is Chapters 181–190, Movement IV, *What People Are Owed*, whose ten days fall in weeks 59 to 61, and its first load-book entry is 184.**

## What Movement III closed, and what it closed *against*

1. **THE SEARCH FOR A PERSON WITH AUTHORITY OVER A BUILDING'S OWN RECORDS IS CLOSED, AND IT CLOSED WITH *THERE IS NOT ONE*.** Closed against: any reading in which a company, a landlord or a woman with a trolley is made into a body that can be asked to decide, and against any reading in which a person or a post is created to hold the answer. **A man of forty-four with a roll of plates: a service plate, a manufacturer's line, a service number off a schedule of doors no building has ever seen, twenty-two years in the trade, and the engineer's sentence that is both true and useless. A landlord on a schedule of title with a letterhead and a solicitor and no hours. A woman of forty-six with forty-one doors a week and a sheet nobody has ever opened.** None of the three has ever been asked to decide anything, and the movement's whole finding is that this is not a conspiracy and not a failure of character; it is a list, a freehold and a piece of paper, and a habit of not asking. **A landlord's name was considered and not used, and a landlord is an entry on a schedule of title and not a character. A company is a service list and a contract and not a faction.**
2. **SERIES MILESTONE 3'S PUBLIC HALF IS PAID, ONCE, IN CHAPTER 172, AND ONLY THE FIRST OF THE TWO.** Closed against: any reading in which it was a scene, a declaration, a kiss, a reconciliation, or a retrospective about Volume 03, and against any reading in which either of them says *we*. **A corridor off a service road, nine men, a public disagreement about method: she says the thing he has built is the only version of a person holding a rule she has seen work in four years of telling rooms it was possible, and he says the difference is who pays, and both are right, and the residue is carried by a man of thirty-four on nights who asked one of them to be wrong and was not given that.** She is named in the load book and not in the prose.
3. **THE PLANS ARE OPEN AND THE FIRST PERSON DOWN THOSE STAIRS WAS A WOMAN OF FORTY-FIVE.** Closed against: any reading in which Marek Senn went first, and against any reading in which the drawer produced an answer. **One roll, a date in ink, nineteen years old, the room 2-07 on the plan and 2-14 on the door, both correct about themselves, and the two records not in the same room since before a service contract existed. It went back in the drawer.** The foreman of fifty-eight said again, in a corridor, that he is not going to be the one who turns his key. Ines Kolar said no in nine seconds and did forty-one doors.
4. **THE MIDPOINT IS DELIVERED AND THE AMENDMENT IS FILED, NOT HIDDEN.** Closed against: any reading in which a name is identified, in which a page is secret, in which a person is a villain, or in which the illegality is a repealed rule. **Nobody has broken a rule. A page is stapled into a working copy over a printed line, with no initials, no date and no name on it, and the staple is older than the ink around it, and the requirement it sits over is in print in the original words three shelves down in a public room and has been for fourteen years.** The illegality is fourteen years of a city operating outside a live requirement because the only copy anybody has ever opened is the one in the box.
5. **A WOMAN OF FORTY-FIVE RECOGNISED A HANDWRITING AND SAID THE SAME NINE WORDS.** Closed against: any reading in which she explains, in which she says a name, in which her recognition becomes a statement, or in which she says anything she has not already said in this manuscript. **She says *and I am not going to tell you why*, which is Chapter 139 of Volume 03's sentence word for word, and the reason she gives is a thing about a person in this city and not about a practice, with the cost of not saying it stated on the page. A first version of that reason named a braid, a bench, eleven men and about eleven years, and a review pass struck all four nouns out of it because together they pointed straight at the shape of the thing this volume is not allowed to say; THE FIELD IS BLANK AND IT IS MEANT TO BE, and the name is not said, and nobody asked her a second question.**
6. **A RULE IS ONLY AS REAL AS THE COPY PEOPLE OPEN, AND THE PERSON WHO RUNS THE ROOM HAD NOT OPENED IT IN NINE YEARS.** Closed against: any reading in which the registrar ruled a column, made a record, adopted anything, or became a post. **She said the volume's sentence and then said the half that costs her, and she ruled no column and will not, and the record is a sentence said once out loud to two people and nobody else, and the only instrument she has is her own hours.**
7. **A PRINTED LINE AT THE FOOT OF A DISTRICT INSTRUCTION HAS BEEN OBEYED FOR NINETEEN YEARS BY ONE CLERK AND BROKEN ONCE.** Closed against: any reading in which she was right or wrong, and against any reading in which the box is corrected, erased, opened or adopted. **She said she does not know whether she was right and is not going to, the page goes back in a stack with a rubber band on it, and there is no way in this city for a thing that was never in a system to be put right and she is not going to invent one. He has not written it down. THE SECOND NAME WAS NOT PUT IN THE BOX AND THE BOX WAS NOT TOUCHED, and that is Movement IV's hardest object and it is intact.**
8. **THE MOVEMENT'S ANTAGONIST IS NAMED AND IT IS A COLUMN HEADING.** Closed against: any reading in which Iven Sore is put on the page in this movement, in which the Continuity Office agrees to anything, or in which a repeal is drafted, minuted, noticed or reported. **A register of removals does not contain the Compact, no line anywhere says who may change it, the man upstairs is a person and not a post and nobody has ever asked him to be the authority over anything, and the only authority that exists in that building is the word at the top of the fourth column.** **The six lines are about a BINDER, not about the four volumes in a box: a binder went down a corridor to a room with a register in it in the week of week thirty-eight, and the working copy in four volumes is a different object on a counter in Lattice Ward that has never been in that room. A review pass corrected a Chapter 180 sentence that had merged the two, and a later writer must not merge them again.**
9. **THE STANDING PROBLEM IS AT SEVEN AND HAS A SECOND HALF.** Closed against: any reading in which a rule about who may be asked was invented, and against the book version of his own unreliability, which is Movement IV's. **He said the condition himself for the second time in four weeks and then, for the first time in a room, said that he can say what he heard in a corridor and cannot hear a corridor, and that in a fortnight he talked over three people and failed to hear one, and that the one he failed to hear is why a thing was not noticed. A district nurse of thirty-four gave her name into the same microphone and said the condition is the only reason four people with a self-ruled file are not a group. Neither is in print and neither was written down.**
10. **THE PRINTING DEBT IS PAID AND IT COST HER A FORM.** Closed against: any reading in which she is forgiven, softened, recruited or given a decision, and against any mechanism for taking a card back off anybody. **A quarterly return with her shop's name against a number and a refusal to put a number in the other field; the hundred and forty-one pounds on a coat hook because a form wants a name at the top of it; about four hundred and thirty-two still in about nine places in one district; a boy of nineteen's handwriting on four hundred pieces of paper and his name on none of them.**
11. **THE EXCHANGE'S BOOK IS AT FORTY-FOUR LINES AND HAS NOW NOT BEEN OPENED FOR THREE SITTINGS.** Closed against: any reading in which a caller signs, in which the book grows, or in which the reason is a mood. **A book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call, given in one go, and Bea Nunn said in addition that the book is the most honest object in this city because it is nearly empty, and that about twenty people have now telephoned her to be told yes.** The next Exchange is the Wednesday of week sixty.

## What Movement IV inherits, dated, with everything reset

- **Volume 04 is Chapters 151 to 200 and its working title is *Civic Weave*. Movement IV is *What People Are Owed*, Chapters 181–190, whose ten days fall in weeks 59 to 61, with weeks 62 and 63 unmapped.** Its pressure is character conflict, moral cost, and **the first time a person is removed from a thing rather than added to it. He is asked to leave a room, and he leaves it, and the room does not stop: a woman of thirty-four with a clipboard asks him to be somewhere else while she does a thing she has to do without an audience, and he goes, and four people in the corridor notice that he went.** What changes: **the volume's argument is answered by people who are not him, again, and the specific thing that changes is that a person in this city can now be removed from a share, a name and a room, one at a time, by the people who put them there.**
- **THE SECOND OF THE TWO PUBLIC METHOD DISAGREEMENTS IS OWED TO MOVEMENT IV AND IS NOT SPENT IN MOVEMENT III.** `outline/volume-04.md` promises it twice; Chapter 172 spent the first and only the first. **The second is a different pair of people in a different room. Closed against: any reading in which the milestone is spent once and the outline's *twice* is quietly satisfied, and against any reading in which Chapter 172's corridor is restaged.**
- **Doreen Abbiss's name in the pencil box is Movement IV's hardest object and it is now heavier, not lighter, because a clerk of fifty-three has said out loud that there is no way in this city for a thing that was never in a system to be put right, and because exactly one person knows she drew the box.** **A name may be put in that box in Movement IV and it must be put there by the person it is for, and it may not take a reference number, a system, a post or a district. There is exactly one box in this manuscript.**
- **Peter Amado, about fifty-one, is owed a way out that nobody has offered him, and is not offered one in this volume either, and is still coming. His microphone question is answered by nobody and a woman of forty-three's hall table is not.**
- **The man of thirty-four on nights, the woman of twenty-six who types things and has four answers written and not sent, the boy of nineteen, the foreman of fifty-eight, the printer of fifty-eight, the clerk of fifty-three, the woman of forty-one, the woman of forty-five with a braid of tools, and a nurse of thirty-four with a file with her name at the top of it — every one of them pays something in this movement and none of them is paid back.**
- **TWO RE-BASES ARE IN FORCE AND NEITHER HAS BEEN SPENT.** The power state, and the recognition re-based into a card in a rack, in his own hand, renewable, removable by a clerk, no title, which costs him not being findable. **No page of Movement I, II or III anticipates one, and no name is on the front of anything.** Both are argued in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md` section 10 and written into `outline/volume-04.md`.
- **Dated, fixed and not to be reset. Week 58 = Mon 292 – Sun 298. Week 59 = Mon 299 – Sun 305. Week 60 = Mon 306 – Sun 312. Week 61 = Mon 313 – Sun 319. Week 62 = Mon 320 – Sun 326. Week 63 = Mon 327 – Sun 333.** The Exchange runs on four-week spacing: 16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 48, 52, 56, and **therefore 60, 64 and 68.** **The Wednesday of week fifty-six was Chapter 175 and the book was not opened for the third time running. THE NEXT ONE IS THE WEDNESDAY OF WEEK SIXTY. Bea Nunn will not open the book on a Friday. The book is at forty-four lines, the fortieth is three years old, line forty-four has a hole in it, and it does not go to forty-five in this volume. Asha Reed's sentence is retired.**
- **The interval map's two-day offset from the Monday of week thirty-one is inherited, is recorded for the eighth time, and is not to be re-derived. No chapter in Volume 04 printed a day number, and no month-name, month-date, day-date or year.**
- **The specification runs to four sections and three lines and the funded third line of nine words is minuted, and the fund's committee does not meet again and nothing may be settled by a committee, a minute, a notice, a report or a leaflet.** The seventh session was Chapter 158, the eighth Chapter 167, the ninth Chapter 177, and the tenth is four weeks after the ninth. **The standing problem is at seven in this volume and is not resolved.**
- **The Friday rota: four days a week at grade two and two Fridays at grade three, on a card in date or an exception recorded and renewed in a box that says *No reason given by holder*.** About eleven people a week, seven on the rota and about four not on it. **The number is on the back of a man's own card, is not on a form anywhere, is not a rate, and is now in three places and a fourth, and none of the four has told the other three.**
- **The master is in an inside pocket on a chair in Lattice Ward, the decision is unmade after a term and a half, the original is in the fourth pocket of the tool roll with the fifth line blank, and the drawer under the bench holds five things, four of them other people's sentences.** The master did not move in Movement I, II or III and may not move until the volume's own choice.
- **The relay may not be staged again in any form and the carrier may not be asked again. There is no panel in this volume and there was none in Movement III.**
- **Series milestone 3's first half is spent and may not be spent again; its public half is spent once, in Chapter 172, and the second of the two is owed to Movement IV. Milestone 4 belongs to Volumes 05 and 06 and nothing in Volume 04 may pre-empt it. Milestones 1 and 2 were both paid in Chapter 163 and are not re-paid.**
- **The seven is still seven, the four things that are not the seven are still four, and the word *pattern* does not appear in Volume 04. Asha Reed's sentence is not said again. Steve Akers is not given to anybody. A woman of thirty-four with a hip is not found. Nell Senn is unwritten. The Crown Clause is never named and the names Iona and Sorn appear nowhere. Nobody identifies an authorship and the name of the person who inserted the page is planted and stays planted.**
- **Open, unpaid, and not to be closed by a document:** the licensor, still blank, behind a postbox in Brin that nobody will open for a stranger; the six lines in the register, of which one has an author still in this city and the man upstairs will not say which; the second visitor column's five other words; the bit of pale card in a gate hut; the unsigned sheet and its four correct steps; the woman of thirty-nine and her page of her own; the four hundred and one replies and the nine who got the company answer; the nine words wrong in a minute; the fifth room and who takes the name out; the unlabelled bay, the list of named holders, the boy at the control post, Delia Frost's landing, Nkechi Osei's Mondays; the hundred and forty-one pounds on a coat hook; **the second name in the pencil box, which nobody has put in and which only the person it is for may put in; and the second of the two public method disagreements, which is a different pair of people in a different room.**
- **NEW AND OPEN, FROM MOVEMENT III:** **a company, a landlord and a woman with a trolley, and the finding that none of the three has ever been asked to decide anything. A set of plans back in a drawer on a hook with a date in ink on it. A page stapled into a working copy with no initials, no date and no name on it. A printed Civic Compact three shelves down on an open trolley shelf in a public room, unread in nine years by anybody including the woman who owns the counter. A rule that is only as real as the copy people open. A line at the foot of a district instruction that says no local additions, obeyed for nineteen years by one woman and broken once. A form with a clerk's name against a number on it, correct, and correct and changing nothing. Nineteen slips in a tray and none of them about a box. A column heading that cannot be asked a question. And a second condition that is on no sheet: he can say what he heard in a corridor and cannot hear a corridor.**
- **MOVEMENT III WENT THROUGH A REVIEW REPAIR PASS, AND THE SIX CLASSES IT FOUND ARE THE ONES A MOVEMENT IV WRITER SHOULD CHECK BEFORE A FILE IS FINISHED RATHER THAN AFTER.** **A review pass returned six blockers and seven should-fixes on Movement III and every one was actioned without a scene being rewritten; the record is `workspace/volume-04/batch-0003/SUMMARY.md`. The classes, in the order a later writer is most likely to hit them: (1) DATE INTERVALS, because the load book counts a day in every chapter and a chapter that runs long drifts — Movement III said *about eleven days* for a breach eleven weeks old, put a Thursday of week 55 on a Wednesday of week 57 as *last week*, said *the eleventh day* for five weeks, and gave two different intervals for the same two events in one file. (2) THREE DOCUMENTS MERGED INTO ONE SENTENCE, which is what a chapter that has accumulated forty-one lines, a ring file, a pencil and a back of a sheet will do. (3) ONE OBJECT IN TWO PLACES, which is what a batch does when it introduces a working copy in one chapter and refers to it in another without noticing that canon already named the other thing a binder. (4) QUOTED CANON THAT HAS DRIFTED — the fourth condition lost its closing clause in one chapter and changed *this department* to *that department* in two, and a canon sentence came back as *a piece of software* where it says *a system*. (5) THE WRITER'S OWN ARITHMETIC INSIDE A SENTENCE: four claims of a word count were wrong, and the motif *nine words* is load-bearing in this volume and must be counted, not estimated. (6) TEMPLATE PROSE — five chapters ended on the same line, two chapters opened on the same sentence, and four load books re-argued the dialogue of the scene they were recording, which is forbidden in terms and is the check a self-review applies least reliably. AND THE SEVENTH: A SUMMARY THAT MISREPORTS ITS OWN BATCH, in this case four claims, two of them a figure printed twice at two values. Every published figure in that summary has now been re-measured on the files as they stand, and its first set of ten overlap figures could not be reproduced and is superseded. A VOLUME 04 WRITER SHOULD RE-RUN EVERY FIGURE BEFORE PRINTING IT.**

# VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT II (Chapters 161–170), "The Reading Room", and what Movement III inherits, dated

**Volume 04 is open at Chapter 170. This block is the state at the end of Movement II. It supersedes nothing in the `VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT I` block below it and it inherits every line of it. The load-book run is 164 to 173. Batch 0003 is Chapters 171–180, Movement III, *A List And A Lock*, weeks 55 to 58, with its midpoint at Chapter 175 on the Wednesday of week fifty-six, and its first load-book entry is 174.**

## What Movement II closed, and what it closed *against*

1. **THE FOURTH COLUMN HAS BEEN ASKED ABOUT AND ANSWERED, ONCE, BY THE PERSON AT THE TABLE.** Closed against: any reading in which the answer is his, and any reading in which the movement produced a document. **It produced a name in a room and no document, and the only copy is in Imogen Bray's head, and she is findable by anybody who walks into that building and asks a woman of thirty-four what she thinks of the fourth column.** Her finding is not a complaint: four hundred people have had that column read out and not one has come back and said what they thought it was, and she cannot tell whether that is agreement or a queue. **The next thing anybody wants to do with a column in this city is to make somebody responsible for it, and she has said out loud that she has been dull at that table for four years and being dull has not protected one single thing.**
2. **THE STANDING PROBLEM HAS BEEN SAID A SIXTH TIME, BY HIM, AND IS STILL UNRESOLVED.** Closed against: any reading in which a rule about who may be asked was invented, and any reading in which it was resolved. **He said it himself, standing up, in the first person, in a room with a microphone on it, which is the first time in a term and a half that he has said it rather than let somebody say it about him. The asker was a woman of forty-three who had read the leaflet and whose question was not stupid, and she was told nobody can say who is allowed to ask him, and she said then I am allowed, and nobody tried, and she named her own cost and it was a hall table.** The count in this volume is now six. The resolution's is the seventh and belongs to the last movement.
3. **ROY AMISS'S UNRINGED TELEPHONE IS PAID, BY A DISTRICT NURSE OF THIRTY-FOUR, IN ABOUT NINE SECONDS, AND IT WAS NOT A RESCUE.** Closed against: any reading in which he is found, thanked, comforted, reconciled with, or given a leaflet, a form, a review or an apology. **He said the thing about the call, which he had practised on a bus, refused to thank her and gave the reason, asked for his own sentence about there being no reason anybody owes him to be written down somewhere, was told there is no record of it, and accepted that he has said it into a telephone in a corridor, which is a worse record than a leaflet. She did not fill in the district's form and gave the reason, and she has about forty more to ring and no rule and no help.** The payment changed almost nothing and that is the point. **The leaflet is still on his table and it is still about a practice and not about him.**
4. **THE TELEPHONE ON A WALL IN A PREFAB OFFICE OFF THE MERIDIAN CORRIDOR HAS BEEN RUNG, BY SOMEBODY WHO IS NOT MAREK SENN.** Closed against: any reading in which the call closed the licensor, or named the holder of the box, or made a foreman a hero. **A stock clerk of thirty-one re-verified a field on a stock return that has not changed in four quarters and has not been thanked and does not know there is a case. A box number and a four-letter post town are a real place: a postbox on an end wall third bay of a sorting office in a town called Brin, a card on it that is the only record of who holds it, a counter, opening hours, about four hundred boxes in the building and about nine in the town that anybody collects from, the rest company names, a woman at the counter who will not tell a stranger and would not tell him standing there, and about two years since anybody touched it.** Closed against: a Volume 04 writer naming the holder of that box, and against anyone using that counter to close anything. **The licensor is unnamed and the field is blank and nothing in Brin closes anything.**
5. **THE NUMBER HAS A SAMPLE AND THE SAMPLE IS ON THE BACK OF A CARD IN A WALLET AND IN TWO HEADS.** Closed against: any reading in which the eleven became a rate, a figure on a form, or a thing anybody can quote. **A lift engineer of twenty-nine has sixteen Fridays on the back of her own card and gave them to the one man who is not allowed to be asked about a number, on purpose, and he wrote no line of it on a docket. A man of thirty-four who works nights has counted it twice since the spring and once since the summer and does not know she has got a list. It is in three places and none of the three has told the other two, and there is no form in this city that takes it.**
6. **THE READING ROOM IS A PLACE PEOPLE WALK TO AND NOBODY DESIGNED IT, AND WHAT HAS BEEN CREATED IS A HABIT AND NOT A MECHANISM.** Closed against: any reading in which the room becomes a service, a post, a mechanism, or a thing with a procedure. **Eleven slips in a tray in about three weeks and not one about a box, behind about forty read-outs in nine years. Four people have asked the registrar a question that was not about a box, which in nine years nobody had, and she has answered all four and has not got a rule for answering a question and has said so out loud. She is a woman who works three days a week and she has said that out loud to a man with a carpenter's pencil in his ear, and what that room does happens when she is standing behind the counter, and a habit can be stopped by that person walking out.** Closed against: any reading in which a field was added to a slip, a second box was ruled, or the tray became a register.
7. **A MAN OF THIRTY-FOUR HAS A REFUSAL WITH NO REASON ON IT, AND THE ONLY EVIDENCE IS THAT IT CAME FIRST.** Closed against: any reading in which he was rescued, taught, corrected, or given a better answer. **The sentence that would have been a better answer was constructed in a stairwell and refused, and the refusal was told out loud, and the reason for the refusal is that a sentence in a stairwell is not a reason and in four years it would be the only thing in this city saying he was right. He wants it kept and not used and there is a difference and nobody in this city has a word for it and he is not asking for one.**
8. **THE PENCIL HAS BEEN TURNED OVER AND THE NAME HAS BEEN SAID, AND IT WAS SAID BY THE WOMAN OF FORTY-SIX BEFORE SHE WAS ASKED.** Closed against: any reading in which she is thanked, recruited, put on a distribution, or made the person who decided something. **The correct action followed a woman of thirty-six turning a sheet over: a line in a building book saying there are fourteen items, no column, not actioned, and in about four years the pencil will have come off and there will be nothing on that page.** Closed against: the forty-first line being adopted by anybody, and against a second sheet being asked for.
9. **THE EXCHANGE'S BOOK DID NOT GO TO FORTY-FIVE AND NO CALLER SIGNED.** Closed against: any reading in which a woman of thirty-one who rang a number to ask whether the room was real became a caller, and against any reading in which a man of thirty-four's two minutes went in the book. **The reason was given out loud in one go and it is a rule and not a mood: a book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call.** The fortieth line is three years old, line forty-four has its hole in it, and the fifth chair against the wall is still empty and was offered a reason to be moved.
10. **BOTH RELATIONSHIP BEATS ARE PAID AND BOTH ARE PAID IN ONE CHAPTER.** Closed against: any reading in which they are paid in a scene, a declaration, a kiss, a reconciliation, or a retrospective about Volume 03, and against any reading in which the two of them use the word *we*. **Milestone 1 is reported and not staged. Milestone 2 is a corridor, three words about the gap, nine words about what he did, and a woman going into a flat.**

## What Movement III inherits, dated, with everything reset

- **Volume 04 is Chapters 151 to 200 and its working title is *Civic Weave*.** **Movement III is *A List And A Lock*, Chapters 171–180, weeks 55 to 58, and the midpoint is Chapter 175 on the Wednesday of week fifty-six.** Its pressure is investigation, the midpoint, and the first time the volume's own argument is proved against the volume's hero. **Its job: they go looking for a person with authority over a building's own records and there is not one in this city yet, and the reason there is not one is that the authority is a company and a landlord and a woman with a trolley, and none of the three has ever been asked to decide anything.** A building plan rolled in a drawer on a hook in a basement in the fourth building, with a date on it, and the room is 2-07 on the plan and 2-14 on the door and both are correct about themselves. **A foreman of fifty-eight has a key to that building and has not used it and said so out loud in a public room, and a man of twenty-two wrote in his own book in the last movement that nobody in this case has earned a basement, and the first person down those stairs must not be him.**
- **TWO RE-BASES ARE IN FORCE AND NEITHER HAS BEEN SPENT.** (1) **The power state.** `outline/series.md`'s *Stage 2, with the first sustained relay* is superseded and a sustained relay is not available at any price. The correct state is **Stage 2 Counterpoint with a permanent sensory sensitivity, an uncorrectable word, a demonstrated-but-unrepeatable relay, and a fourth condition that stops him doing the thing and does not stop him being asked about it.** (2) **The recognition.** *A recognised researcher, not a public hero* is re-based into **a card in a rack in a reading room, in his own hand, on purpose, renewable, removable by a clerk, no title on it, which costs him the last thing he has, which is not being findable.** **Neither of Volume 04's first two movements is a recognition and no page anticipates one.** Both are argued in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md` section 10 and written into `outline/volume-04.md`. `outline/series.md` was not edited and is not to be silently contradicted.
- **Dated, fixed, and not to be reset. Week 46 = Mon 208 – Sun 214. Week 47 = Mon 215 – Sun 221. Week 48 = Mon 222 – Sun 228. Week 49 = Mon 229 – Sun 235. Week 50 = Mon 236 – Sun 242. Week 51 = Mon 243 – Sun 249. Week 52 = Mon 250 – Sun 256. Week 53 = Mon 257 – Sun 263. Week 54 = Mon 264 – Sun 270. Week 55 = Mon 271 – Sun 277. Week 56 = Mon 278 – Sun 284.** The Exchange runs on four-week spacing: 16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 48, 52, and **therefore 56, 60, 64, 68.** **The Wednesday of week fifty-two was Chapter 165 and the book was not opened. The next one is the Wednesday of week fifty-six and it is the midpoint chapter's week. Bea Nunn will not open the book on a Friday. The book is at forty-four lines, the fortieth is three years old, line forty-four has a hole in it, and it is not a register, and it does not go to forty-five in this volume. Asha Reed's sentence is retired.**
- **The interval map's two-day offset from the Monday of week thirty-one is inherited, is recorded for the seventh time, and is not to be re-derived. No chapter in Volume 04 printed a day number, and no month-name, month-date, day-date or year.**
- **The specification runs to four sections and had two lines and a funded third line of nine words, and the fund's committee does not meet again and nothing may be settled by a committee, a minute, a notice, a report or a leaflet.** The seventh session was Chapter 158, the eighth was Chapter 167, and the ninth is four weeks after the eighth.
- **The Friday rota: four days a week at grade two and two Fridays at grade three, on a card in date or an exception recorded and renewed in a box that says *No reason given by holder*.** About eleven people a week, seven on the rota and about four not on it. **The number is on the back of a man's own card, is not on a form anywhere, is not a rate, and is now in three places and a fourth: a lift engineer's cardstock, his head, and a man of thirty-four who works nights, and none of the three has told the other two.**
- **The master is in an inside pocket on a chair in Lattice Ward, and the decision is unmade after a term and a half, and the original is in the fourth pocket of the tool roll with the fifth line still blank, and the drawer under the bench holds five things, four of them other people's sentences.** The master did not move in Movement I or Movement II and may not move in Movement III.
- **The box in pencil has one name in it, still cannot be emptied, and this movement deliberately did not put a second name in it. It may take a name in Movement IV and it must be put there by the person it is for, and it may not take a reference number, a system, a post or a district.**
- **The relay may not be staged again in any form, and the carrier may not be asked again. There is no panel in this volume and there was none in Movement II.**
- **Series milestone 3 is spent and may not be spent again. Milestone 4 belongs to Volumes 05 and 06 and nothing in Volume 04 may pre-empt it. Milestones 1 and 2 are both PAID, in Chapter 163, and no scene, declaration, kiss or *we* was used. Milestone 3 is assigned by the outline to Chapters 166 and 167, which Movement II spent on the telephone on a wall and the standing problem, so it is UNDELIVERED, and `workspace/volume-04/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` reassigns it to Chapter 172 and says in terms that it has never once been said.**
- **The seven is still seven, the four things that are not the seven are still four, and the word *pattern* does not appear in Volume 04.** Asha Reed's sentence is not said again. Steve Akers is not given to anybody. A woman of thirty-four with a hip is not found. Nell Senn is unwritten. The Crown Clause is never named and the names Iona and Sorn appear nowhere.
- **Open, unpaid, and not to be closed by a document:** the licensor, still blank, and now with a postbox in Brin behind it that nobody will open for a stranger; the six lines in the register, of which one has an author still in this city and Iven Sore will not say which; the second visitor column's five other words; the bit of pale card in a gate hut; the unsigned sheet and its four correct steps; the woman of thirty-nine and her page of her own; the four hundred and one replies and the nine who got the company answer; the nine words wrong in a minute; the fifth room and who takes the name out; the unlabelled bay, the list of named holders, the boy at the control post, Delia Frost's landing, Nkechi Osei's Mondays; the hundred and forty-one pounds, which has gone into a coat with a person nobody can name; **the printer's debt, which Movement II planted and did not pay: a woman of forty-six is still the person who decides what a card is for, and a boy of nineteen's handwriting is on four hundred pieces of paper and the four hundred and thirty-two older ones are still in about nine places in one district;** **and the set of plans in a drawer on a hook in a basement in the fourth building, which a foreman of fifty-eight has a key to and has not used.**
- **OWED TO MOVEMENT IV AND UNASSIGNED, FOUND BY A REVIEW OF MOVEMENT II AND NOW RECORDED SO IT CANNOT BE LOST:** **`outline/volume-04.md` promises the public disagreement about method TWICE in this volume — *they will disagree about method in front of other people, twice, and neither of them will use the word we to do it, and neither of them will be wrong.* Movement II spent the chapters the outline gave it and delivered neither. `workspace/volume-04/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` assigns the FIRST to Chapter 172, the Tuesday of week fifty-five, and states in terms that it has never been said. THE SECOND IS UNASSIGNED, IS OWED TO MOVEMENT IV, IS NOT TO BE SPENT IN MOVEMENT III, and is a different pair of people in a different room. A Volume 04 writer who reaches the end of Movement III with only one has not failed; a Volume 04 writer who spends the second before the volume's resolution has spent the volume's promise twice in three weeks and there is nothing left for the ending to pay.** Closed against: any reading in which the milestone is delivered once and the outline's *twice* is quietly satisfied.
- **NEW AND OPEN, FROM MOVEMENT II:** **a woman of thirty-four at a register table in Crown Terrace who has an opinion about the fourth column and is findable, and whose finding is that four hundred people have been told and none of them came back.** **Sixteen Fridays on the back of a card in a wallet, in a man's head, and in a man of thirty-four's memory, with none of the three knowing about the other two.** **A postbox in Brin on an end wall third bay with a card on it and a counter and a woman who will not say a name, and about two years since anybody touched it.** **A man of fifty-eight's name at the bottom of a slip in a tray in a public room, and a key to a basement he has not used, and a slip in the same tray with Marek Senn's name at the bottom of it, which is the first slip in that room with one thing written across all four printed lines in the first person and signed, and the registrar did not know until that morning that it was allowed.** **A tray with eleven slips in it in about three weeks and a twelfth on the Thursday after, none of them about a box, and a woman of forty-one who works three days a week who has said out loud that a habit can be stopped by her walking out, and who has two numbers she has put side by side unasked: about forty binder read-outs in nine years and about two hundred people through that door since the spring.** **A man of thirty-four who has said no twice to being the man on a card for his own four people, once with a reason and once without, and who wants the second kept and not used.** **A woman's name in a box in pencil, said out loud in an office by a woman of forty-six to a woman of thirty-six, and a line in a building book about fourteen items that changes nothing. THE NAME HAS NOT TRAVELLED: it is in two heads, neither of them his, and he does not know it is anywhere and is not going to find out this term.** **Eleven people who have telephoned a woman of sixty-one and been told yes.** **And a card on a hall table in Nine Bridges with a fifth line on it that nobody can answer a question about.**

# VOLUME 04 — MOVEMENT I (Chapters 151–160), "One Box", and what Movement II inherits, dated

**Volume 04 is open. `outline/volume-04.md` was written in the same run as this batch and supersedes the Volume 04 paragraph of `outline/series.md` on two points. The load-book run continues from 153 and Movement I is entries 154 to 163. Batch 0002 is Chapters 161–170, Movement II, *The Reading Room*, weeks 51 to 54, and its first load-book entry is 164.**

## What Movement I closed, and what it closed *against*

1. **THE BOX IN PENCIL HAS ITS FIRST NAME AND IT CANNOT COME OUT.** Doreen Abbiss, thirty-eight, a night care worker, gave her name on a telephone in about nine seconds and then asked for it out. **A name in pencil on a page in a stack of about two hundred cannot be taken out: the page cannot be taken out of the stack, the stack cannot be taken out of the counter, and a hole in a stack is a thing somebody asks about.** Closed against: any reading in which the name is removed, corrected, or made reversible, and any reading in which a third party is a proper route to putting a name in that box. **A Volume 04 writer may put a second name in that box, and it must be put there by the person it is for, or not at all.**
2. **THE PLATE IS A SERVICE PLATE AND THE ROOM HAS NEVER HAD ITS OWN NUMBER ON THE DOOR.** About nine hundred doors in four buildings are the same, the numbers come off a room list the company does not own, and a set of plans in a drawer on a hook in a basement has the rooms. **Nothing broke and nobody did anything on purpose.** Closed against: any reading in which the plate was swapped by a person, and any reading in which a service plate is a conspiracy. **It is a maintenance contractor, a list and a landlord who does not care.**
3. **A SEAL IS A COLUMN IN A REGISTER, AND A REGISTER IS IN A PUBLIC BUILDING WITH A COUNTER ON IT.** The Marrow Falls material was never locked. Nothing in the reading room is locked and there is a sign saying so that nobody reads. Closed against: any reading in which there is a lock, a padlock, a bar across a door, or a room that cannot be entered. **The route is a table by a counter and a woman of thirty-four who reads it out because it is a public document.**
4. **THE FOURTH COLUMN HAS NEVER HAD A PERSON IN IT.** About nine hundred job titles and not one name, in a register going back fourteen years, read out daily by a woman who has asked twice and been told once that it is how the register is written. **She has never been asked what she thinks of the fourth column and said on the Tuesday that if he comes back she would like to be.** Closed against: any reading in which the authority column is a conspiracy or a lie. It is how the register is written.
5. **THE CHOICE WAS MADE AND IT COST HER.** He put two letters of her name on a slip and did not write the third, and she said *you have just made me the reason*, and he asked her properly, and she said yes, and she said the cost before the yes. Closed against: any reading in which the choice is his sacrifice, and any reading in which the refusal to put her name on a paper is a defence of principle. **It is a refusal to end the one person's unfindability, and it cost her the daytime, and she said so out loud, and the slip is in a bin by a bell in Lattice Ward and the corridor is exactly where it was.**
6. **THE HUNDRED AND FORTY-ONE POUNDS HAS LEFT THE SHOP.** It went back to the person who paid it, in a coat, in front of a boy of nineteen, and the cost is that there is now nothing on that table to take. Closed against: any reading in which the money is recovered as evidence. **It is a loss, and it was chosen, and the printer is not thanked.**
7. **THE READING ROOM'S RULE IS A RULE AND NOT A MOOD.** A box does not come down without a slip; a slip is a piece of paper and a person. **No field was added to anything, no second box was ruled, and a slip without a name is a piece of paper with no tray.** Closed against: any reading in which the reading room becomes a mechanism, a service or a post. **It is a room with a counter, a bell, a trolley, a woman who works three days a week and a rule that predates her.**
8. **THE EXCHANGE'S BOOK DID NOT GO TO FORTY-FIVE.** Nine people, a Wednesday, nobody called, and the reason is a rule. **A caller who was in the room would not sign, gave her reason out loud, and then talked for nine minutes and none of it was written down.** Closed against: any reading in which the room writes her down, or in which the line in the book is the resolution.

## What Movement II inherits, dated, with everything reset

- **Volume 04 is Chapters 151 to 200 and its working title is *Civic Weave*.** Its central pressure: a document in this city can say *who ended it*, in pencil, in a clerk's hand, on one page on a counter, and the same city has about four hundred people holding a page with their own date on it and no person at the top of it. **Its first question is what the difference is between a form that has one box in it and a form that has none, and who is supposed to be the difference, given that the person who drew the box was told twice that there was no box for it. Its second question, which the series sketch does not have, is that whoever goes into that corridor next is going looking for a person with authority over a building's own records, and there is not one in this city yet.**
- **TWO RE-BASES ARE IN FORCE AND ARE DECISIONS.** (1) **The power state.** `outline/series.md`'s *Stage 2, with the first sustained relay* is superseded and a sustained relay is not available at any price. The correct state is **Stage 2 Counterpoint with a permanent sensory sensitivity, an uncorrectable word, a demonstrated-but-unrepeatable relay, and a fourth condition that stops him doing the thing and does not stop him being asked about it.** (2) **The recognition.** *A recognised researcher, not a public hero* is re-based into **a card in a rack in a reading room, in his own hand, on purpose, renewable, removable by a clerk, no title on it, which costs him the last thing he has, which is not being findable.** Both are argued in `workspace/volume-03/batch-0005/VOLUME-CLOSE.md` section 10 and written into `outline/volume-04.md`. **`outline/series.md` was not edited and is not to be silently contradicted; the supersession is recorded here, in `outline/volume-04.md` and in `state/current.md`.**
- **Dated, fixed, and not to be reset. Week 46 = Mon 208 – Sun 214, and Volume 03 ended on the Monday night of week forty-six. Week 47 = Mon 215 – Sun 221. Week 48 = Mon 222 – Sun 228. Week 49 = Mon 229 – Sun 235. Week 50 = Mon 236 – Sun 242. Week 51 = Mon 243 – Sun 249.** The Exchange runs on four-week spacing: 16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 48 — and **the Wednesday of week 48 happened in Chapter 156 and the book did not open — and therefore 52, 56, 60, 64, 68.** **The next Exchange is the Wednesday of week fifty-two and Bea Nunn will not open the book on a Friday. The book is at forty-four lines, the fortieth is three years old, line forty-four has a hole in it, and it is not a register, and it does not go to forty-five in this volume. Asha Reed's sentence is retired.**
- **The interval map's two-day offset from the Monday of week thirty-one is inherited, is recorded for the fifth time, and is not to be re-derived. No chapter in Volume 04 Movement I printed a day number, and no month-name, month-date, day-date or year.**
- **The specification runs to four sections and had two lines and a funded third line of nine words, and the fund's committee does not meet again and nothing may be settled by a committee, a minute, a notice, a report or a leaflet.** The seventh session happened in Chapter 158 and the eighth is four weeks after it.
- **The Friday rota: four days a week at grade two and two Fridays at grade three, on a card in date or an exception recorded and renewed in a box that says *No reason given by holder*.** About eleven people a week, seven on the rota and about four not on it. **The number is on the back of a woman's own card, is not on a form anywhere, and is not a rate. It is now known to a woman of twenty-nine, a man of thirty-four on nights and Marek Senn, and to a district card, and to nobody else.**
- **The master is in an inside pocket on a chair in Lattice Ward, and the decision is unmade after a term and a half, and the original is in the fourth pocket of the tool roll with the fifth line still blank, and the drawer under the bench holds five things, four of them other people's sentences.** The master did not move in Movement I and may not move in Movement II.
- **The box in pencil may take a name in Volume 04 and may not take a reference number, a system, a post or a district.** It has one name in it and cannot be emptied.
- **The relay may not be staged again in any form, and the carrier may not be asked again.** There is no panel in this volume and there will be none.
- **Series milestone 3 is spent and may not be spent again. Milestone 4 belongs to Volumes 05 and 06 and nothing in Volume 04 may pre-empt it.** The bench is not reopened, Chapter 102's Tuesday is not revisited, the Chapter 7 page stays closed, and Movement I did not touch the relationship at all.
- **The seven is still seven, the four things that are not the seven are still four, and the word *pattern* does not appear in Volume 04.** Asha Reed's sentence is not said again. Steve Akers is not given to anybody. A woman of thirty-four with a hip is not found. Nell Senn is unwritten. The Crown Clause is never named and the names Iona and Sorn appear nowhere.
- **Open, unpaid, and not to be closed by a document:** the licensor, still blank; the six lines in the register, of which one has an author still in this city and Iven Sore will not say which; the second visitor column's five other words; the bit of pale card in a gate hut; the unsigned sheet and its four correct steps; the woman of thirty-nine and her page of her own; the four hundred and one replies and the nine who got the company answer; the nine words wrong in a minute; the fifth room and who takes the name out; the unlabelled bay, the list of named holders, the boy at the control post, Delia Frost's landing, Nkechi Osei's Mondays; **Roy Amiss's unringed telephone, which Movement I did not pay and which Movement II owns**; the box number and the four-letter post town, in two hands, one of them a telephone on a wall that nobody has rung; **and the hundred and forty-one pounds, which has now gone into a coat with a person nobody can name.**
- **NEW AND OPEN, FROM MOVEMENT I:** **the woman of thirty-four at the register table has said that if he comes back she would like to be asked what she thinks of the fourth column, and that is a dated invitation from the Tuesday of week forty-eight.** **The plans in a drawer on a hook in a basement in the fourth building, which nobody has stood in front of since the spring, and which he has written in his own book that nobody has earned.** **A woman of thirty-six who does the sheets up in about four weeks, who will turn the sheet over, and who does not know him or the woman of forty-six.** **A hundred and forty-one pounds and a customer who has been into the shop twice.** **A printer's own name on four hundred cards and a boy of nineteen's handwriting on all of them.** **And a service list held by a company that cannot give it away, in four offices, that has been asked for twice.**

# VOLUME 04 MOVEMENT I — REPAIR PASSES, COMPACTED. The full record of all three is in `reviews/volume-04-batch-0001.md` and in the `VOLUME 04 MOVEMENT I` block of `state/continuity.md`

**Three review and repair passes have been run on Chapters 151–160. **No thread was opened, closed, re-dated, re-costed or re-worded by the second or third pass, and nothing in the twenty-six dated pressures in `state/current.md` or the six this movement added was touched.** No batch was restarted and the load-book run is still 154 to 163, with the next run beginning at 164 in Chapter 161. This block replaces about 13 KB of running narrative; nothing was deleted from the repository.**

**FIVE THINGS THAT WERE WRONG ON THE PAGE AND ARE NOW RIGHT, condensed, because a writer who remembers the old chapter will contradict them.** The choice in Chapter 157 no longer says *tear* before it happens, and what is on the page is a request that existed for about a minute and was destroyed. The motive in Chapter 157 is arithmetic and not a corridor: a woman of forty-six with a trolley, and a woman of thirty-four on the Tuesday afternoon of week forty-eight who refused to read six lines and read the four column headings instead, **and Doreen Abbiss is the one name because she is the only person in this city with a name in a box she cannot get it out of.** The interval between the name going into the box and everything else is **seven days**, not four and not nine, and she met him for the first time on the half landing at twenty past midnight that same night. The page is in two places and there are two pages, and the counter's stack of about two hundred is a third thing again. The condition sheet is forty-one doors a week and has always been, out of about nine hundred rooms on four pages, one sheet a week, **about a thousand condition sheets in the ring file, nineteen years**, and the man of fifty-five told her about the basement drawer about four years ago and not before she came.

**TWO FIGURES THAT WERE PRINTED WRONG.** Eleven thousand premises is the reach of the levy note — five documents — and the four thousand is the leaflet, and the leaflet lands in nine hundred rooms; **no sentence in this manuscript puts four thousand sheets in eleven thousand premises.** And the standing problem has been said publicly four times, the fourth in the Friday briefing in Chapter 153 and the fifth in a room with a microphone in Chapter 158, which says five on the page; **Movement II's is the sixth and the resolution's is the seventh.**

**TWO THREADS THAT ARE OPEN AND WERE NOT FLAGGED, AND BOTH BELONG TO MOVEMENT II.** **Talia Venn has not appeared in any of Chapters 151 to 160**: milestone 1 and milestone 2 of `outline/volume-04.md` are both undelivered, milestone 2's arithmetic has broken because the Monday of week forty-nine is Chapter 159 and is behind us, so **the real gap is three weeks — say the gap or leave it alone, and do not put the two of them in a room that pretends it was four days.** And **the Exchange's outlined beat is on the page and the book is still at forty-four lines**: she was never asked to sign, no caller signs in Movement II either, and that is a rule and not a mood.

**FOUR THINGS A LATER WRITER MUST NOT GET WRONG.** Two canonical durations in Chapter 151 that are not the same afternoon: about nine seconds to give a name to a telephone, and four seconds to take a share off a bay in a residential home. **Petra Lindqvist says the same true sentence about her counter twice, in Chapter 149 and Chapter 151, and Chapter 151's wording is the current one — the repetition is not a slip.** Chapter 153's Friday is the Friday of week forty-seven and its word ran from half past nine, not from ten past nine as it did in Chapter 142, and any later reference to either must carry its own week. **The eighty-character overlap sweep has a standing exception list and it is not empty**: Chapter 160 must reproduce Chapter 150's inventory because the final image has to be reached item by item, Chapter 158 must echo Chapter 148 because it is the seventh session after the sixth in the same room with the same officer and the same third line, and the canonical refrains are neither to be cut nor increased.

**FOUR THREADS THE REPAIRS TOOK CARE NOT TO DISTURB.** The pencil box holds a woman's name and it cannot come out, and **a second name may go into it only if the person it is for puts it there and never by a third party — which is the whole of what this movement is about.** The standing problem about who may be asked is said four times and not resolved, and no rule about findability was invented. **Roy Amiss's unringed telephone is untouched, is not paid, is owed a thing that is not a leaflet, and is to be paid by somebody who is not Marek Senn.** And the building plan in a drawer on a hook in a basement is unread on the page, which is where it was, and **Movement III owns it, and the first person down those stairs must not be Marek Senn.**
